# Design decisions log

Decisions made during implementation, with the reason for each. Newest milestone first.
Anything marked **(review)** is a judgement call that the product owner may want to change. Items marked "confirmed by owner" were reviewed and accepted.

## Standing decisions (the ones in force; summary)

Moved here from `CLAUDE.md` on 2026-11-20, which keeps only a one-line version of each. The
dated log below has the reasons.

- **License: JUCE under AGPLv3**, so this whole project is AGPLv3 (`LICENSE`). Every binary
  release must ship with (or link to) its complete source. Don't add dependencies that are
  incompatible with AGPLv3 (check before adding any).
- **Format: VST3 only.** No AU, CLAP, or standalone (add to `FORMATS` only if decided later).
- **Voices: 16 per part** (32 total in Dual mode). This is the CPU budget target.
- **Character:** clean by default. One "Vintage" amount controls drift, voice-to-voice variation,
  and filter/envelope tolerance.
- **Stack:** JUCE 9.0.5 and Catch2 v3.16.1 as git submodules in `external/`, pinned to exact tags.
  Upgrade them deliberately, in their own commit. C++20, CMake ≥ 3.28, Ninja on macOS.
- **Targets:** macOS universal (`arm64;x86_64`, min macOS 11, the default in `CMakeLists.txt`); Windows
  x64 + ARM64 merged into one `.vst3` bundle (`Contents/x86_64-win`, `Contents/arm64-win`) by CI.
  MSVC static runtime. **Linux x86_64 and ARM64** (owner decisions): built on `ubuntu-22.04` /
  `ubuntu-22.04-arm` for a glibc 2.35 floor, GCC, static libstdc++/libgcc, merged into one bundle
  (`Contents/x86_64-linux`, `Contents/aarch64-linux`). ARM64 is CI-validated only (no pluginval
  build exists for it) and not performance-tuned for small boards. Never raise that runner image
  above the oldest one offered without a decision. **Before pushing engine or test changes run
  `scripts/linux-check.sh`** (Docker; native Linux ARM64 container; GCC build + all tests, about
  1 min warm; `--sanitize` for ASan/UBSan): it catches GCC-only errors without a CI round trip.
  The x86_64 Linux binary is proven in CI only: `gh workflow run build.yml --ref <branch> -f targets=linux`
  runs the Linux rows, the sanitizer job and the distro load tests.
- **Identity:** company "Exitium", bundle ID `com.exitium.vintagesynth`, manufacturer code
  `Exit`, plugin code `Vsy1`. **Never change these after the first release**, because hosts identify
  the plugin by them.
- **pluginval: Claude runs it itself** (official Tracktion release v1.0.4, unpacked in the session
  scratchpad). Don't hand it to the user unless the harness refuses the command. See skill `validate-plugin`.
- **Repo:** GitHub `romuaa/vintage-synth` (**public since 2026-11-20**), branch `main`. `main` is
  protected (owner, 2026-11-20): changes only through a pull request, no force-push, no deletion,
  admins included; no required status check (the build workflow is manual-only).
- **Workflow: orchestrated agents, branches + PR** (skill `orchestrate`). Agents commit on feature
  branches; the orchestrator opens the pull requests. **Merge to `main` only after
  the owner approves that milestone.** Never commit to `main` directly, never force-push.
  The owner auditions at the end of M2, M5 and M6 and sets the UI direction before M7.
- **Contracts:** `docs/architecture.md` defines the interfaces agents build against (note events,
  voice allocation, new parameters, effects, arp/seq). Change it only in its own commit.
- **CI:** `.github/workflows/build.yml` builds, tests, and runs pluginval on macOS and Win x64.
  Win ARM64 runs build, unit tests and host_smoke (there's no ARM64 pluginval). Check results with
  `gh run list` / `gh run view <id> --log-failed` (gh is installed and authenticated). Never claim
  Windows is green without a passing run. CI builds every target with Ninja and a compiler cache
  (ccache on macOS, sccache on Windows) inside the newest Visual Studio found by `vswhere`; never
  hardcode a VS version. LTO applies to the shipped plugin only; tests and `voice_bench` link
  `vs_test_core` (same sources, no LTO), so add new source files to `VS_COMMON_SOURCES` or `VS_VINTAGE_SOURCES` (`VS_PLUGIN_SOURCES` is their union).
  Runner images are pinned (`macos-26`, `windows-2025`, `windows-11-arm`, `ubuntu-26.04`);
  Dependabot doesn't bump them, so check github.com/actions/runner-images when touching CI.
  Pushing to `main` cancels a running build of `main` (concurrency group): wait for a run you
  still need before pushing again.
- **Verification is local-first (owner decision).** Everything this Mac can check is checked here by
  `scripts/preflight.sh`: macOS universal build + tests + pluginval, the VST3 validator (built once
  from the pinned SDK commit into `~/Library/Caches/vs-preflight/`), ASan/UBSan, the Linux ARM64
  container, the security scan, repository hygiene (incl. a hashed brand-name check and "no session
  link in commits"). `--quick` while iterating, the default set before handing over.
- **CI never runs by itself** (no PR, branch or `main` triggers on `build`). The orchestrator
  dispatches it by hand, once per merge-ready branch, when Actions minutes are available:
  `gh workflow run build.yml --ref <branch> -f targets=no-macos` (`all` before a release; also
  `windows`, `linux`, `linux-x64`). Agents never trigger CI. **Windows and Linux x86_64 are
  confirmed by CI only**: never claim them green without a passing dispatched run. A dispatch only
  works for refs once the workflow file is on the default branch.
- **Supply chain:** every third-party action is pinned by full commit SHA with a `# vX.Y.Z` comment.
  `.github/dependabot.yml` updates Actions and submodules monthly. Before merging a submodule PR,
  check it points at a release tag and update the versions named here and in README.
- **Security scan:** `.github/workflows/security.yml` runs Trivy (vuln, secret, misconfig) on push,
  PR and weekly; HIGH/CRITICAL fixable findings fail it. Results are in the job log and the
  `trivy-results` SARIF artifact; setting the repo variable `CODE_SCANNING=true` also uploads
  the SARIF to code scanning (not set). GitHub's CodeQL default setup (C/C++, Python, Actions)
  runs on pushes and pull requests since the repository became public (owner: keep,
  2026-11-20); it has no workflow file.
- **B-Side (owner, 2026-11-14; `docs/architecture.md` 8.12):** identity `Bsy1` /
  `com.exitium.bside`; two parts; Init uses chip Model B; mimic the original chip as closely as
  possible (its own alias budgets, 8.4.5, approved). **Reference emulator approved as a developer
  tool only:** built outside the repository (`~/Library/Caches/bside-oracle/`), never linked or
  shipped, never named in the repository; only generated reference data is committed.
- **Vintage first release (owner, 2026-11-19):** the repository becomes public at the start of
  the release work (the owner flips visibility, after a pre-public audit of the whole history),
  so CI is free. **No code signing and no installers** for now (no Apple Developer membership,
  no Windows certificate): a release is one zip of plain plugin binaries per platform that the
  user copies into the plugin folder, with `LICENSE`, the font licence and install notes.
  **Platforms:** macOS universal, Windows x64 and Linux x86_64; Windows ARM64 and Linux ARM64
  ship marked "experimental" (no pluginval build exists for them). **CI stays manual-only, also
  when public:** no workflow may gain an automatic trigger except the cheap `security` one; the
  local gate checks this. B-Side is not in the release (`VS_BUILD_BSIDE=OFF`). **Version: 0.9.0, a
  public beta** (parameter IDs and the state format are not frozen until 1.0.0).
- **M7 editor (owner, 2026-11-14; `docs/architecture.md` 9.8):** round-3 wireframes approved;
  fixed aspect, 75–200 % (1360×784 at 100 %); embedded font Inter (SIL OFL 1.1, its licence file
  kept in the repository); dark theme only; static graphs with live markers; delay/reverb on/off
  as a level-0 switch (no new parameters); values shown as hosts show them (0–10); help bubbles
  after 600 ms plus the help line.
- **M7 UI (owner, 2026-11-14):** the tabbed wireframes are approved as the direction, with a
  colour tint per part, Shape split into LFO and Envelope pages, and hover help on every control.
- **Panel interpretation, DSP ranges and design choices:** see `docs/decisions.md`. Add every new
  judgement call there (with the reason), and mark the ones the user should review.

## B-Side S3b-2: aliasing of the static curves (2026-11-23, all review)

Contract: `docs/architecture.md` 8.19.4, part "S3b-2". Code: `source/bside/chip/HalfBand.*` (new), `ChipVoice.*`
("ALIAS"), `Chip.h` ("BEND"), `ChipRender.cpp`, `RenderTables.*` (the bend, bent tables), `Filter.*` (split entry
points), `OutputStage.*` (the mixer in pieces). Tests: `tests/bside/unit/ChipVoiceAliasTests.cpp` (new), changes in
`ChipVoiceTests.cpp` and `ChipBusTests.cpp`; bench cases `preB`, `s3bB`, `preA`, `twoxA`, `s3bA`, `busesA0`. Design study:
the developer cache (`s3b-alias`). Nothing was generated with the reference adapter in this step.

| # | Point | Reading taken (reason; measured figures) |
|---|---|---|
| A1 | **The half-band filters: Kaiser designs at prepare, pass edge 20 kHz, deviation 1e-3.** | A half-band's pass-band deviation equals its stop-band level (H (f) + H (r / 2 - f) = 1), so 8.19.4's "within 0.01 dB" (1.15e-3) already means about 59 dB of stop band, more than its "at least 40 dB"; the study's 51 taps at 88.2 kHz were a 40 dB design with 0.061 dB of ripple. The pass edge is 20 kHz at every rate, so the stop band starts at rate / 2 - 20 kHz, which is at least (rate - 20 kHz) / 3 from 80 kHz up (the larger of 8.19.4's two edges). Designed in closed form for any rate (pairs added until a grid of the pass band meets the deviation; allocation-free): pairs 21 / 11 / 5 / 4 / 4 / 4 / 4 at 88.2 / 96 / 128 / 176.4 / 192 / 352.8 / 384 kHz (delay 2 P - 1: 41 / 21 / 9 / 7 / 7 / 7 / 7), pass band 0.0078 dB at worst, stop band -60.3 dB at worst; the 2x part's half-bands 4 pairs (3 at 352.8 and 384 kHz). Written in a difference form (each pair minus twice the centre, added to the centre) so that a constant passes exactly: a held static DC and the curve's operating point subtracted after it cancel exactly (silence in, exact zeros out). An equiripple design would need about two pairs fewer at 96 kHz; not built. **(review)** |
| A2 | **The alias gate's stimulus: ideal voices for the curves, real chips where the filter does not reweigh the renderer.** | 8.19.4 measures the chain's output relative to voice 1's fundamental. With real chips through the band- and high-pass, at resonance 15 or cutoff 1024, the figure is the renderer's own components (inside its 8.4.5 budget at its output) weighed by the linear filter: a high-pass at 2047 cuts a 1 kHz fundamental by about 46 dB and passes components near 17 kHz; the resonance lifts components near the corner. The same rows with every curve linear (the renderer without the bend, the filter's linear part, no mixer curve, no anti-alias stage) give the same figures (Model B: chain minus linear between -2.5 and +0.8 dB over all 1232 such rows; e.g. saw 1 kHz, high-pass 2047, 88.2 kHz: -77.9 / -77.9 dB). No treatment of the curves can change that. So the gate is run (a) on every row of 8.19.4 with ideal band-limited voices put into the chain's buses (`ChipVoice::processBuses`; every harmonic below half the rate; Model A's bent exactly, as the folded bend renders them): this isolates the curves and their anti-alias stages; (b) with real chips (renderer, bend, chain) on every row whose filter passes voice 1's fundamental and the renderer's components alike: all direct, and the low-pass at 2047 with resonance 0; (c) the real chips through the other settings are measured and printed beside the linear chain's figure, not gated. 2 x 7 x (202 + 26) = 3192 gated rows; 2464 reported. **(review)** |
| A3 | **Not met: Model A rows that drive the mixer past the limit of its curve.** | Two or three full-level voices through Model A's low-pass at resonance 15 push the mixer's input below -3.30, where its quintic turns and the curve is flat (OutputStage, S3a): a slope-continuous corner, whose products fold at twice the rate itself, before the decimator (a 100 dB decimator was tried: the figures did not move). 84 gated rows reach the limit; 9 of them miss 8.19.4's limit, all ideal-voice rows at 88.2 / 96 / 128 kHz: three voices at 2 kHz, low-pass 2047, resonance 15: -85.5 / -86.1 / -92.3 dB (limit -99); at 3.85 kHz: -78.9 / -81.3 dB (limit -83); saw + square 5:4 at 2 kHz: -89.3 / -91.2 / -98.3 dB; three voices at 2 kHz, cutoff 1024: -96.8 dB (88.2 kHz). The real chips show the same regime (reported rows: -84.7 dB at 96 kHz, three voices at 2 kHz, low-pass 2047, resonance 15). Every row that stays inside the curves' limits meets 8.19.4 (Model A at worst -117.7 dB ideal, -115.6 dB chip; Model B -126.1 / -111.0 dB). The test classifies a row by simulating the curve's input without anti-alias stages; the normal run asserts that every row over the limit is such a row, the hidden full set asserts every row and fails on these nine. Possible remedies, each the owner's or orchestrator's call: 4x for Model A's curves (about twice the 2x part's cost), a smoother continuation of the curve past its turning point (a change of S3a's fitted law outside its data), or antiderivative anti-aliasing of the limit at 2x (a half-sample delay and about 0.2 dB at 20 kHz to compensate). **Corrected in the review round (R5):** the flat limit is replaced by a smooth continuation; the shortfall is now 4 rows of statement (a) and 40 of (c), all driven past the curve's fitted range or at resonance 15 (R6). **Settled in the closing round (C1, C2):** with (c) corrected 8 rows remain, pinned as a known deviation (O6). **(review)** |
| A4 | **Configurations blended across a write are blended before the anti-alias stages; the copies carry no FIR state** (the S3b-1 reviewer's note c). | A copy made at the start of a write's window cannot have the new mode's history in a FIR whose delay (up to 41 samples) exceeds the write's look-ahead (about 9 samples): its output would mix both modes in the FIR's tail, an error of first order. Instead the configurations' filter outputs (the copies of the filter, as before) and gains are blended linearly at the mixer's input, the output function's coefficients (Model A) likewise, and what follows the curve (volume, Y0, the static DC's subtraction, the post gain) as coefficients delayed with the signal through the anti-alias stages. A volume write stays exact (tested against the ideal switch of the held renders: 1.1e-8 to 3.0e-8 of the volume's change, the kernel's step read two ways; one sample off 0.03 to 0.06); a mode write leaves a second-order term (write budget below). **(review)** |
| A5 | **The bend folded into the renderer, the full form; the single sum carries it when it is on; off by default for a bare chip** (note a). | `Chip::renderBendVoices`: triangle and saw through renderValue / renderDerivative (f (w) and f' (w) w' at the line's position, so every boundary, the Model A converter bits' edges included, gets its slope term: the full form, not the cheaper one); the piecewise-constant waveforms through bent tables (wave[] and the combined waveforms' values and moments of f (w); f is increasing, so the cells' changes are kept). The rings are shared, so the single sum is bent too when the bend is on; it is off for a bare chip, which keeps every renderer test's meaning, and ChipVoice turns it on. Test whose expectation changed: "buses: each oscillator on the bus its routing bit names ..." (the bent case compared with the bent chip's own single sums instead of OutputStage::shapeVoice of the unbent voice per sample, which the band-limited bend no longer equals). Accuracy against the exact bend of the band-limited voice: harmonics above -40 dB within 0.04 dB; the DC of the naive bent waveform kept. Cost: busesA - busesA0 = 4.1 to 4.4 ns at 96 kHz (S4 gate patch; the S3b-1 per-sample bend cost 2.2 to 2.9). **(review)** |
| A6 | **Writes land at the filter's signal time; two S3b-1 tests read the filter's lag** (note b). | Model A's bus pre-filter delays what the filter sees by its delay: cutoff, resonance and the configurations' windows are applied at the filter's signal time, the counts the stage uses are delayed with the pre-filtered buses, and everything after the filter is delayed with the signal, so a write lands at its cycle in the output (tested: impulse and step centres at latencySamples () to 1e-6 and 0.002 samples, Per Note and Shared). `filterLagSamples ()` (new) is output index minus the filter's signal time. Changed tests: "the cutoff runs per sample ..." compares the filter's own output (test aids `lastFilterInput`, `lastFilterOutput`) with the rule written out, at the filter's lag (latencySamples () + 1 now also counts the stages after the filter); "a full write queue ..." uses the filter's lag. Their rules and numbers are unchanged; the cutoff test compares to 1e-12 instead of bit for bit (GCC contracted Model A's output function differently in the two inlined copies: one rounding, 2.2e-16, Linux step; `Filter::processDouble` now runs through `processLinearDouble`; a cutoff write one sample late still fails it). **(review)** |
| A7 | **Shared: `finishShared (filter, linear, lowPass, static, n, out, k)`** (note d). | Model A's output function runs in the 2x part, per chip on its share (8.19.4), so the owner hands each voice the shared filter's linear output (`Filter::processLinearDouble`) and low-pass value, and the filter itself (its output function's coefficients); the share is (linear - static) / n + static and the output function scaled by 1 / n, as OutputStage "SHARED" defines it on the whole output. The bus pre-filter runs per chip before the owner's sum (`filterInputAc`, `staticFilterBusDc` are pre-filtered; filtering each and summing equals filtering the sum). One chip in Shared is still Per Note bit for bit (S3b-1's test unchanged), Model B equals Per Note for identical voices (0.000 dB), Model A keeps the pinned figures. **(review)** |
| A8 | **Model A's voices' DC through its own pre-filter, held values short-cut.** | The voices' DC moves only at routing writes; once it has stood still for the filter's length the pre-filter's output is that value and the dot product is skipped, in the one function that Per Note and Shared both call (so one chip stays bit for bit). **Corrected in the review round (R9):** the voice computed the FIR and only replaced its result; the shortcut now lives in HalfBandFir itself (a held input returns as it is and the taps are skipped). **(review)** |
| A9 | **The anti-alias filters start primed with their first input** after reset (the output starts on its value, as the renderer's rings do). **(review)** |
| A10 | **Results of the other points.** | Pass band (a 1e-3 sine through the chain, 20 Hz to 20 kHz, every rate): within 0.023 dB, within 0.002 dB of the designs. Products of a 6.6 kHz sine and of 6.6 + 8.1 kHz (direct, both models; Model A also through its low-pass): 252 products above -100 dB within 0.011 dB of the exact curves. Saw and square harmonics above -40 dB (1 and 3.85 kHz, every rate, both models): 476 within 0.06 dB; weaker ones move by up to 12.2 dB (accepted, B2). Write budget of S3b-1 through the new chain: worst -93.6 dB (Model A, mode low <-> band, volume DC off; budget -90; S3b-1: -111.5); the held states without writes, formerly -61 to -95 dB, now -111 to -151 dB. Split bit-identity, no allocation, stability, the Shared tests and every `bside_trace` gate pass. **(review)** |
| A11 | **Cost** (`chip_bench`, this Mac, three runs interleaved with the base binary, ns per internal sample, 96 kHz). | **Not met: Model B's filter + output stage + pre-filter, `s3bB` 7.6 to 7.8 ns (budget 7; `s3aB` 5.8);** 6.8 to 7.3 at 192 kHz, 15.2 to 15.7 at 48 kHz (no budget there). The FIR alone (`preB`): 4.9 to 5.5 ns. One further attempt (a ring per sample parity so the pairs form one contiguous window): slower, 9.1 ns, reverted; not tried: an equiripple design (about two pairs fewer) or dropping the difference form (-0.6 ns in a scratch measurement, but held DCs would no longer cancel exactly). Model A (no budget; the owner's S4 question): `preA` 8.8, `twoxA` 9.0 to 9.1, `s3bA` 21.5 to 21.6 (`s3aA` 8.0); the bend in the renderer 4.1 to 4.4 (A5). The chain on the S4 gate patch: `chainB` 31.6 (base 27.8 to 28.4), `chainA` 68.0 to 68.8 (base 44.2 to 44.5); `busesA` now includes Model A's bus pre-filter (ChipVoice::renderBusesUntil runs it): 47.9 to 48.4 (base 35.2 to 35.7), `busesB` 20.6 to 20.7 (base 20.4 to 20.6). **(review)** |
| A12 | **Mutations** (each applied, the tests named run, reverted). | Fail: the mixer FIR bypassed (gate); Model A's 2x part bypassed (gate); the bend's slope term dropped (gate); ChipVoice leaving the bend off (`bside_trace`: 2 cases); the post-curve coefficients one sample early (blend); the pass edge 10 % low (half-band, pass band); the FIR's plain form (silence: constants not exact); the Shared output function not scaled to the share (Shared figures); the interpolator's wrong polyphase sample (half-band); a stale filter in the old configuration (write budget and others); the latency without the post stages (latency, blend); Model A's bus pre-filter bypassed (gate). **Survive:** Model A's voice counts not delayed with the bus pre-filter, and the output function's coefficients not delayed to the interpolated low-pass value (both act only inside a routing write's or a mode write's window; the write budget stays met with them). Reported, not pinned. **Corrected in the review round (R2, R11):** "both act only inside a window, harmless" was wrong for the counts: the reviewer measured an isolated routing step moving by +30.7 / +16 / +7.4 samples at 88.2 / 96 / 128 kHz with the counts not delayed. Both mutations now fail the write-timing test (R3). **(review)** |

## B-Side S3b-2 review round (2026-11-23, all review)

Contract: `docs/architecture.md` 8.19.4, "After the independent review of S3b-2" (commit 98f7b15). Reviewer's material:
the developer cache (`s3b2-review`).

| # | Point | Reading taken (reason; measured figures) |
|---|---|---|
| R1 | **Model A's output-function coefficients with the interpolated sample.** | The interpolator's second output is its input shapeDelay (P - 1) samples ago, its first half a sample earlier; both took the coefficients of P - 1 samples ago, so a mode write's step landed 0.31 samples early (reviewer). The first output now takes the mean of the coefficients of P - 1 and P samples ago. The gain is inside the interpolated input and the post-curve coefficients are delayed by the whole 2x part, so they were already aligned. Measured after the fix: every write type within 0.0005 samples (R3). |
| R2 | **The voice counts delayed with Model A's bus pre-filter** (they were; the mutation that removed it survived). | Pinned by R3's routing case that changes the counts (the mutation moves the step by 2.5 to 37 samples). |
| R3 | **Write timing per type, both models, Per Note and Shared.** | A held voice (test bit), every internal rate: volume (volume DC on and off), routing (oscillators 1 and 2 swap buses so the counts and the mixer's gain stay), routing with the counts changing, mode (low- to high-pass), resonance (Model A; Model B's linear filter has no step on a held input). The step's centre is taken on the mixer curve's input (the output inverted through OutputStage::mixerCurve), which a curve whose slope changes along the step does not bend. Within 0.02 samples (largest 0.0005); the routing case with the counts held to 0.1 (the gain's step times the sum's step has its centre 0.06 samples late on Model A whatever the timing). Shared for volume and routing (two identical chips). Fails on: shapeDelay 0, the interpolated sample's coefficients half a 2x sample early, counts not delayed, resonance not blended. |
| R4 | **Resonance writes blend two configurations** (they applied at their sample: a step up to half a sample off its place, 0.29 to 0.36 measured). | As mode writes: a copy of the filter keeps the old resonance over the write's window; their poles differ, so the states part inside the window only. The S3b-1 cutoff test no longer writes the resonance (its rule "from the write's own sample" is the cutoff's alone now); D4's "resonance writes apply from the write's own sample" is superseded. Shared: the owner's filter takes the write filterLagSamples () after it (ChipVoice.h "TOPOLOGY"), a switch at a sample. |
| R5 | **Model A's mixer curve continued smoothly past its fitted range.** | The quintic turns at -3.297 and 4.081 with curvature 1.68 and -2.74, so no continuation can keep value, slope and curvature there and stay monotonic. The joins are moved inward to -3.08 and 3.80, outside every gated trace's range (measured: the gated `a_*` reach -3.06 .. 3.55; the reported `a_s3a_*` -3.75 .. 3.79), and beyond them N = bound +- a exp (-b u) with a = s^2 / |k|, b = |k| / s from the quintic's slope s and curvature k at the join: C2, monotonic, bounded (-2.870 and 5.558). Between the joins the quintic is unchanged to the bit (a comparison and the polynomial, as before; the exponential only outside). Its effect alone on the trace lines: only `a_s3a_voices_a` s13 changes (its corner became measurable: +71 %, peak +3.72 dB, before "no corner the estimator resolves"); the gated lines are bit for bit the same. One alternative was tried: a hyperbolic continuation (a / (1 + b u), C2 too), 1.8 dB worse on the worst row; not kept. |
| R6 | **The restated gate: not met on Model A's overdriven rows.** | (a) 2828 rows, 4 over: Model A, all through the low-pass at 2047, resonance 15, two or three voices at 2 kHz, 88.2 and 96 kHz: -93.4 / -96.7 / -96.5 / -96.3 dB (limit -99; before the continuation -85.5 / -89.3 / -86.1 / -91.2). (b) 588 rows, none over (closest Model B -111.0, Model A -115.6). (c) 2240 rows, 44 over: 38 Model A rows at resonance 15 through the low-pass, two or three voices (up to 18 dB over the 4x reference: the quintic driven hard folds at twice the rate whatever its ends), 4 Model A rows near -140 dB within 0.7 dB over, 2 Model B rows 0.1 and 0.3 dB over (-111.6, -121.2 dB). Two shapes of continuation and two strengths of the 2x half-bands were tried (R7); the point is stopped here as the contract says, and the owner decides (four times the rate for Model A's curves is not taken). The normal run asserts its reduced set outright and therefore fails on these rows. **Settled in the closing round:** (c) corrected (C1), the 8 remaining rows pinned (C2, owner decision O6); the normal run passes. |
| R7 | **Model A's 2x half-bands at about 100 dB** (deviation 1e-5; were 1.15e-3). | What leaks through 60 dB half-bands (an image of the band, the decimator's stop band) put a floor near -130 dB that the 4x reference (120 dB filters) does not have: 72 rows of (c) over before, 44 after. Pairs at twice 88.2 / 96 / 128 / 176.4 / 192 / 352.8 / 384 kHz: 7 / 6 / 5 / 5 / 4 / 4 / 4 (delay 2 P - 1 at 2x), so Model A's latency at 96 kHz is 8 + 21 + 11. |
| R8 | **The half-bands designed to the fewest pairs** (8.19.4 after the review). | For each pair count from below Kaiser's estimate the window's parameter is searched (2 .. 12 by 0.05); the first count whose best parameter meets the deviation is taken. The internal rate's pre-filter at 1.15e-3 (0.01 dB): 19 / 11 / 5 / 4 / 4 / 2 / 2 pairs (were 21 / 11 / 5 / 4 / 4 / 4 / 4; the reviewer's minimum with a Kaiser window: 19 / 11 / 5 / 4 / 4 / 2 / 2). An equiripple design (about 10 at 96 kHz) is not built: the budget was met without it (R10). |
| R9 | **HalfBandFir in the plain form with a held-input shortcut** (A8 corrected). | The difference form cost a subtraction per pair; the plain form (1/2 the centre plus the pairs) is used and a held input, once it fills the window, is returned as it is with the taps skipped: constants are exact (silence and the static DC cancel exactly, tested) and held DCs cost nothing. The interpolator and decimator keep the difference form (few pairs). |
| R10 | **Model B's cost** (`s3bB`). | Model B's filter computed the low-pass value for Model A's output function on every sample since the split (S3b-2, 3 multiply-adds that Model B never uses); it is computed for Model A only. With R8 and R9, `s3bB` (the 7 ns budget) is 6.3 to 6.4 ns at 96 kHz (three runs interleaved with the S3b-1 binary, load average about 2; `s3aB` 5.1 to 6.2, base 5.5 to 5.9): the 7 ns budget is met. Model A (no budget): `preA` 7.6 to 8.1, `twoxA` 10.1 to 10.4 (the 100 dB half-bands of R7), `s3bA` 20.4 to 20.9; the chain on the S4 gate patch: `chainB` 30.9 to 32.7 (base 27.2 to 27.8), `chainA` 63.6 to 80.9 (base 42.5 to 44.8), the bend in the renderer `busesA` - `busesA0` 3.8 to 4.7. |
| R11 | **Mutations of the bend that survive.** | The converter switch's slope term in `renderFinishOsc` (a dropped term changes no output of a bent saw in a glide, sync or ring: a switch there happens only after renderSyncOsc has already switched at the write), and the derivative at an event behind the sweep position without the cell (2.5e-5 at most in a glide of frequency writes every 32 samples, below every gate: no test resolves it). The slope terms of the line's own events and of renderDerivative do fail the new sync and ring test (-79 / -72 dB against -96). Reported, not pinned. **Closing round (C5):** the converter switch was unreachable for every waveform and is removed. |
| R12 | **Shared: the output function per bus sample; the owner applies cutoff, resonance and mode filterLagSamples () after the write.** | finishShared took the shared Filter and read its output function once per owner block, so a mode or resonance write inside a block took effect at the block's end (Model A); it now takes Filter::Shape per bus sample (interface changed). Tested: a Shared mode write against Per Note, equal outside the window, its step within 0.6 samples (a switch at a sample against a band-limited blend); the mutation (one shape per block) fails it. |
| R14 | **GCC: no contraction of multiply-adds across statements in the chip code** (`-ffp-contract=off` on `bs_juce_free`, public). | GCC (11 and 13) contracted the filter's inline step differently in a test's Shared owner and in the voice, one rounding apart (5.6e-17), and "one chip in Shared is Per Note bit for bit" failed on Linux. Moving the step out of line fixed it at 1.1 ns per sample on this Mac (`s3bB` 7.4 to 7.5, over the budget), so the compile option was taken instead (Clang contracts within one expression only, MSVC not at all: they were deterministic already). S4 must give the plugin's B-Side sources the same option. |
| R13 | **Tests added in the round.** | Write timing per type (R3); Shared mode write (R12); the chain's output with a moving cutoff against a twin built from the public pieces (Chip, Filter, OutputStage, HalfBand with each delay explicit), to 1e-9, both models, 96 and 88.2 kHz; sync and ring with bent saws (aliasing at most -96 dB; measured -112 to -132 dB) and a glide across converter switches (printed); the restated gate with the 4x reference (test code only). |

| C4 | **Full set** (2 models x 7 rates, 5656 rows, as committed). | (a) 2828 rows, (b) 588, (c) 2240; none failing; the 8 pinned rows measured at their figures (to 0.1 dB); 137 s. Closest unpinned row per statement and model (margin to the limit): (a) Model A 88.2 kHz three voices at 3.85 kHz, low-pass 2047, resonance 15, -85.7 dB (limit -83; -2.7), Model B -126.0 dB (-23.0); (b) Model A -115.6 dB (-16.6), Model B 88.2 kHz saw + saw 3:2 at 3.85 kHz all direct -111.0 dB (-8.0); (c) Model A 88.2 kHz three voices at 2 kHz through the high-pass at 2047, resonance 0, -99.1 dB (-0.1), Model B 96 kHz saw + square at 3.85 kHz, voice 1 through the band-pass, -103.2 dB (-0.2). The two (c) rows this close to the absolute limit are also within 1 dB of the 4x reference (R6 listed neither), so a platform's rounding cannot fail them. **Superseded by S2 (second review):** measured in one phase constellation only. **(review)** |
| C5 | **The converter switch in `renderFinishOsc` was unreachable and is removed** (R11's surviving mutation). | The Model A converter bits rendered (imin) depend on the mode, the DAC, the frequency and test bit and the clock. The mode is set only in renderSyncOsc; the frequency and the test bit change only in write (), setOscillator () and powerOnKeepingTime (), each followed by renderAfterChange, which runs renderSyncOsc for all three oscillators with the same rule; the clock does not change while rendering; and the rule is idempotent (iminWithHysteresis (r, c) = c for every c it or iminFor produced at r). So the switch at a sample end recomputed the same value every time, for every waveform, not only a bent saw. Checked: the whole B-Side suite (333 tests) with an abort in the branch never reached it. The branch is removed with the reason in a comment; switches happen at writes, where renderAfterChange emits the value and slope steps. The other survivor of R11 (the derivative's cell at an event, 2.5e-5 at most) stays reported, not tested (8.19.4 after the fix round). **(review)** |

Unchanged from R10 within the noise: the closing round changes test code and removes a branch that never ran. **(review)** |

## B-Side S3b-2 round after phases (2026-11-23, all review)

Contract: `docs/architecture.md` 8.19.4, "After the round over phases" (commit 24c30af). The full set's raw output: the

| # | Point | Reading taken (reason; measured figures) |
|---|---|---|
| P1 | **The two Model B rows of S3: the test's reference was wrong, not the chain.** | Measured on both rows in their failing constellation, the chain's mixer path rebuilt test-side from the chip's buses: the chain -101.94 dB (88.2 kHz, saw + saw 3:2 at 2 kHz, band-pass 1024, resonance 15, step 2.0); the same path with the chain's pre-filter and the curve at 4x -101.94; at 1x with the pre-filter -101.94 (so the chain's cubic aliases nothing measurable here); linear (no curve) -102.10, with or without the pre-filter (the line at 8215.9 Hz is the renderer's residue); the 4x reference as used, WITHOUT the pre-filter, -103.39; at 1x without the pre-filter -93.85. Same at 192 kHz (three voices at 2 kHz, band-pass 2047): chain -98.66, 4x with the pre-filter -98.66, reference as used -100.52. A stronger (1e-5) or narrower (pass edge 16 or 18 kHz) pre-filter changes the chain by at most 0.01 dB. So the reference differed from the chain in more than the rate of the curves: Model B's pre-filter was missing from it (Model A's reference had its pre-filters). Fixed in the test (renderReference4x runs Model B's mixer input through the chain's own pre-filter); both rows now equal their reference (difference 0.00 dB). No product change, so no new design and no bench (`s3bB` unchanged). Mutation: without that pre-filter in the reference the full set fails on these two rows again (the second review round's run). **(review)** |
| P2 | **Resonance writes against an ideal render: 12 of 16 rows were over -90 dB; fixed.** | The ideal render (test-side, `resonanceWrites`): the chip's buses interpolated to eight times the rate (three half-bands of 120 dB), the filter and stage at that rate with each write applied at the nearest 8x sample, the curves at 8x, back by three half-bands of 120 dB; ours minus the ideal render (its level and offset fitted, the best integer alignment) is the excess, measured off the lines, relative to the output's strongest line other than DC. Before (the dynamics switched at the window's first sample): excess up to -78.4 dB (Model A, resonance 0 <-> 15, low-pass, 88.2 kHz); the cause: the filter's dynamics switched at an integer sample whose distance from the write's place changes from write to write, a different trajectory each time, which the off-line measure sees. Fix (`ChipVoice.h` "TIMING"): the output equation follows the blend as before; the dynamics change at the write's place between two samples, as the mixture of two copies switched at the sample before and after it (`Filter::peekLinearDouble`, `takeStateMix`). After: excess at most -95.9 dB (Model B -95.9 to -113.3, Model A -97.1 to -108.0 over the 16 rows). Per Note write timing unchanged (0.0005 samples). Mutations: dynamics at the window's start (the old code): 22 of 27 rows over, worst -78.4; the mixture weight forced to 0 or to 0.5: worst -77.8 / -78.5 (all fail). **(review)** **Corrected after the third review (Q1, Q2):** the two-copy placement held for a resonance write on its own only; a volume or mode write inside its window ended it early (-78.7 dB), a cutoff write inside it did not reach the copies, and setFilter (nullptr) inside it crashed. Replaced by one state with every dynamics change at its place. |
| P3 | **The write budget's reference line: the output's strongest line other than DC.** | With volume DC on the output's mean (the mixer's offset) is tens of dB above the audio, and its window main lobe (+-8 bins) was taken as "the strongest line": the existing write-budget test flattered every volume-DC-on row by about 20 dB. The reference now excludes the main lobe of DC, in the existing test and in the new one. The existing rows still pass (worst: Model A mode low-pass <-> band-pass, volume DC off, -100.0 dB; routing (pulse) on Model A -111.3). **(review)** |
| P4 | **The normal run of the alias gate: 26 s on this Mac** (was 161 s in the second review round; limit 45 s). | Every listed row in its worst constellation (stored in the list) and in the common reset, so the two-sided check is exact; at 88.2 kHz every row of (a) and (b) and the (c) rows at resonance 15 or with three voices, in the reset; at 96 and 128 kHz the rows that can overdrive the mixer (two or three voices through the low-pass at resonance 15), so a listed row taken off the list fails here too. The full set over all constellations stays behind its tag (about 9 minutes). Whole `bside_unit` default run on this Mac (load average 4 to 5): 181 s before this branch (247fc84, 317 test cases), 226 s now (333 test cases), of which the alias gate 26 s. **(review)** **Corrected after the third review (Q6):** the reduced set measured unlisted rows at the reset only, so 3 of the 16 entries could be removed without a failure ("a listed row taken off the list fails here too" was not true). The class candidates now run in every constellation: 39 s. |
| P5 | **Gate mutations on the new normal run.** | A pin loosened by 10 dB: fails (stale, more than 2 dB better); a pin's stored constellation forgotten (step 0 instead of 2.0): fails (stale: the reset alone measures it 2.1 dB better); an entry removed (96 kHz three voices 2 kHz): fails (the row is over its limit unlisted; failed to fail before P4 added the 96 and 128 kHz candidates); an overdriven row inside the limits listed: fails (stale); a row that does not overdrive listed: fails (stale and not overdriven); the flat clamp: fails (25 rows); the 2x part removed: fails (206 rows). **(review)** **Corrected after the third review (Q6):** "an entry removed: fails" held for the entry tried; three others escaped. All 16 are now tried (Q6). |
| P6 | **GCC 11 ran the normal run's three statements as (a).** | On Linux (the container's GCC 11.4) a range-for over `{ Statement::a, Statement::b, Statement::c }` that passed each to a lambda returning the row selector gave Statement::a to all 18 calls (instrumented in the container): (b) and (c) measured nothing, and the test failed only on its row counts. A small reproduction of the pattern compiled correctly, so the cause is not isolated. The three calls are written out; the container then measures 452 / 84 / 272 rows like macOS (34 s there).
