## B-Side S3b-2 round after the third review (2026-11-23, all review)

Contract: `docs/architecture.md` 8.19.4, "After the third independent review of S3b-2" (commit 654204f). Reviewer's
material: the developer cache (`s3b2-review3`). Each finding reproduced first with the reviewer's programs (resonance
then volume 10 cycles later, Model A 88.2 kHz: -78.7 dB; the cutoff inside a resonance window up to 3.0e-2 off on Model
A; `setFilter (nullptr)` inside a split: SIGSEGV).

| # | Point | Reading taken (reason; measured figures) |
|---|---|---|
| Q1 | **One state for all dynamics changes (the mechanism replaced, not patched).** | Per Note the filter keeps one state whatever writes are pending. Each change of its dynamics lands at its own place: a resonance write at the write's place (the centre of its blend weights), between two samples, by one step mixed from the old and the new parameters (first order in the place; several in one sample mix in turn, up to 4); a cutoff write from its own sample on. The configurations being blended are only output equations on that state (Filter::peekLinearDouble / peekWithCutoffLinearDouble) plus their stages. So a volume, mode, routing or cutoff write cannot move a resonance write's place (there is no split to end), the cutoff reaches the one state at its sample, and setFilter is safe at any time (waiting changes are dropped with the filter left). Chosen over patching the two-copy mixture because each combination (a further configuration, a cutoff, a missing filter, a retiring window) needed its own rule there. Capacity: 32 changes may wait for their place (a further one applies the oldest at once); 5 configurations blended at once as before, independent of the dynamics. Cost: no-write path unchanged (`s3bB` 6.1 to 6.4 ns at 96 kHz, three runs, load 2.2 to 3.4); a resonance write about 350 ns (Model B) and 500 ns (Model A) at 96 kHz (`resB` - `plainB`, `resA` - `plainA`, x 64; new bench cases); per sample inside a window: one peek per configuration and one step (the copies of the old design ran a full step each). **(review)** **Corrected in the closing round (Z4):** the cost stated here is for a cutoff standing still; with it moving a resonance write costs about 2.1 us (Model B) and 3.3 us (Model A), a tick writing cutoff, resonance, mode and volume at once about 3.5 / 6.0 us per voice. |
| Q2 | **The combined-write gate: 150 rows, all within the budget.** | Resonance 0 <-> 15 (low-pass at 1024) alone, and followed by or following a volume (15 <-> 12), mode (low- <-> band-pass) or cutoff (1024 <-> 1280) write at 1, 10, 40 and 200 cycles, a write every tick, both models, 88.2, 96 and 192 kHz, voices at 1 and 2 kHz: worst excess -97.7 dB (Model B, 88.2 kHz, resonance then cutoff 10 cycles later); every row gated (the ideal's own error at most -109.5 dB). The normal run holds the 10-cycle rows at 96 kHz (3.8 s); the full set by its tag (334 s). The reviewer's unfixed code fails the normal run (-81.2 dB). **(review)** **Extended in the closing round (Z3):** the rows the contract asked for that the rebuilt test had dropped (resonance alone 0 <-> 15 and 4 <-> 12, low- and band-pass, volume DC off) are back, with high-pass and band + high-pass: 240 rows, worst -90.7 dB (Model B, 88.2 kHz, resonance 0 <-> 15 through band + high-pass, a voice at 2 kHz). |
| Q3 | **The ideal render, rebuilt (finding 6).** | The same chain at 32 times the rate: Model A's bus pre-filters and Model B's mixer pre-filter at the internal rate as in the chain (Model B: decimated, filtered, interpolated); each resonance, mode and volume write placed between two high-rate samples (the boundary sample mixed, outputs and state), a cutoff at the start of its own sample; the post-curve coefficients (volume, Y0, the static DC, the post gain) applied after the decimation as ideal band-limited steps (a running sum of a Hann-windowed sinc, +-256 samples) at their exact places. Applying them as hard steps at the high rate left an artefact near 20 kHz that did not shrink with the rate (-80 dB on a Model A volume write); found and replaced. Convergence (hidden test `[.chain-writes-convergence]`): its own error (the render at a factor minus at half of it, less 6 dB) falls about 6 dB per doubling; at 32 times at most -112 dB on every normal-run row. **(review)** |
| Q4 | **The place of the dynamics, tested by itself (finding 4).** | A twin Filter runs the voice's recorded filter input with the cutoff at its own sample and the resonance's dynamics at the place + delta (two filters switched either side, mixed), the new output equation from the write's window on; its output against the voice's from the window's start for delta on a 0.05-sample grid. Measured: best delta 0.00 and rms 3e-17 to 2e-16 at 0 on every case (both models, 88.2, 96, 192 kHz; alone and with a cutoff write 30 cycles before, at or after); 0.05 samples off gives 1.3e-6 to 7e-5, one sample 2e-5 to 1.6e-3. Mutations: place + 1 sample and + 3: best delta 1.00 / 3.00, fail; mixture weight 0: -0.95, fails; the cutoff missing from the newest output parameters: fails (3.7e-4 at 0); the reviewer's unfixed code: fails (best delta 0.4 to 1.2 with a cutoff in the window). The combined-write gate does not see a place moved by 1 or 3 samples (the same error at every write, on the lines): this test does. **(review)** |
| Q5 | **Splits and setFilter.** | New test: bursts of resonance, volume, mode, cutoff and routing writes at the same and neighbouring cycles, blocks of 1, 3, 64 and 777 samples: bit for bit; the same with switches to Shared (setFilter (nullptr), an owner running its own filter) and back placed inside bursts' windows and (the reviewer's case) at the first sample a resonance write's configuration is open: no crash, finite, bit for bit for every block size. The reviewer's unfixed code crashes on it (SIGSEGV, exit 139). Shared one chip is Per Note bit for bit for routing, mute and volume writes (the existing test); not for resonance, mode or cutoff writes, which the owner of a shared filter applies as switches (ChipVoice.h "TOPOLOGY"; cutoff: Per Note may run processWithCutoff where the owner runs setCutoff). **(review)** |
| Q6 | **The reduced alias gate: every single removal fails (finding 5).** | The normal run measures every row that can overdrive the mixer (two or three voices, all through the low-pass at resonance 15) in all five constellations at 88.2, 96 and 128 kHz, the other rows of the reduced set in the reset. Tried for all 16 entries: each removal fails the normal run (1 row NOT MET each). Alias part of the normal run: 39 s on this Mac (limit 45; the 88.2 kHz (c) reduced set now resonance 15 only). `voicesAt` makes the voices of any step on demand (finding 7). **(review)** |
| Q7 | **Shared, mode writes on Model A:** the output step at 0.6 samples is accepted (orchestrator, contract after the round over phases); the switch itself stays within 0.5 (unchanged). **(review)** |

## B-Side S3b-2 closing round after the fourth review (2026-11-23, all review)

Contract: unchanged (8.19.4 after the third review). The fourth review found no code defect in the one-state rule; this
round changes tests and records only, and removes one branch shown unreachable.

| # | Point | Reading taken (reason; measured figures) |
|---|---|---|
| Z1 | **The sanitizer failure was the test's.** | `offLine` (ChipVoiceTests.cpp) read the ideal render at `i - best` over ours' window [warm, warm + n); a negative best shift (the ideal later than ours) read past the ideal's end (AddressSanitizer: 8 bytes, 0 bytes after the vector). The window now starts `-best` samples earlier when the shift is negative (the signal is periodic; warm is 0.4 s against shifts of at most 200 samples); nothing padded. Figures: the full write set's 150 rows of the last round moved by at most 0.3 dB (Model B, 96 kHz, cutoff then resonance 1 cycle later: -102.1 -> -102.4); the normal run's rows are among them (the 10-cycle rows at 96 kHz). The whole bside_unit runs clean under AddressSanitizer + UndefinedBehaviorSanitizer (times in Z7). **(review)** |
| Z2 | **Combinations of resonance changes against a twin of the documented rule** (mutations M8, M5, M5b). | `dynamicsTwin` runs the rule (each change begun at its window's first sample, placed at the window's centre; at most 32 waiting, a further one applies the oldest at once; at most 4 mixed in one sample, beyond that the last kept one takes the newest resonance; the newest begun output equation on the one state) on the voice's own filter input and must equal the voice's filter output to 1e-12 of its peak, every sample. Cases: two resonance writes 0, 1, 3 and 10 cycles apart, the same value, two values, back to the old value (both models, 88.2, 96, 192 kHz); 4 changes in one sample (the limit), 5 and 6 (beyond); 32 changes waiting (the limit) and 33 (one beyond), Model B (on Model A the write queue of the same size fills first at that density). All equal to 1e-12. Mutations: the final resonance of a multi-change sample taken from the first change (M8): fails (0.22 of peak); the pending limit 31 (M5): fails (0.027); kMaxStepMix 3 (M5b): fails (5e-4). **(review)** |
| Z3 | **The write gate holds the rows the contract named** (and the fourth review's worst). | Normal run (96 kHz, voice at 2 kHz, both models, 22 rows, 22 s): resonance 0 <-> 15 through the low-, band-, high- and band + high-pass, 4 <-> 12 through band + high-pass, and the 10-cycle combinations; worst -91.9 dB (Model B, band + high-pass). Full set by tag (240 rows, 567 s): resonance alone 0 <-> 15 and 4 <-> 12 through all four outputs with volume DC on and off, and the combinations at 1, 10, 40 and 200 cycles, 88.2, 96 and 192 kHz, voices at 1 and 2 kHz; every row gated (the ideal's own error at most -109.5 dB), none over -90; worst -90.7 dB (Model B, 88.2 kHz, resonance 0 <-> 15 through band + high-pass, 2 kHz; the fourth review measured -90.7 too), then -91.9 (same, 96 kHz), -92.3 (high-pass, 88.2 kHz). Inside the budget by 0.7 dB at worst: reported here, not changed. Mutation M7 (the ideal without Model B's pre-filter): fails the normal run (band + high-pass -86.9 dB). **(review)** |
| Z4 | **The cost of a resonance write, also with the cutoff moving** (chip_bench, 96 kHz, three runs, load 2.6 to 2.7). | Cutoff standing still: about 0.37 us (Model B), 0.58 us (Model A) per write (`resB` - `plainB`, `resA` - `plainA`, x 64). Cutoff moving (`resMoveB` - `moveB`, `resMoveA` - `moveA`): about 2.1 and 3.3 us: each step copy, the filter and the newest output parameters rebuild processWithCutoff's table after setResonance (the table depends on the resonance), about three rebuilds of 0.6 to 0.85 us. Not all necessary: the newest output parameters already hold the new table and the copy and the filter could take theirs from it (two of three); not done, recorded for S4. A tick writing cutoff, resonance, mode and volume at once, cutoff moving (`tickB`, `tickA` - move): about 3.5 and 6.0 us per voice (the fourth review: 4.4 / 8.1 us). No-write path: `s3bB` 6.5 to 7.0 ns (budget 7; 7.0 in the first run). **(review)** |
| Z5 | **Records corrected.** | `ChipVoice.h`: the Shared owner's S4 obligation described the abandoned two-filter scheme; rewritten for the one-state rule (one shared state, one mixed step at the write's place, the configurations' output equations blended on it). ONE STATE: on Model A an old configuration's output function acts on the newest configuration's low-pass value (the 2x part carries one low-pass stream), bounded by the write gate (Model A at most -97 dB). TIMING: the capacities with what bursts beyond them cost (the fourth review: six changing writes in 30 cycles -80 dB on Model A; forty resonance writes one per cycle -67 dB Model A, -75.7 dB Model B; six in one sample -90.4 dB) and the write costs of Z4. **(review)** |
| Z6 | **The "place already passed" branch in stepDynamics was unreachable; removed.** | A change is pushed at the first sample of its write's window, at least 7 samples before its place, and stepDynamics runs at every filter sample. Checked with an abort in the branch over the whole default bside_unit (336 test cases) and the full write set: never reached. The branch is gone; the comment says why. **(review)** |
| Z7 | **Sanitizer run times of bside_unit** (ASan + UBSan, RelWithDebInfo, the gate's flags, this Mac). | The whole bside_unit run directly, load average 4 to 5 (another agent was building): 701 s at the branch point (247fc84, 317 test cases, 6 skipped) and 970 s now (336, 6 skipped); clean (no AddressSanitizer or UndefinedBehaviorSanitizer report). The added 269 s are the S3b-2 tests (the alias gate in every constellation for the class candidates, the write gates against the 32-times render). **(review)** |

## B-Side S3b-1: two buses and the whole chain (2026-11-23, all review)

Contract: `docs/architecture.md` 8.19.4, part "S3b-1". Code: `source/bside/chip/Chip.h` ("BUSES"), `ChipRender.cpp`,
`ChipVoice.*` (new), `OutputStage.*` and `Filter.*` (entry points only; no law changed). Tests: `tests/bside/unit/
ChipBusTests.cpp`, `ChipVoiceTests.cpp` (new), `tests/bside/trace/AnalogChain.h` (`renderChain` is now the real chain),
`AnalogReferenceTests.cpp`. Fitting and checking data generated with the built adapter under the developer cache
(folder `s3b1`), never committed; no committed reference data was regenerated and no constant was set on it.

| # | Point | Reading taken (reason; measured figures) |
|---|---|---|
| D1 | **The buses' interface.** | `Chip::renderBuses` / `renderBusesUntil` WRITE (not add) six arrays per internal sample in double: the filter bus, the direct bus, each bus's envelope sum (the converter values) and voice count; `BusBlock::from (k)` offsets them. Writing, not adding: the envelope sums and counts are per chip and must not be summed by accident; a Shared owner sums what it needs itself (`ChipVoice::filterInputAc`). The single sum (`render`) is unchanged: all 299 earlier `bside_unit` tests and every renderer gate pass on it unchanged; it equals the buses' sum within the shaped rounding's 4.75 float steps over random schedules with routing writes (Model A with the bend off, D3). **Where the contract's "equals the sum of the buses" cannot hold:** a muted oscillator 3 (0x18 bit 7, not routed) is in the single sum and on neither bus (8.4.3 row 14 mutes it on the direct path only). **(review)** |
| D2 | **A routing write is a band-limited step of each oscillator's bus membership, on the long kernel, at the write's cycle** (corrected by D14: one cycle before it, as the reference). | Membership 0/1 per bus; the step is placed in the rings like every other step (same latency, same `base` convention), its band-limited part kept per oscillator (`devF`, `devD`) and the naive part as a ring of routing bytes, written only for 32 samples after a change. The long kernel because the step multiplies a full-band oscillator (the short kernel's stop band, about 85 dB, leaves less margin to -90 dB); tested against the long kernel's own table at the write's cycle, resolved to one cycle (0x17, 0x18 bit 7, `setFilter`, `powerOnKeepingTime`). The write does not end the renderer's piece: the oscillators render exactly as without it (any split stays bit-identical; their rendering is shared with the single sum). The batched path's constant loop waits until the route ring has settled (32 samples after a routing write); the general path and the batched sample loop keep the ring. **(review)** |
| D3 | **Model A's voice bend is applied on the buses** (`OutputStage::shapeVoice`, per voice, from its contribution and its envelope value). | `OutputStage.h` places it in the renderer (decisions "B-Side S3a round 2" R3); without it the distortion gates `a_distortion_a` (s00, s01: second harmonic 17.6 and 4.9 dB low) and `a_s3a_dist_a` fail through the real chain (checked by switching it off, fix round). **Corrected in the fix round:** the first version also said `a_dac_a`'s triangle would fail without it; false: worst line 1.60 dB without the bend, 1.70 dB with it (allowed 3), so that gate does not pin the bend. It is a static curve at the internal rate and aliases (on a bus: Model A triangle+saw at 96 kHz -69.1 dB at 1 kHz, -80.8 dB at 3.85 kHz; -62 dB for saw+pulse at 88.2 kHz): S3b-2's work (8.19.4 measures -53 dB for the bend and mixer together). The buses' alias test therefore measures Model A with the bend off (test aid `renderBendVoices`) and reports it on. **(review)** |
| D4 | **Volume and mode writes: the configurations before and after are blended with the long kernel's unit step, before the board filters.** | A write of 0x18's low seven bits starts a transition at the first ring sample of its step's window: a copy of the stage and, Per Note, of the filter (which runs on beside the current one on the same input; mode writes do not change the filter's poles, so both states stay equal) keep the old configuration; every sample the mixer outputs (and the post gains, volume DC off) of all open configurations are summed with weights h_i - h_i+1 (h the unit steps, `Chip::unitStepTaps`, the rings' arithmetic) and the board filters run once on the blend. With volume DC on a volume write is therefore the chip's own step at its full size (tested: the output equals the old-volume render plus (new - old) times the long kernel's step one cycle before the write's cycle (D14; the first version: at the write's cycle) within 1e-6 of the step; one cycle early or late is more than 100 times worse). Bounds: 4 configurations (a fifth write within 17 samples retires the oldest at once, a step that is not band-limited), 32 pending writes per queue (beyond: D17; the first version applied the new write at once, out of order). A change of the mute bit alone is the renderer's (D2), not blended. Cutoff and resonance writes apply from the write's own sample, not blended (a cutoff jump is the filter's own change of state, which the chip makes too). **(review)** |
| D5 | **The cutoff runs per sample only while it moves.** | A cutoff change less than 256 samples after the previous one switches the filter (and any open copy) to `processWithCutoff` until the cutoff has stood still for 256 samples; then `setCutoff` and `process` (cheaper: `s3aB` 5.5 against `cutB` 10.4 ns, K4). A write every tick (a program's sweep) stays on `process` with one `setCutoff` per write. Decided per output sample from the sample index: block splits do not change it (tested). **(review)** |
| D6 | **The write budget is measured on what the writes add.** | On the chain's output before the board filters the static curves alias on their own at the internal rate, with no write at all: held states (Model B, 1 to 2 kHz) -79 dB for a pulse, -92 to -95 dB for a saw; Model A -61 dB (saw, pulse) to -89 dB: above -90 dB without any write (S3b-2's work). So the test renders the same chain held in each of the two states and compares the output with the writes against the two switched by the ideal band-limited step (the long kernel's table) at each write's cycle; what remains is what the writes add, measured off the signal's lines relative to the output's strongest line, every tick, 88.2 / 96 / 192 kHz, 110 / 1000 / 2000 Hz: routing (saw, pulse), oscillator 3's mute, volume (volume DC on and off), mode (low <-> high, low <-> band): worst -111.5 dB (Model A routing, pulse); Model B worst -121.2 dB. Volume and mode writes are blends by construction (D4), so their figures (-198 dB and below) show only that the blend is the band-limited switch at the right cycle; a naive switch fails the test (mutation table). The raw output figures are printed beside them. **(review)** |
| D7 | **"No waveform selected": no new constant; the digital hold and fade on the routed bus meets generated data.** | The renderer already renders the digital core's held and fading output (8.15 timing constants, followed); with the buses it reaches the bus its oscillator is routed to. Checked against eight traces generated for this step (both models; a pulse held high and a 1 kHz-ish saw; direct and through the low-pass at 2047; four deselections each, 1.6 s held then fading): sample-aligned (delay 0), the 25 ms frame means of our chain's output and the reference's differ by at most 0.0048 reference units (rms 0.0012 to 0.0029) on steps of 0.1 to 0.18; the residual is a constant offset of about 0.002 that follows the reference's board high-pass dead band (8.15.6). Nothing to fit. Proposed as a gate trace (D12). **(review)** |
| D8 | **Shared through the chain** (`ChipVoice::renderBusesUntil`, `filterInputAc`, `staticFilterBusDc`, `finishShared`). | The owner sums every chip's `filterInputAc` plus one chip's `staticFilterBusDc` into one Filter and hands each voice the output and the filter's static output of one chip's static DC; each voice takes `OutputStage::sharedShare` (the double form of `sharedFilterShare`). With real chips (a 110 Hz triangle at full envelope routed to the filter, cutoff 900, all three modes, a volume step 0 -> 15): Model B equals n Per Note chips at n = 1, 2, 16 (DC step 0.000 dB, rms ratio 1.0000); Model A n = 2 / 4 / 8 / 16: DC step -0.15 / +0.60 / +1.88 / +2.62 dB, rms 1.014 / 0.724 / 0.365 / 0.195 of n Per Note chips, inside the figures `OutputStage.h` pins (+-0.3 dB, +-0.05). In Shared the voice's 0x18 still sets its stage; the shared filter's cutoff, resonance and mode are the owner's (8.5), so mode transitions blend the stages only. **Corrected in the fix round:** "Model B equals Per Note" holds for IDENTICAL voices only (the test's case); with different notes each chip's mixer curve sees its share of the summed filter output, and Model B differs from Per Note by -38.8 dB (n = 2) to -31.8 dB (n = 16) rms, all voices to the filter (mixed routings -32.1 / -30.4 dB); Model A -10.2 / -1.2 dB (D16). The static output per block was wrong across routing writes (D16). **(review)** |
| D9 | **The trace gates through the real chain** (`AnalogChain.h` `renderChain` = ChipVoice; S3a's harness kept as `renderChainTestSide` for `FilterDiagnosticTests.cpp`). | Every S3a `[analog]` gate passes through the real chain. Moved report lines (all others identical): `a_routing_b` outliers 3.15 -> 3.17 % (gated, allowed 5); `a_routing_a` 30.04 -> 30.15 % (reported); `a_modes` Model A 4.82 -> 4.93 % (gated, allowed 5; mean 0.84 -> 0.85 dB), Model B 2.46 -> 2.48 %; `a_s3a_modes_a` 6.86 -> 6.87 % (reported). Nothing is ambiguous any more (no committed trace was still skipped: S3a's per-voice harness had already covered `a_routing_*` and `a_modes_*`). **Model A routing, evidence for 8.19.3:** `a_routing_a` split by frames: the 45 frames with no voice routed to the filter 5.34 % outliers, mean 0.91 dB, level 0.34 dB (just outside the 5 %); the 207 with a voice routed through the filter (cutoff 300, several voices: R1, R2) 35.6 %, mean 4.15 dB; Model B's same split 1.71 % / 0.26 dB and 3.51 % / 0.66 dB. Reported, not gated. **(review)** |
| D10 | **New gate: the triangle segment of `a_dac_*`** (8.19.4). | s18 (110 Hz triangle, direct, full level) through the chain: harmonics 2..8 above -60 dB re the fundamental within the distortion tolerance of 8.9.4 (+-3 dB): Model A worst 1.70 dB (h2 -33.5 against -34.2, h4 -44.4 against -42.7), Model B 0.60 dB (h2 -39.2 against -39.8). It does not depend on Model A's voice bend (1.60 dB without it; D3 corrected); it gates the converters and the mixer curve. The saws (s16, s17) through the chain: within 0.10 dB (printed). **(review)** |
| D11 | **Cost** (`chip_bench`, this Mac, arm64, best of three runs of best of 5, ns per internal sample at 96 kHz; the base is 7d854ff's binary, run interleaved with ours, load average about 2.7). | **The single sum** (`render`) is within +0.5 of the base in every case (`typical` 12.6 -> 13.1; `s4gate` 30.3 -> 29.8, `combined` 91.1 -> 89.8, the rest equal or lower). **The buses** (`renderBuses`, `CHIP_BENCH_BUSES=1`): Model B `typical` 13.7 (+1.1), `silent` 2.3 (+0.4), `comb12kB` 154.9 (-1.2), `noise` 160.0 to 161.1 (+1.0; +3.1 at 192 kHz, 107.5 against 104.4, not explained); Model A with its bend (the stage's per-voice work moved into the buses, D3): `typicalA` 36.5 (+1.7), `sawsA` 42.9 (+1.5), `s4gate` 32.8 (+2.5), `combined` 92.5 (+1.4); without the bend (`CHIP_BENCH_NOBEND=1`): `typicalA` 34.3, `sawsA` 40.4, `s4gate` 30.5 (+0.2), `combined` 90.1. **Not met:** the contract's 1 ns on `typical` (+1.1, the run-to-run scatter is about 0.3) and `noise` at 192 kHz (+3.1). Already taken: a membership table per routing byte and the common case written out (from +2.3 on `typical`); the six separate stores cost about 0.5 of it (measured by leaving four out); an array of six-value records instead of six arrays was not tried. **The chain** (the S4 gate's patch, oscillators 1 and 2 through the low-pass, cutoff 1024, resonance 8, volume 15): `chainB` 26.2, `chainA` 41.0 with a cutoff write every tick; `moveB` 34.1, `moveA` 63.6 with one every 64 samples (`processWithCutoff`); the buses alone `busesB` 19.2, `busesA` 32.8. So the filter, the stage and the voice's bookkeeping cost 7.0 (B) and 8.2 (A) ns, 14.9 and 30.8 ns with a moving cutoff. Against S4's 8.5 (about 31 ns per chip at 96 kHz, filter included): Model A's chain is 41.0 ns, the owner's budget question. **(review)** |
| D12 | **Proposed gate traces for the next reference regeneration** (not committed data). | (1) The fade pairs of D7 (generator `s3b1/fade/gen.py` in the developer cache): Model A and B, oscillator 1 pulse width 0 high and saw at register 0x0400, sustain 15, routed direct and through the low-pass at 2047, deselected four times for 1.6 s; gate: 25 ms frame means within 0.01 reference units. (2) A routing trace with writes inside notes (a voice moved between the buses while it sounds, every 20 ms), both models, for the write path through the reference. **(review)** |
| D13 | **GCC 13 `-Warray-bounds` in `renderLines`** (decisions C9, open since S3a). | The piece-event adders (`addRaw`, `addJump`, `addHeld`, `addFlip`) return at once for an oscillator outside 0..2 (never reached; the event's oscillator comes from a crossing variable GCC 13 cannot bound). Checked with g++ 13.3 (the Ubuntu 24.04 image of `scripts/linux-check.sh`): every chip source, every `bside_unit` and `bside_trace` source and `chip_bench` compile with warnings as errors. **(review)** |
| D14 | **Write timing follows the reference** (fix round; 8.19.4 "After the independent review"). | A routing write (0x17 bits 0..2, oscillator 3's mute 0x18 bit 7, `setFilter`, `powerOnKeepingTime`) and a volume or mode write take effect `Chip::kAnalogWriteLeadCycles` = 1 cycle before the renderer's convention for an oscillator write at the same cycle (`renderRouteChange`, `renderEventTime`; the step's `base` stays within 0 .. 2 at every rate of 4.4). Measured with the reviewer's isolated-step traces (16 steps each, 48 kHz midpoints of the reference's debug output against `renderChain`, mean offset in cycles; 192 / 384 kHz internal): volume +0.91 / +0.99 -> -0.10 / +0.00 (both models), routing Model A +1.03 / +1.23 -> +0.03 / +0.21, Model B +0.93 / +1.19 -> -0.10 / +0.20, mute +0.90 / +0.99 -> -0.10 / +0.00 (B +0.89 / +0.98 -> -0.10 / 0.00), mode +0.89 / +0.98 -> -0.10 / +0.00 (both); pulse-width writes (unchanged, the control) -0.08 / -0.01 (A), -0.11 / 0.00 (B). Every write kind within a quarter of a cycle on average. Why one cycle: an integer convention for registers that only the analog stages read; the data shows no fractional part beyond the method's 0.1 cycle. Cutoff and resonance writes keep their sample (not measured against the reference). Tests whose expectation changed: `buses: a routing write moves ...` (the step expected one cycle before the write's cycle; the old place is now a failing "late" case), `chain: a volume write with volume DC on is the chip's own step ...` (likewise), the write-budget test's ideal switch (same convention). The convention is pinned in `ChipVoiceTests.cpp` (`kReferenceLeadCycles`, the measured offsets in its comment). **(review)** |
| D15 | **Proposed gate traces for the next reference regeneration: the isolated-step traces** (not committed data; the reviewer's, under the developer cache `s3b1-review/traces`). | `vstep_*`, `route_*`, `mute_*`, `mode_*`, `pwstep_*` for Model A and B: oscillator 1 (oscillator 3 for the mute) a held tone, cutoff 2047, one register written every 19 997 cycles from cycle 60 000 to 359 955 (0x18 volume steps 0x0f / 0x00 / 0x08 / ...; 0x17 0x00 <-> 0x01; 0x18 0x1f <-> 0x9f; 0x18 0x1f <-> 0x4f; pulse width 0x03 0x0f <-> 0x00), end 419 952. Gate: the mean midpoint offset per trace within 0.25 cycle at 192 kHz, the step sizes within 0.5 dB (volume steps today: 0.34 dB). **(review)** |
| D16 | **Shared is exact per sample** (fix round). | `finishShared` takes the shared filter's static output per bus sample (an array the owner fills beside the filter's output: one `staticOutput` of the static DC it fed in, a quintic at most) instead of one value per owner block; the voice sets it on its stage per sample (compared, so an update only where it changes), and `OutputStage::sharedShare` returns the filter's output itself for one chip. The first build: n = 1 off Per Note by up to 0.0305 (Model B) and 0.145 (Model A) reference units after a routing write with volume DC off, until the owner's block ended, so the output depended on the owner's block length. Now (the reviewer's harness): n = 1 differences 0 (B and A, volume DC on and off, writes inside the block), n = 2 identical for owner blocks of 64, 17 and 1. Tested: n = 1 bit for bit Per Note and n = 2 bit for bit across block lengths 64 / 17 / 1, with routing, mute and volume writes, volume DC on and off, both models. Chosen over the voice computing it from a const reference to the shared filter: the owner knows which chip's static DC it fed in (with different routings the chips' own differ). Shared no longer updates the never-prepared filter copies of the blending configurations. **(review)** |
| D17 | **A full write queue applies its oldest entry at once** (fix round). | Reachable: a write waits about latency + 1 = 9 samples; at 88.2 kHz a cutoff write every 3 cycles fills 32 (any number of writes at one cycle does). The first version applied the NEW write at once and the queued older ones after it, so a stale value won. Now the oldest pending entry is applied as if its sample had come (a cutoff or resonance lands up to 9 samples early; a 0x18 entry opens its blending window early, where its weight is still 0, so it lands exactly), then the new write waits; order and every value kept. Tested with a cutoff write every cycle at 88.2 kHz (568 overflows): the cutoff in effect at every output sample equals the rule's, written out in the test; 40 0x18 writes at one cycle stay bounded. `ChipVoice.h` "QUEUE". **(review)** |
| D18 | **The chain's latency is a run-time value** (fix round). | `ChipVoice::latencySamples ()`, fixed at `prepare`, today the renderer's `Chip::kLatencySamples` (kept); `ChipVoice::kLatencySamples` is gone, its users (`AnalogChain.h`, the chain tests) read the instance's. S3b-2's rate-dependent pre-filters add their delay there. **(review)** |
| D19 | **Tests that can fail** (fix round; 8.19.4 "After the independent review"). | New tests, each shown failing on a mutation (logs `s3b1-fix/mut` in the developer cache): volume with volume DC off scales the output by the volume ratio (kills `postGain` always 1); a reference computation of the chain from a twin chip's buses with the rule "per sample while the cutoff moved within 256 samples, else setCutoff" and "from the write's own sample" written out with its numbers, bit for bit (kills `kCutoffSettle = 0`, always moving, cutoff and resonance one sample late, resonance ignored); silent voices with volume DC off settle to 0 before the board filters at every routing (kills the static DC not refreshed on the voice counts; measured 0 (B) and 4e-17 (A)); Shared exact per sample; the different-notes figure pinned at -38.8 +-1 dB (measured -38.76); the full queue. `AnalogReferenceTests.cpp`: the `a_routing_*` comment describes the real chain; the `ambiguousSamples == 0` REQUIREs removed (`renderChain` has both buses, nothing is ambiguous by construction: they could not fail). The unit `[buses],[chain]` run and the mutation list are in the hand-over report. **(review)** |
| D20 | **Cost after the fix round** (one attempt, 8.19.4). | Profiled (`sample`, `noise` buses against single sum): about 88 % of the time is the digital core (`renderPieceAdvance`), identical in both; the bus path's own tail (`renderBusesUntil`'s self time, the six stores and four dot products) is 1.7 % against the single sum's 1.5 %, about 0.6 ns per sample. Interleaved runs (5 each, this Mac, load about 2.7): `typical` 96 kHz single 13.1 to 14.0, buses 13.7 to 14.1 (median +0.6, inside 1 ns); `noise` 192 kHz single 106.1 to 107.8, buses 107.8 to 109.1 (median +1.2, range 0.0 to +2.5). Final table (best of three runs, base binary interleaved, load about 4.3): buses over single `typical` +0.7 (96 and 192 kHz), `noise` +2.7 (96 kHz) and +2.5 (192 kHz; the base binary +2.0 in the same runs). **Not met: `noise` stays over 1 ns** (+1.2 to +2.5 at 192 kHz), about the bus tail's 0.6 ns plus scatter; `typical` is inside. The attempt: the common case's memberships selected instead of multiplied by 0 / 1, one helper for all three paths: no gain (`noise` 192 kHz 107.5 to 114.7, `typical` unchanged), reverted. Model A's bend surcharge (buses with against without, 96 kHz): `typicalA` +2.4, `sawsA` +2.9, `s4gate` +2.2, `combined` +2.9 ns; the chain: `chainB` 26.9, `chainA` 42.8, `moveB` 34.7, `moveA` 65.0, `busesB` 19.6, `busesA` 33.7 ns (unchanged from the base within 0.3). S3b-2 moves the bend. **(review)** |

## B-Side Model A cutoff steps, scope 512 (2026-11-23, all review)

Contract: `docs/architecture.md` 8.19.3, "Scope of the cutoff-step fix" (owner decision O9 "512") and "After the review of scope \"512\"" (rows S7 to S10). It replaces the
first round below (M1 to M10) where they differ. Code: `source/bside/chip/Filter.*` (Model A's mapping only).

| # | Point | Reading taken (reason; measured figures) |
|---|---|---|
| S1 | **Two parts, chosen by the register value.** | Codes 0 .. 479 run the S3a form's own code (the ladder of ratio 2.1756 summed bit by bit at run time, interpolated between codes, shifted by variation, ln fc over 24 points), written with the same expressions so the rounding is the same; nothing there reads the refitted converter. Codes 480 .. 2047 run the refitted form of the first round (a weight per bit, a compile-time table of the 2048 converter values, ln fc over 129 points, the first round's fit without the hand-shaped knots of M6). A register value strictly between 479 and 480 interpolates ln fc between the two. Proof (the test as first built; now S10): a test recomputes the S3a law and compares bits for every register value 0 .. 479 in quarter steps at variation 0, 0.25, 0.5, 0.75 and 1 (the variations whose shift is an exact product), on `setCutoff` and on `processWithCutoff`'s own path (`cornerWithCutoffHz`): 9585 of 9585 identical; and a dump of the S3a build against this one (corner, `linearPart`, `linearPartWithCutoff` at three rates, two resonances, seven modes, variation 0, 0.37, 0.5 and 1) is identical byte for byte with Apple clang and with GCC 11 (-O3, in the Linux container). The test's replica of the S3a law could not be exact at 0.37 under GCC (6 of 7668 corners: the compiler fused the shift into a multiply-add in one place and not in the other), hence the test's variations. The variation shift is written in the same single expression as in S3a: computing it once beforehand changed the last bit at variation 0.37 (fused multiply-add). **(review)** |
| S2 | **The join at 480 .. 511** (corrected after the review of scope 512; see S9). | The S3a corner at 479 (estimator 1490 Hz) lies 9 % below the refitted one at 480 (1635 Hz). Codes 480 .. 511 take fitted converter values (`kAJoin`) instead of the sum of their bits: from 479 with the reference's step in the committed noise walk (-2.23 %; held-out walks at three levels: -0.47, -4.53, -2.33 %), then evenly (+1.0 % per code at the -3 dB point) to code 511, which lies on the refitted curve. Their corners cannot be shaped on the curve, because code 512 lies at the same converter value as codes 484/485 there. **(review)** |
| S3 | **(Superseded by S7 and S8.)** The fall at 511 -> 512 was first set 2.2 % smaller than the noise walk's so that the sweep's lower band passed by 0.02 dB; the review found it below every noise measurement of the reference and failing the noise gate on held-out data (+2.50 points), and found the cause named here wrong: the lower band's miss is the level dependence of the reference's corner (O8), not the shape of our filter's response. **(review)** |
| S4 | **The constraint at code 612** (8.19.3: the value that keeps the committed gate). | The reference's own corner there (`a_filter_a` s06): 3292 Hz. Ours with the value that keeps `a_modes_a`: 3260 Hz (-0.97 %), `a_modes_a` 4.93 % outliers; the unconstrained fit: about 3290 Hz (+0.9 % higher, within 0.1 % of the reference), `a_modes_a` 5.01 % (allowed 5). The constrained value is used. **(review)** |
| S5 | **Gates and committed data** (figures as built after S7). | Noise walk: every step from 479 -> 480 up to 1600, within 2 points: worst 1.43 (1151), 52 steps (at 1599 and 1600 the reference's corner lies beyond what the estimator resolves in every pass); `a_cutcode_0_a` .. `a_cutcode_4_a` + one bypass twin, codes 479 .. 1600 only. Sweep: the reference's step per write as constants (`tests/bside/trace/CutSweepReference.h`, measured with `SweepSteps.h` on the adapter's 48 kHz render of the committed `a_cutsweep_a`); every write with both codes at or above 480 (304 per band) within 0.5 dB except the named exception of S8, every four such consecutive writes within 0.7: worst 0.34 dB (2 .. 5 kHz, 622 -> 628) and 0.23 dB (0.8 .. 2 kHz, 693 -> 687), four-write sums 0.44 / 0.65; S3a's form had 0.58 / 0.79 and 0.77 / 0.87. Writes below 480 are printed where they differ by more than 0.5 dB (O9: open). Reference data 2.0 MB -> 1.5 MB (27 -> 21 files). **(review)** |
| S6 | **Committed gates and reported lines** (as built after S7). | `a_filter_a`: worst 4.97 % (s03, code 306, the S3a value), s05 (510) +3.72 -> -0.96 %, every other line as S3a's or within 1.3 %; peaks unchanged (0.22 dB). `a_modes_a` 4.93 %, 0.85 dB (unchanged). Rate gate Model A 0.104 dB, phase 33.67 degrees (allowance unchanged), `processWithCutoff` 0.049 dB / 0.782 degrees, at every boundary 0.0489 dB / 0.782 degrees. Model B: bit-identical (output hashes of `process` and `processWithCutoff` over random settings equal). `a_s3a_*` corner lines that moved by 1 point or more against S3a's form (the list is those; many others moved by less, e.g. `a_s3a_level_a` s03 -6.0 -> -6.5 %, `a_s3a_res_a` s15 -0.9 -> -1.4 %): `a_s3a_cutoff_a` s09 (code 480) -8.6 -> -9.9 % (worse), s10 (490) -3.5 -> -6.2 % (worse), s11 (500) -0.2 -> -4.0 % (worse); `a_s3a_voices_a` s02 +224.5 -> +223.4 % (better), s13 +72.5 -> +71.3 % (better). Cost (96 kHz, same flags, three runs, load average 3.1 / 2.4 / 3.0, measured before S7; S7 changes one constant): `cutA` 29.3 to 29.6 -> 21.0 to 21.2 ns, which holds for cutoffs from 480 up (the bench's are); below 480 `processWithCutoff` costs what it did (the review: 32.9 -> 31.9 ns); `s3aA` 8.0 -> 7.8, `filterA` 4.2 -> 3.9, `cutB` 11.0 -> 10.9, `s3aB` 5.8 -> 5.8; `setCutoff` Model A 83.1 -> 67.7 ns. **(review)** |
| S7 | **The fall at 511 -> 512 on the reference** (8.19.3, after the review of scope 512). | Code 511 lies on the refitted curve: the fall is -15.38 % in the committed noise walk (the reference's -14.99 %: 0.39 points; held-out walks of the review at three levels -15.5, -17.7, -17.9 %), the -3 dB point by the analytic estimate -15.0 %, the converter value d by 6.0 %. In the owner's sweep the 2 .. 5 kHz step at 510 -> 515 is -1.44 dB against the reference's -1.67 (0.23 dB miss). **(review)** |
| S8 | **One named exception to the sweep gate: 510 -> 515 and 515 -> 510 in 0.8 .. 2 kHz.** | The reference does not move there (+0.07 / -0.07 dB); ours falls -0.46 / rises +0.46 dB, a miss of 0.53 dB, and stays 0.40 to 0.53 dB for any fall between -11 and -15 %. Cause: at full level the reference's corner at 511 is about 40 % above ours, so that band lies below its corner: the level dependence of Model A's corner, parked (O8). Gated at the measured miss + 0.05 dB (0.58), named in the test (`AnalogReferenceTests.cpp`); every other write keeps 0.5 dB and every four-write sum 0.7 dB (the sums containing these writes meet it: worst 0.65 dB in that band). **(review)** |
| S9 | **The join checked against the reference.** | Its two ends are steps of the noise-walk gate (479 -> 480: ours -2.41 %, reference -2.23 %; 510 -> 511: ours +1.44 %, reference +1.18 %); the rise from 480 to 511 is gated within the range the reference shows across levels (+18 to +43 %): ours +37.2 %, the committed walk +20.4 %. A single join value raised by 1 % in the corner is caught only by the pinned-law test. **(review)** |
| S10 | **The bit-identity oracle is the S3a law itself, verbatim, in its own translation unit** (`tests/bside/unit/S3aCutoffLaw.*`). | A replica inside the test file was not exact under GCC at variation 0.37 (a multiply-add fused in one place and not in the other), and committed values from one build could not hold for another compiler's `exp`. The verbatim copy, with the S3a function boundaries, matched this build bit for bit under Apple clang and under GCC 11 (-O2 and -O3) at 200000 pseudo-random register values and variations. The test covers every quarter step at six variations (0.37 included) and 200000 pseudo-random values up to 479.0 (300.1 included) on `setCutoff` and on `processWithCutoff`'s own path: 211502 of 211502 identical. "Below 480" means register values up to and including 479.0. **(review)** |

## B-Side Model A cutoff steps (2026-11-23, all review)

Contract: `docs/architecture.md` 8.19.3, "Model A's cutoff curve at the converter's boundaries" (owner decision O8
"jumps"). Code: `source/bside/chip/Filter.*` (Model A's converter and curve only). Tests: `tests/bside/trace/
AnalogReferenceTests.cpp` (two gates), `SweepSteps.h` (new), `tests/bside/unit/FilterOutputTests.cpp`. Reference data:
`tests/bside/tools/gen_cutcode_traces.py` (new) and the adapter of the MANIFEST. Fitting data (never committed, under
the developer cache, folder `modela-steps`): the same walk at sustain 8 (codes 0 .. 1632 every 16, 256 .. 464 every 4,
two passes) and scatter studies.

| # | Point | Reading taken (reason; measured figures) |
|---|---|---|
| M1 | **Reference data: `a_cutcode_0_a` .. `a_cutcode_6_a`, one shared bypass twin, `a_cutsweep_a`.** | One noise voice at sustain 4 ("a low level"), low-pass, resonance 0; 24-frame segments, 60 per file. Every multiple of 32 from 64 to 2016 (where the converter falls back): codes b-1, b, and b-2 at multiples of 64 (one step inside the stretch). Two passes over every code (up, down), six more over 446 .. 640, each at another place so each sees other noise; the corner per code is the mean over the passes whose corner the estimator resolves. Every file has the same write cycles, so one bypass twin serves all (checked: the twins of three files have identical segment spectra); 2.0 MB with the sweep. `a_cutsweep_a` has the writes of the owner's listening case; its adapter render is identical to the study's reference file. **(review)** |
| M2 | **What the reference does at the boundaries** (sustain 4, mean over passes). | It falls back at every multiple of 32, as the converter does: 511 -> 512 by 14.8 %, 1023 -> 1024 by 9.4 %, 767 -> 768 by 5.4 %, 575 -> 576 by 3.6 %, 447 -> 448 by 4.9 %, 1535 -> 1536 by 1.9 %, odd multiples of 32 by 0.2 to 1.7 %; between boundaries it rises by 0.1 to 2 % per code. The fixed form fell by 22.1 % at 512 (estimator; 17.4 % in its prototype corner) and by 1.5 % at 576: worst 7.3 percentage points (512). The step at 512 depends on the level (sustain 2: -45 %, the knee sits on it; sustain 8: about -18 %); at 768 and 1024 it does not (sustain 2 and 4 within 0.4 points). **(review)** |
| M3 | **Converter: a fitted weight per bit.** | A single ladder ratio (best 2.197) leaves the falls at 544, 576, 608, 704 about half the reference's (worst 2.8 points). Bits 5 .. 10 are fitted, bits 0 .. 4 keep the ladder's shape (fitting them too gave bit 0 1.6 times the ladder's and falls at multiples of 16 the data do not support). Weights in `Filter.cpp` `kAConverter`; within 2 % of the ladder's except bits 0 .. 4 (+6 % together). The converter is a table of 2048 values built at compile time. **(review)** |
| M4 | **Curve: ln fc piecewise linear over 129 points of d** (was 24). | From code 446 up: the reference's shape at sustain 4 (a spline fitted to the per-code means, 24 knots) plus a level offset fitted on the sustain-8 walk and the `a_filter_a` corners (weight 3), flat across the intervals of the falls at 512, 768 and 1024 so that it adds nothing to those steps. Below 446: the fixed form's corner per code, refitted to the new converter. Above 1600 (no corner the estimator resolves): the fixed form's shape, joined. Committed gate data used for constants (as R10): the `a_filter_a` corners set the level offset, and the corner at 612 is pinned to the fixed form's (the `a_modes_a` trace is at 612; 0.9 % higher there moved its outliers from 4.93 to 5.01 %, allowed 5). **(review)** |
| M5 | **The per-code gate covers codes 446 .. 1600; below 446 it is reported.** | At sustain 4 the reference's corner at one code varies by 5 to 25 % from segment to segment below about 450 (six passes at 380 .. 388: standard error of a step 5 to 22 points), so a 2-point gate there measures noise; longer segments did not reduce it. Above 1600 neither side has a corner below 20 kHz. Result: worst 1.36 points (575; 56 steps), before 7.30 (512). Monotonic: wherever the reference's mean rises by more than 0.5 % (the resolution of two passes) our mapping rises (7 of 7); the unit test also requires ours to rise at every code that is not a multiple of 32 and to fall at every multiple. **(review)** |
| M6 | **(Superseded by S1 to S6: the region below 480 is the S3a form again.)** The knee at 383 -> 384 made flat. | Below 446 the data disagree: at sustain 4 the reference does not fall at 384 (+0.5 %, unresolved), in the owner's sweep (full level) its band step there is -0.5 dB, at sustain 8 it falls by about 7 %. The fixed form fell by 10 % (prototype), -1.4 dB in the sweep; the refitted converter made it 10.2 %, -1.8 dB. The knots around d = 0.19 .. 0.23 are reshaped (least squares on the codes 300 .. 430, the fall at 384 set to 2.7 % in the prototype corner): codes 368 .. 400 move by up to 7 %; `a_filter_a` at 306 and 408 unchanged; the sweep's step at 384 is -0.8 dB. The reported `a_s3a_cutoff_a` corner at 380 (sustain 8) moves from +2.6 % to +6.0 % in one of its four repeats (mean of the four from -0.5 to +2.6 %). The owner may prefer the sustain-8 shape (the fall kept, -1.8 dB in the sweep): one change of knots. **(review)** |
| M7 | **The sweep gate: band steps by a zero-phase Butterworth band, the reference's figure a constant.** | The study's method with its brick-wall FFT band replaced by a 4th-order Butterworth band run forward and backward (`SweepSteps.h`), in the test and for the reference alike. The reference's audio is not committed data (its `.bsspec` frames are 25 ms, too coarse for one period), so its figure is measured outside with the same function on the adapter's render of the committed trace and written in the test: 1.665 dB (2 .. 5 kHz), 0.897 dB (0.8 .. 2 kHz). Ours: before 2.233 / 0.911 dB (fails the gate), after 1.398 / 0.601. Gated per band. **(review)** |
| M8 | **`processWithCutoff` needs no new table.** | Its table is over the corner, not the register: the corner is computed from the register value exactly as `setCutoff` does, and the table holds only the parameters that depend smoothly on the corner (damping, weights, g' against tan). A fall-back is therefore never interpolated across. New test: at and around every multiple of 32 (b - 1, b - 0.5, b, b + 0.5, b + 1), every rate, mode sum, resonance 0 / 8 / 15 and variation 0 / 0.5 / 1: within 0.0489 dB and 0.782 degrees of `setCutoff` (allowed 0.05 and 1). Smearing the converter across neighbouring codes in `processWithCutoff` fails it (5.3 dB). **(review)** |
| M9 | **Cost.** | `chip_bench` at 96 kHz, this Mac, load average 3.5 to 3.9 (other agents), before and after built with the same flags, three runs each: `cutA` 29.3 -> 20.9 ns (the converter table; with the bit loop kept it was 31.8 to 32.0), `s3aA` 8.0 to 8.1 -> 8.0, `filterA` 4.2 -> 4.2. `setCutoff` (mean over the seven modes, resonance 8): Model A 82.7 to 82.9 -> 67.2 ns, Model B 35.5 -> 33.4. "`cutA` within 1 ns of its figure" read as "no more than 1 ns slower". **(review)** |
| M10 | **(Superseded by S6.)** Committed gates that moved. | `a_filter_a` worst corner 4.97 % (s03, code 306, unchanged; the knee below 446 keeps the fixed form's corners), s05 (510) +3.72 -> -0.82 %, s10 (1020) -2.34 -> -1.08 %, every other within 1.3 %; peaks unchanged (worst 0.22 dB); `a_modes_a` 4.93 % outliers, mean 0.85 dB (unchanged); rate gate Model A 0.104 dB, `processWithCutoff` 0.049 dB / 0.782 degrees (unchanged); phase worst 33.67 degrees (unchanged figure, within its allowance). Reported `a_s3a_*` peaks within 0.1 dB of before except where better (`a_s3a_res_a` s05 -0.18 -> -0.10 dB); `a_s3a_cutoff_a` worst corner 8.62 -> 8.42 %. |

## B-Side S3a closing step (2026-11-23, all review)

What the closing step of `docs/architecture.md` 8.19.3 changed and measured. Fitting traces (never committed, under the
developer cache, folder `s3a-close`): noise on oscillator 3 through Model B's high-pass and band-pass at cutoffs 300,
1024, 1700 and sustain 3, 8, 15, each with a bypass twin; held-out: cutoffs 600 and 1300 at sustain 5, 11, 15. No
constant of this step was set on committed gate data. Model A's filter dynamics are unchanged (8.19.3, O1).

| # | Point | Reading taken (reason; measured figures) |
|---|---|---|
| C1 | **Rate dependence: matched poles, a numerator designed per mode combination** (O2). | `Filter` keeps its per-sample arithmetic (the trapezoidal state-variable filter in state-space form); only its parameters change. Poles: matched (exp (p T)); (g, k) solved for them, g^2 = (1 + a1 + a2) / (1 - a1 + a2), g k = 2 (1 - a2) / (1 - a1 + a2), without cancellation (expm1, 1 - cos as 2 sin^2; real poles by their two exponentials), so 15 Hz at 384 kHz is exact. Numerator: in closed form per mode combination (the sums are designed as wholes, not as sums of per-mode numerators: a notch is a cancellation, which per-mode magnitude fits do not preserve): its squared magnitude, a quadratic in sin^2 (w / 2), takes the prototype's DC gain exactly (the static output is unchanged) and its magnitude at min (fc, n2 / 2) and n2 = min (20 kHz, 0.4 x rate); factored with the prototype's signs at DC and half the rate (first minimum-phase; since the fix round the zeros are put on the prototype's side, C11). Model B's true notch (low + high, no band term) gets its zeros on the unit circle at the prototype's notch (the two-node fit gave 0.97 dB there). Gate (unit test `[rate]`, every rate of 4.4, mode sum, resonance, every 4th cutoff, Model A at variation 0 / 0.5 / 1; on the class's own coefficients, which a second test checks against its impulse response to 2e-7): worst **0.187 dB** Model B (high-pass, 88.2 kHz), **0.104 dB** Model A (low + high, 88.2 kHz), allowed 0.5; before (plain trapezoidal, same measure, every 8th cutoff): 3.19 / 3.23 dB at 88.2 kHz, 0.61 / 0.62 dB at 192 kHz. No setting needed the "figure + 0.1 dB" rule. A least-squares numerator on a fixed grid was tried first: degenerate where the gated band is narrow (low cutoffs, 5.6 dB at 352.8 kHz). **(review)** |
| C2 | **Model A: the mapping on its linear part.** | Its prototype is the linear part of its outputs (the static functions' linear term on the low-pass, the band weights on the band-pass, the high weight on the high-pass). The static functions stay functions of the low-pass state (now the low-pass of the mapped g, k); the linear output row carries the designed numerator minus their linear term, so the linear part is the designed one and every output keeps its meaning. Committed Model A gates at 192 kHz: corners move by up to 0.8 % (s14 -0.30 -> -1.11 %), peaks by 0.04 dB, distortion lines by 0.1 dB; all within tolerance. **(review)** |
| C3 | **`processWithCutoff`: a table of the mapping.** | Per knot g' / tan (pi fc / rate), k' and the three output weights, linear in between; Model B: a knot every 64 register units (33 knots: within 0.004 dB of `setCutoff`); Model A: four knots per octave of the corner at resonance 0, on its Q law's own knots (27 knots: within 0.049 dB), so its damping, band weights and `variation` follow the cutoff given (8.19.3; was: the stored damping and weights). Gated at 0.1 dB against `setCutoff` over the rate gate's set, on a filter whose stored cutoff is elsewhere, and in time (a swept cutoff, noise: rms difference at most 0.2 %, allowed 1.16 %). The table depends on the rate, resonance and mode only; it is rebuilt at the first call after one of them changed (bounded: 33 mappings, about 1 us). Interpolating g' itself, or over log2 fc without the Q knots, missed 0.1 dB (notch 0.47 dB; Model A at 235 Hz 0.11 dB). **(review)** |
| C4 | **Cost.** | Per sample unchanged by C1 (`process` 2.16 ns alone, as before). The coefficient update is dearer: `setCutoff` 10.2 -> 28.3 ns (Model B), 37.8 -> 63.6 ns (Model A) at the closing step; the fix round's side rule (C11) adds to it: `setCutoff` at 96 kHz, mean over the seven modes, Model B 27 to 29 -> 34 to 37 ns (largest 50 ns, the modes with one right-half-plane zero), Model A 63 -> 78 ns (largest 97 ns); `chip_bench` does not show it (below). `chip_bench` writes a cutoff every 64 samples, so `s3aB` shows it. `chip_bench` at 96 kHz, indicative. Closing step (4 runs each, load average about 3): `s3aB` 4.9 -> 5.9 to 6.0 ns (budget 7; about 0.5 from C1's updates, 0.5 from the second-order term of C6, since removed), `cutB` 9.5 -> 10.3 to 10.4 ns, `s3aA` 6.8 -> 7.5 to 7.7, `cutA` 22.9 -> 26.9 to 27.0. Fix round (C6 term removed, C11 added; 3 runs each, the base commit's binary alongside): `s3aB` 5.5 ns (base 5.9 to 6.1), `cutB` 10.3 to 10.4 (base 10.3 to 10.9), `s3aA` 7.5 (base 7.6), `cutA` 27.1 to 27.3 (base 27.0 to 27.2); one run of each was disturbed (`s3aB` 8.8, `cutA` 30.6) and is not counted. `processWithCutoff`'s table rebuild after a resonance or mode write: about 0.60 us (Model B) and 0.85 us (Model A) with the write's own update, measured (a write every sample costs that every sample; `Filter.h` "THREAD AND COST"). **(review)** |
| C5 | **Shared topology: the static DC in full to every stage** (8.19.3; `OutputStage.h` "SHARED"). | Each stage now gets the shared filter's static output for one chip's static DC in full plus 1/n of the rest (`OutputStage::sharedFilterShare`; one subtract, multiply, add per sample in the owner); `setFilterStaticDc` gets the static output in full. Before: the output and the static DC / n, so each stage's mixer curve sat at another operating point (a 0 -> 15 volume step with held routed voices: Model B +2.0 to +3.5 dB for n = 2 .. 16, Model A up to +5.8 dB). Now Model B equals n Per Note chips (0.00 dB DC, rms ratio 1.0000, n = 1 .. 16; gated at 0.05 dB and 1 %). Model A keeps a documented, pinned difference (its static functions saturate on the summed signal): full envelope, n = 2 / 4 / 8 / 16: DC step -0.1 / +0.6 / +1.9 / +2.7 dB, audio rms 1.01 / 0.72 / 0.37 / 0.20 of n Per Note chips; half envelope within 0.8 dB. Making Model A's DC match too needs each chip's own static functions per sample (a cost per chip), not done. Fix round: with the second-order term of C6 removed Model B's filter is linear and the claim is plain; the test now also runs where the band- and high-pass carry the signal (a 220 Hz sawtooth through the high-pass at cutoff 64 and through the band-pass at cutoff 300, resonance 8, volume DC on and off; with it off the output's mean is compared): n = 1 .. 16, worst DC step 0.000 dB (volume DC on), mean 0.022 dB (off), rms ratio 1.00000 (gated at 0.05 dB or 1e-6, and 1 %). With the term restored this test fails (C12). **(review)** |
| C6 | **Model B band/high second-order term: tried, then removed after the third review** (8.19.3, "After the third review"). | **The attempt (closing step).** On the fitting traces the reference's floor below the high-pass corner is flat at about -38 dB against the bypass at sustain 8 and 15, about 10 dB above ours, and grows with the level. A term 0.10 b^2 on the band- and high-pass part b of Model B's output (fitted on those noise floors; the negative branch, -0.19, fitted worse) gave: mean stop-band error, fitting traces high-pass 5.84 -> 0.25 dB, band-pass 0.56 -> 0.25; held-out high-pass 7.34 -> 0.16 dB, band-pass 0.66 -> 0.14. On the whole filter output (tried in `OutputStage`) it failed `a_routing_b` (10.4 % outliers, mean 1.21 dB). **The third review's measurement** (its own tone and noise traces, generated with the reference adapter): it agrees with the reference on noise through the high-pass (floor +0.0 to +0.8 dB) and on a high-pass triangle at 220 Hz (second harmonic -33.0 dB, reference -33.1); it over-predicts elsewhere: band-pass triangle at its centre, second harmonic -33.2 dB against the reference's -39.3 (-43.5 without the term), band-pass square at 140 Hz -31.0 against -38.1, high-pass square at 440 Hz -25.0 against -34.6 (-35.4 without), low + band triangle -37.0 against -48.5 (-41.9 without). The term's mean, 0.05 \|b\|^2, is a DC that moves with the audio; in Shared it acts on the summed band/high signal, n times too large; it aliases at -36.1 dB against the fundamental at 88.2 kHz, -61.3 dB against one voice at full scale (budget -100 dB, 8.4.5). The claims in `Filter.h` ("the stop band's floor of those two modes", "the low-pass part has none", "the term carries no DC") went further than the noise-only fit had tested. **Removed** in the fix round: Model B's `process` and `processWithCutoff` are the linear output row again, the two-tone test that pinned the term is gone, `s3aB` is back to 5.5 ns (C4). **Known deviation that remains:** on noise at mid and full level Model B's high-pass stop-band floor lies about 6 to 7 dB below the reference's (`Filter.h` "KNOWN DEVIATION"); the band-pass floor 0.66 dB on average (held-out traces). A later attempt must fit tones and noise together and meet the alias budget. The gate trace of C10 stays proposed. **(review)** |
| C7 | **Board low-pass, measured separately** (8.19.3). | Unchanged (the trapezoidal one-pole prewarped at 15.4 kHz, now pinned by a test: -2.99 dB at 15.4 kHz against 1 kHz at every rate). Its deviation from the analog one-pole at 20 kHz: -0.48 dB at 88.2 kHz, -0.39 at 96, -0.20 at 128, -0.08 at 192, -0.02 at 384 kHz. |
| C8 | **Tests that can fail** (8.19.3). | Every new or changed test was shown to fail on a mutation (the list is in the hand-over report). The held-voices volume test writes 15 -> 6 and now also requires the audio to scale by the volume once (a mutation that dropped the volume with volume DC off survived the DC checks); its volume-DC-on case requires the mixer's DC step through the board high-pass within 3 %. "every resonance and cutoff law is pinned" pins every law it names at every register value, resonance and variation. The old check that Model A's `processWithCutoff` equals `process` bit for bit at the stored cutoff is replaced by the 0.1 dB gate (8.19.3). One mutation survives by design: a Model B table four times coarser still meets 0.1 dB. The third review found what the closing step's tests left unpinned: the numerator's side (swapping b0 and b2 survived), the input guard's threshold (a guard at 1e30 survived) and Model A's table density (three knots per octave survived); the fix round pins the first two (C11, C13) and reports the third (C12). **(review)** |
| C9 | **GCC 13.** | The test sources passed temporaries to `renderedTrace` and `planNamed`, which return references into static maps: a `-Wdangling-reference` false positive; each call site now names its string. Checked with g++ 13.3 (the Ubuntu 24.04 image of `scripts/linux-check.sh`): every `bside_unit`, `bside_trace` and `chip_bench` source compiles with warnings as errors. **Open, outside this step:** `ChipRender.cpp` fails GCC 13's `-Warray-bounds` (an index the compiler cannot bound in `renderLines`); GCC 11 (the CI and preflight compiler) accepts it. **(review)** |
| C10 | **Proposed gate trace for the next reference regeneration** (not committed data). | The held-out pair of C6, generated like `gen_s3a_traces.py`'s `noise_grid`: Model B, oscillator 3 noise (frequency 0xffff, sustain retriggered per segment), routing 0x04 (twin 0x00), resonance 0, mode 0x4f (high-pass) or 0x2f (band-pass), cutoffs 600 and 1300 at sustain 5, 11, 15, one segment each (8 + 48 frames). Gate: the mean stop-band response (bins below fc / 6 for the high-pass, beyond 8x for the band-pass, where the reference is above -112 dBFS) within 1 dB. **(review)** |
| C11 | **Phase: the numerator's zeros on the prototype's side** (fix round, 8.19.3 "After the third review", "Phase"). | The magnitude design of C1 leaves two factors of the numerator with the same magnitude, one the other with every zero reflected through the unit circle (b0 and b2 swapped). The closing step kept the one with \|b0\| >= \|b2\|: the phase was up to 180 degrees off the prototype's where the prototype has zeros in the right half-plane (Model B band + high 167.5, all 180; Model A band + high 100.7, all 180; measured over the gate's set with the design copied into a harness). The fix round keeps the factor whose zeros lie where the matched images exp (s T) of the prototype numerator's zeros lie (cL + cB s + cH s^2): no zero in the right half-plane -> keep; two (Model B and Model A all three modes) -> swap; one, real (Model B low + band, band + high; Model A low + band, high, band + high) -> the factor whose zeros lie nearer the images (the other zero's image: exp (s T), 1 for a zero at the origin, -1 for one at infinity), so only the right-half-plane zero is outside. In every mode this rule picks the best of the four reflections (none, either one, both) found by that harness. Model B's notch branch is unchanged. Magnitudes are unchanged (the `[rate]` figures stay 0.187 dB Model B, 0.104 dB Model A; processWithCutoff 0.004 / 0.049 dB); the per-sample arithmetic is unchanged; the update gains up to four logarithms and two exponentials. **Gate** (unit `[rate]`, same set and band as the magnitude gate): phase within 30 degrees of the prototype; worst Model B 33.42 degrees (band-pass, 88.2 kHz, FC 244, resonance 2, at 20 kHz), Model A 33.67 (band-pass, 88.2 kHz, FC 672, resonance 15, at 20 kHz). Seven settings cannot meet 30 degrees at no per-sample cost and are gated at their figure + 2 (named in `FilterOutputTests.cpp` `phaseAllowed`): band-pass and low + band at 88.2 kHz (Model B 33.42 / 31.79, Model A 33.67 / 32.99) and band-pass of both models and Model A low + band at 96 kHz (30.62, 30.83, 30.26); all at 20 kHz, the top of the band, at low cutoffs where the prototype is just above the gate's -20 dB; there the other factor is about 180 degrees off, so a smaller figure needs another magnitude design or a third coefficient. Every other setting: at most 24.3 degrees (Model A all, 88.2 kHz). `processWithCutoff`'s table follows (`setCutoff`'s design per knot): its complex response against `setCutoff`'s, phase within 1 degree (worst 0.78 degrees, Model A all, 88.2 kHz; Model B 0.33). Mutations: swapping b0 and b2 fails the gate for both models (C12). **(review)** |
| C12 | **Mutations of the fix round** (8.19.3 "Tests that can fail"). | Each new or changed test shown failing on a mutation, each mutation reverted (logs outside the repository). Phase gate (`[rate]`, both models): b0 and b2 swapped (the reviewer's mutation), the side rule off (always minimum-phase), the other factor where one zero is in the right half-plane: each fails both models' phase check. `processWithCutoff` phase: the table's band weight negated (exactly the other factor: b0 and b2 swapped leaves wL and wH and flips wB) fails it, magnitude unchanged; Model A's table at three knots per octave still **survives** (within 0.1 dB and 1 degree; reported, not pinned). Input guard: thresholds 1e30 and 0.9e6 fail the new test. Shared: the second-order term of C6 restored (the base commit's `Filter.*`) fails the sawtooth high-pass condition (n = 2: -0.29 dB DC step) and the phase gate; the old wiring (shared output / n, static DC too) fails it. Earlier mutations rerun on the touched code: the plain trapezoidal mapping restored fails the magnitude gate of both models; notch zeros 3 % high fails Model B's magnitude gate. **(review)** |
| C13 | **Input guard pinned; the table rebuild and subnormal input documented** (fix round). | A new test pins the threshold: an input of magnitude 1e6 or more gives, sample for sample, what a twin fed 0 gives; 0.99e6 is an input like any other (guards at 1e30 and at 0.9e6 fail it). `Filter.h` "THREAD AND COST" states that `processWithCutoff` rebuilds its table at its first call after a resonance, mode or rate change (C4's measured cost), so such a write every sample costs that every sample; "INPUT GUARD" states that a subnormal input reaches the output through the direct term (the states flush below 1e-15) and that callers run with denormals disabled. **(review)** |

The 7 ns budget is not raised now. |

In the owner's words: the fixed form is too soft and lacks the edge of the reference; the signal-dependent form is far too bright and harsh; the right sound is somewhere between the two. | Owner, 2026-11-23, by ear. The fixed form still ships in S3a as the recorded open point; the next step is a form between the two (the signal dependence at reduced depth), judged by ear again before any gate work. |
| O5 | The owner listened to in-between candidates of the Model A filter (the signal dependence at 25, 50 and 75 % depth, and 60 % depth on the fixed form's rest; developer cache, 2026-11-23). | **Closest per case, none accepted as a whole:** one-voice sweep: 75 %, but the sweep has audible jumps in every file, the reference is smooth; three-voice sweep: 60 % on the fixed rest, smooth, but every file sounds clipped far more than the reference; resonant sweep: 75 %, no jumps, a little harsher than the reference; chord at growing level: 60 % on the fixed rest, spot on except the last, loudest strike, which is a little too loud; bass line: 75 %, nearly acceptable, lacks some clarity and resonance; short golden trace: 50 %, spot on. | Owner, 2026-11-23, by ear. The files do not clip (no sample above 0.82), so the clipping heard is distortion made by the chain. Next: a diagnosis of the jumps, the three-voice distortion, the loudest strike and the resonance, then one candidate form; no implementation round before the owner has heard it. |

Options put to the owner: pin as a known deviation; four times the rate for Model A's curves (estimated 10 ns more per internal sample); a study of another technique. | **Pin:** S3b-2 ships with these rows named as a known deviation, each gated at its measured figure + 1 dB so that it cannot get worse unnoticed; every other row keeps the full limits. Revisited with the Model A filter form and the S4 budget. | Owner, 2026-11-23. The precedent of 2026-11-18 (fix, no exemption) was a case audible in ordinary playing and cheap to fix. |

## B-Side S4 contract: orchestrator's calls (2026-11-24, confirmed by the owner)

Readings taken while writing contract 8.21. None changes a parameter ID or the state. **The owner confirmed all nine on 2026-11-24** (row 1: "ok"; rows 2 to 9, after a plain-language explanation of each: "leave them all as they are"), so the "(review)" marks on them in 8.21 are settled.

| # | Point | Reading taken (reason) |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Model A's CPU figures under O11:** 16 voices 11 %, Dual 22 %, Dual with the effects 27 %, 16 voices at a 192 kHz host 30 % (Model B: 5, 10, 15 and 15 %, unchanged). | The owner was told "about 10 % and 20 %". The chain alone measures 9.8 % for 16 voices; the part adds the program engine, the allocator, the note gains and the decimator, so a gate at exactly 10 % would fail on the part's overhead or tempt a cut in Model A's sound, which O10 rules out. The figures are Model B's times the measured ratio of the two chains (2.1), rounded up, with the effects' 5 % added unscaled. They are ceilings, not targets: the measured figures are reported. **Confirmed by the owner ("ok", 2026-11-24).** |
| 2 | **Structural switches fade and restart the part's voices:** a change of `chip.model`, `filter.topology`, `chip.clock`, `chip.resolution` or `chip.volume_dc` fades the part's sounding voices over 3 ms, resets them and continues in the new configuration; held keys do not retrigger. | A sounding chain cannot change its model, its filter's owner or its clock without a step, and a Shared filter cannot serve chips of two models. The steal fade already exists and is click-free. Vintage's key-mode change is the precedent. |
| 3 | **One latency for both models and both parts:** the longest chain's (Model A's), fixed at prepare; a shorter chain is padded. | A host is told the latency at prepare and `chip.model` can be automated; the shell requires both parts to report the same figure. Costs Model B 11 internal samples at 96 kHz. |
| 4 | **Shared filter, mode and resonance writes: switched at the nearest sample** (`ChipVoice::sharedSwitchSample`), within half a sample (0.6 on Model A's output); the band-limited blend of `ChipVoice.h` is not built. | 8.19.4 leaves the choice to S4 with the deviation recorded. Shared is not the default topology, the blend costs a second output equation per sample on the shared path, and Model B's budget has 0.3 % of room. To revisit if the owner hears it. |
| 5 | **Host-driven values reach the chip once per control interval** of about 1 ms (a whole number of internal samples on the part's own sample count), in both resolutions; ticks stay exact to the cycle. | Glide, bend and vibrato written only at ticks (50 per second) would step audibly; per sample would cost a register sync per voice per sample. 8.4.6 already lets host modulation move between ticks in Authentic ("smooth, then rounded to the register"). |
| 6 | **Key follow:** `filter.key_follow` at 100 % moves the cutoff by 16 register units per semitone around note 60 (192 per octave), added before the macro offset and clamped. | The contract gave the range and no law. Exact tracking in Hz needs the cutoff's Hz mapping, which is S6's (8.7: the cutoff is shown in register units until then), and differs per model. |
| 7 | **Vibrato:** one sine LFO per part, free-running from prepare and reset, depth = mod wheel times `mod.wheel_vibrato` (0 to 100 ct). | As Vintage's one LFO per part (3.2); 8.7 gives rate and depth and no shape. |
| 8 | **A voice leaves and enters the sum silently:** after the idle condition of 8.5 a voice renders on until its output has stayed below -120 dB re full scale for one control interval (at most 500 ms, then the 3 ms fade); a voice that starts from idle starts with its filters settled on the chip's static level. | Idle voices must cost nothing (8.5), but the filter and the board filters ring after the envelopes reach 0, and with Volume DC on a chain started from nothing would step by the whole voice DC. |
| 9 | **Model routing:** the part is built by the stronger model, not by the one the S4 row of 8.10 names. | It is real-time code around the chip chain with a cost gate that has 0.3 % of room (this repository's `orchestrate` skill routes such work there). Tests, benchmarks and patches stay with the cheaper model. |

### S4a: the implementer's readings (2026-11-24, all review)

Taken while building the part against 8.21.1, where the contract was silent or its wording
could not be met as written. Not yet checked by the independent review.

| # | Point | Reading taken (reason) |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | Latency | The reported latency includes the delay of the board low-pass (about 10 µs, measured at prepare). Without it an edge arrives 1.39 host samples late at 192 kHz without oversampling, outside the contract's own 1-sample test; every stage that adds delay belongs in the latency. (review) |
| 2 | Tempo | Read from the play head in `applyInstrumentParams` (playing, with a tempo and a position), otherwise `arp.tempo`; a held block keeps the previous tempo. No new shell hook. (review) |
| 3 | Structural switch | Voices that sounded become idle, so held keys stay silent. A note played during the fade starts after it in the new configuration; a release in the meantime is delayed by the fade length. `chip.clock` is global and switches both parts. (review) |
| 4 | Unison | A voice starting from idle gets every oscillator's accumulator set to frac(k²/2n) of a cycle right after tick 0; no random base (determinism). A busy voice that retriggers keeps its phases. (review) |
| 5 | Smoothing | Over 5 ms at the control points: oscillator fine and pulse width, cutoff, key follow, variation, the three macros, velocity to volume and to cutoff, vibrato rate, wheel depth, unison detune, bend and wheel. Not smoothed: `porta.time`, `tick.free_hz`. The note gain is computed at note start. (review) |
| 6 | Control points | `setHost` / `sync` are skipped when no host input can have moved; `sync` writes only changed values, so the output is the same. (review) |
| 7 | Retarget | The pitch is synced at the event's cycle, not at the next control point. (review) |
| 8 | Render spans | Per Note voices render spans of up to 1024 internal samples with the control states precomputed; Shared renders in lockstep segments of up to 64, split at any voice's tick. (review) |
| 9 | Quiet path | After 0.1 s of exact silence with no voice sounding, the chain after the sum is reset and not run; the decimator's tail is dropped then. (review) |
| 10 | Idle chip time | Caught up once per render call and before a note starts on the voice. `resume()` (Upper back from Whole) resets Upper's chips to power-on, because how long Upper went unrendered depends on the host's blocks. (review) |
| 11 | Quiet criterion | Voice × note gain × `kChipGain` below 1e-6 per sample for one control interval, decided at control points. In practice most voices leave by the 500 ms + 3 ms rule, because the board high-pass keeps a 133 ms tail of the envelope's step above -120 dB. (review) |
| 12 | Idle and looping lanes | Idle uses `anyWaveLaneRunning` as 8.5 words it, so a looping lane keeps a voice until the 30 s rule, although 8.16 says no lane can raise the gate after note-off. (review) |
| 13 | `kChipGain` = 0.9739 | Calibrated on the note's peak, which is its onset (the envelope's step through the board high-pass); the steady pulse then peaks near -17.9 dBFS. To be weighed in S4b's level rule. (review) |
| 14 | Settled start | Filters are primed on the static level (envelopes 0, no audio), not on the first sample. Shared: a chip joining a running shared filter is primed on that filter's fixed point for its own DC only. (review) |
| 15 | Steal | After the fade the chain is suspended (it restarts settled), then the engine's `noteOn(stolen)` runs. (review) |
| 16 | All Sound Off | Note-off and `enterIdle` (gates cleared, the idle writes), then suspend. (review) |
| 17 | Tables | A claim not committed by the next read is aborted. If a claim returns nothing while the shell's read succeeded, both parts keep their previous tables but the parameters are applied (the shell cannot fail the block from the hook). (review) |
| 18 | Shared filter writes | Cutoff, resonance and mode follow the most recently started note through a tap on the sink; a new note also pushes its current registers. Cutoff applies `filterLagSamples` later; mode and resonance at `sharedSwitchSample`. (review) |
| 19 | CPU benchmark patch | 16 notes D3 to F4 around the pitch `chip_bench` uses; oscillators 1 and 2 through the filter, as in `chip_bench`'s chain case. With A3 to C5 the figures are higher (reported: 5.63 % Model B, 11.27 % Model A). (review) |
| 20 | Tail | 3 × the nominal release of the enabled oscillators of Lower, plus Upper unless Whole. (review) |

### S4a: after the independent review (2026-11-24, all review)

The reviewer's own tests (`tests/bside/review/PartReviewTests.cpp`) found three defects the
author's tests could not see; the orchestrator's calls on them are in 8.21.1, "After the
independent review".

| # | Finding | Call (reason) |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | Shared: an idle voice carries 1/n of the other notes' filtered sound, never gets quiet, leaves by the 500 ms limit and takes that share with it (a burst of -18 dB on Model B and -12 dB on Model A re the held note). | The quiet criterion is judged on the voice's own contribution, and the shares move to their new values over the 3 ms fade when a chip joins or leaves (weights sum to 1). Row 8 of the contract's readings ("leaves and enters the sum silently") is the owner's confirmed wish; this is what it takes in Shared. (review) |
| 2 | A structural switch on Model B ends its fade with a step of about 4 % of the signal, because the latency pad is cleared while it still holds faded samples. | The fade must reach the output in full; gate: no step above 3/288 of the signal at the fade's end. (review) |
| 3 | A note played and released inside a switch's fade sounds up to 3 ms too long; a delay flag can outlive its note in Solo. | A delayed note is released as much later as it started. (review) |
| 4 | The pad's assertion fails at host rates of 16 kHz and below, and the ring has no margin at 8 and 11.025 kHz. | The ring covers every rate from 8 kHz up with margin; tested at 8, 11.025 and 16 kHz. (review) |
| 5 | Implementer's readings 1 (latency includes the board filters' delay), 2 (tempo only while the host plays and gives a position) and 3 (`chip.clock` switches both parts) differ from the contract's wording. | Accepted and written into the contract: each is the stricter or the only workable reading. (review) |
| 6 | CPU: Model B misses its gates at the orchestrator's bench (16 voices 5.15 to 5.22 % against 5 %, Dual 10.34 to 10.51 % against 10 %); Model A sits on its gates; the chain measures about 1 ns slower than on `main`. | The gates stand. The author's two cost rounds missed, so the fixes and one more cost round go to a stronger model, as the escalation rule says. If that misses too, the choice goes to the owner. (review) |

### S4a: readings of the fix round (2026-11-24, all review)

Taken while fixing the review's findings and in the cost round. A second independent review of
this round is not yet in.

| # | Point | Reading taken (reason) |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | Shared shares | Each chip has a share that ramps over the 3 ms fade; its weight is share / sum of shares, so the weights always add up to 1. A note joins at share 0 when another chip already carries the output, and at 1 when none does (so one voice is still Per Note bit for bit). The chip chain takes the weight per bus sample (`finishSharedWeighted`); the counted share computes what it did. (review) |
