| 2 | Shared, idle | An idle chip hands its share on only while another chip can take it; otherwise it keeps it and is judged on its whole output. The quiet criterion is the chip's actual output at weight 0, not a separate own-contribution signal: the board high-pass still holds the memory of the share (133 ms), so most voices still leave by the 500 ms + 3 ms rule, as in Per Note. This is short of the contract's wording ("stops costing CPU within the same times" holds; "judged on its own contribution" only in effect). (review) |
| 3 | Shared, steal | The stage weights stay at a sum of 1 through a steal, which conserves DC; the audible sum dips by at most a quarter of the stolen chip's share at mid-fade (measured with 16 voices: 0.0185 of the chord, against 0.0625 lost before). Scaling the other chips up instead produced a DC step on Model A, because a chip's board high-pass has already removed the DC of its share. (review) |
| 4 | Shared, fading chip | A fading chip's input to the shared filter fades with its output when others carry the output. (review) |
| 5 | Late notes | A Retarget of a waiting note restarts its delay count; a Retarget of a sounding late note ends its delay. A note-on and note-off in the same sample while waiting is released at once at its start. Mono modes never emit a steal, so "a steal in Solo" does not exist; the real case (a late start after a switch in Solo, then a legato return) is fixed and tested. (review) |
| 6 | Pad ring | 128 samples: twice the largest possible difference between the two chains (63). (review) |
| 7 | Cost round | One change: Per Note, the chain runs a block of bus samples in one loop when nothing can happen inside it (no write in flight or due, cutoff standing still, not the first samples after a reset or suspend), otherwise per sample as before; the same bits, shown by a direct comparison test and by every chip test passing unchanged. Shared and the float variant stay per sample. The suspected slowdown of the chain against `main` was not reproduced (measurement noise). The part's own overhead (about 0.15 % of a core) was left alone. (review) |
| 8 | Steal thump (open, for the owner's ear) | A stolen voice restarts with its envelopes at the chip's power-on value, as 8.6.4 asks under Clean and Precondition: with 16 held notes a steal differs from the no-steal render by about 0.16 within 10 ms. Not changed in S4a; to be judged in the S4b audition. (review) |

### S4a: after the second review (2026-11-24, all review)

A second reviewer checked the fix round with its own tests (`PartReview2Tests.cpp`). It found
no defect in the block path (47.8 million samples compared against the per-sample path, none
differ), the late-note timing, the switch fade or real-time safety.

| # | Finding | Call (reason) |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | All Sound Off leaves the stopped chips one internal sample ahead of the part's clock; in Shared a later note on such a chip makes the output depend on the host's block length (up to -21.6 dB). Older than the fix round. | A defect: fixed, and a Shared segment must never read bus samples it did not render, in any build type (8.21.1). (review) |
| 2 | Shared: a note of another velocity that joins or leaves changes the level of the held notes' filtered sound (5.9 dB on Model B, 7.2 dB on Model A for velocity 1 against 0.01 at `vel.volume` 100 %), smoothly over 3 ms. | Accepted as a property of the topology and written into the contract: volume stays per note (8.5), so the filtered sum is heard at the share-weighted mean of the note gains. The alternatives (dividing the share by the note gain, or scaling each note's input to the filter) either overdrive Model A's output stage with mixed velocities or break "one voice in Shared equals Per Note bit for bit". Pinned by a test; for the owner's ear in the S4b audition. (review) |
| 3 | The steal dip of the fix round's reading 3 was understated: the true bound is t(1 − t)/(n − t) of the stolen chip's weight, 0.26 with 16 carriers and 0.34 with 2; smooth, not a click. | The contract carries the true bound and the steal test asserts it. (review) |

### S4b contract: orchestrator's calls (2026-11-24, all review)

| # | Point | Reading taken (reason) |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | A level trim for the level rule | One new per-part parameter `mix.level` (-24 to +6 dB, default 0), appended before B-Side's first release. The chip's volume register cannot trim: 16 steps, and it moves Model A's DC and distortion. A part level is also what a player expects next to pan. (review) |
| 2 | Size and content of the bank | 16 programs (the contract allowed 10 to 20): Init, three leads, two basses, two table arpeggios, three percussion sounds, two Model A pads, one effect, one Dual layer, one Split. Each row of 8.10's list is covered at least once; drums are one sound per program, because kit mode is S5. (review) |
| 3 | `kChipGain` | Recalibrated once so that Init measures within 1 dB of Vintage's Init on the same phrase, in place of the onset-peak rule of S4a: two instruments of one maker should sit at the same loudness, and the onset peak said little about how loud a held note is. The owner hears the before and after in the audition. (review) |
| 4 | Goldens | Six programs through the shared render harness (one lead, one bass, one arpeggio, one percussion sound, one Model A pad, the Dual layer), regenerated once after the audition if sounds change. (review) |
| 5 | Windows CI time | S4b's default tests may add at most 20 s locally, and the step reports where the Windows test time goes, because S4a's Windows x64 job was cancelled at its 60-minute limit. A workflow change, if needed, is its own pull request. (review) |
| 6 | `mix.level` as built | A linear per-sample ramp over 5 ms, not steps at the control points; exactly unity within 0.001 dB of 0 dB so that a host round trip of the default changes no bit; the fingerprint that pins "0 dB changes nothing" is checked on macOS arm64 only (other targets' maths libraries may differ in a last bit). The parameter count is 204; the two IDs are appended to each part's block, so `upper.*` and `chip.clock` move by index, not by ID. (review) |

## B-Side S3a round 2: the review's findings (2026-11-20, all review)

What round 2 changed, what it measured and what stays open. Fitting traces (never committed, under the developer cache):
noise responses over cutoff x resonance x sustain (g1, 210 segments), with a held DC voice (g4), three noise voices (g5),
mode sums (g3), held tones and two tones (dg), static levels (p1b, p4 to p6), steps (v2). Gate traces (committed, written by
`tests/bside/tools/gen_s3a_traces.py`, never fitted on): `a_s3a_res_a`, `a_s3a_level_a`, `a_s3a_voices_a`,
`a_s3a_cutoff_a` (each with a bypass twin), `a_s3a_modes_a` (+ twin), `a_s3a_dist_a`, `a_s3a_steps_a`, `a_s3a_steps_b`.

| # | Point | Reading taken (reason; measured figures) |
|---|---|---|
| R1 | **Model A's cutoff follows the DC routed into the filter** (finding 2). | Measured on g4 (noise at sustain 8 plus held DC voices, 3 voices routed): FC 600 resonance 0: one voice 3191 Hz; three voices 2006 Hz; plus a held pulse at sustain 8 / 15: 1477 / 1205 Hz; a held triangle low (negative DC): 2157 Hz. FC 1024: 9087 -> 7165 -> 5630 Hz. ln fc falls about 0.7 per render unit of DC at FC 600 and 0.35 at FC 1024, as a triode law does; at FC 200 the DC raises it slightly (341 -> 382 Hz). Q falls with the DC too (FC 600: 0.41 -> 0.27). **Not modelled as built** (row R2). **(review)** |
| R2 | **Level-dependent integrators: four structures tried, none within tolerance; the filter keeps round 1's dynamics.** | (a) ln fc as a function of the instantaneous low-pass level (per converter value): 1.0 dB rms on g1, extrapolates badly to three voices (h2: corners up to +134 %). (b) A transistor law per integrator driven by its own output (6 parameters fit the nominal cutoff curve to 2.8 % rms): 2.3 dB rms after 3 iterations, not pursued. (c) The same law driven by followed DC and swing: 3.8 dB rms. (d) Per-converter-value tables driven by followed DC, swing and band-pass swing, with level-dependent damping and (later) per-cutoff resonance scaling, fitted with corner and peak residuals: 0.79 dB rms on the fitting data but still corners -23 % (FC 600 res 0) and peaks 2 to 3.7 dB low at resonance 15 on its own fitting data, and the committed `a_filter_a` corners then failed (up to 25 %). Round 1's dynamics (static tables, no level dependence) pass every committed `a_*` gate, so they stay; **findings 1, 2 and 4 stay open** (gate figures: report). The structure the data point to: a gate-voltage shift of both integrators by the routed DC (triode law), a convex AC term at low cutoffs, and a resonance law whose corner shift depends on the cutoff (+170 % at FC 300, +45 % at 600, +28 % at 1024 from resonance 0 to 15). **(review)** |
| R3 | **Model A's held-tone distortion is per voice, bent before the envelope** (finding 3). | The second harmonic of one triangle through the low-pass at 2047 is -27.4 to -28.4 dB at sustain 4, 7, 10, 15 (independent of the level), and on the direct path -28.7 dB at sustain 4 falling to -34.2 at 15; two triangles (160, 211 Hz) give f2 - f1 13 dB below 2 f1 on the low-pass and 4 dB *above* it on Model B (Model B: one static curve of the sum). So Model A bends each voice's waveform before its envelope: v = e f (u / e), f (w) = w - 0.1751 w^2 - 0.0978 w^3 (`OutputStage::shapeVoice`), then the sum-level curves (filter functions, mixer). **Interface consequence (S3b):** the renderer applies `shapeVoice` per voice before summing into its bus (u and e are per-voice values it has: about 4 multiplications and a division per voice and sample); a bus-level model cannot do it: a function of the bus sum produces f2 - f1 4 dB above 2 f1 (Model B shows exactly that) where Model A's reference shows it 13 dB below. Fitted (g, dg; rms 0.83 dB over 28 spectra, largest 3.4 dB). Gated on `a_s3a_dist_a`: harmonics through the low-pass at 2047 and 1024 and direct within 2.2 dB. Reported: the band-pass at 1500 (h2 7.3 dB, h4 9.5 dB off: the band-pass functions were not refitted) and the two-tone products on the low-pass (up to 4.5 dB). **(review)** |
| R4 | **Output stage refitted with the voice bend** (Model A). | M 0.3880, voice DC 0.3865, envelope DC 0.4031, scale 0.1797, N = x + 0.0745 x^2 + 0.0495 x^3 + 0.0015 x^4 - 0.0032 x^5 (limited at -3.30, 4.08), gains gv 0.1318, gl 0.0495, gb 0.0722, gh 0.0788; filter output functions low (-0.5986, -0.1274, -0.0395, 0.0434, -0.0063) x 1.10 (row 8), band leak (0.1574, -0.0601, 0.0215), high leak (0.0141, -0.0122). Static rms 0.0073 reference units, largest 0.039. |
| R5 | **Volume DC off** (finding 8). | Row 15 (revised). Reference with volume DC on (`a_s3a_steps_*`, gated on the volume writes of Model A: within 0.5 dB): routing writes with silent voices step the output (Model A: 1 -> 3: -0.150, 3 -> 7: -0.178, 7 -> 0: +0.460, 0 -> 4: -0.129 reference units at volume 15); ours follow within 2 dB on the first frame (reported, row R9). |
| R6 | **Resonance write DC step** (finding 9). | Reference (Model A, a held DC voice routed, low-pass): 0 -> 15: -0.0440, 0 -> 8: -0.0191 reference units (-27.1 / -34.4 dBFS). Ours on `a_s3a_steps_a` (first frame): -31.2 / -38.9 dBFS against -29.6 / -38.3: now 1.6 dB smaller instead of larger (the low-pass gain table still acts on the DC). Reported, not gated; **open**. **(review)** |
| R7 | **Shared topology** (finding 7; `OutputStage.h` "SHARED"). | One filter per part; its input is one chip's static filter-bus DC plus every chip's `filterBusAc`; each chip's stage gets the shared output / n and the filter's static output / n. With n = 1 it is Per Note. Tested for 1 to 16 identical chips: Model B within 0.5 % of n Per Note chips (its mixer curve is nearly linear), Model A finite, never silent, growing less than n times (summed distortion). Not compared with the reference (one chip has three voices; three voices in one chip are `a_s3a_voices_a` and the distortion gates). **(review)** |
| R8 | **Cost** (finding 6). | The input guard moved out of the per-sample dependency chain (the input is checked; the states can then never become non-finite); only the joint state flush remains; Model B's mixer curve skips its zero terms; the board filters run in their state-space form; with volume DC off no extra filter (the volume follows the high-pass). `chip_bench` (load average 9 at the time, indicative): `s3aB` 4.9 ns at 96 kHz (budget 7), `s3aA` 6.9; a cutoff every sample (`Filter::processWithCutoff`, cubic curve and a rational tan in line): `cutB` 8.4 ns (3.5 extra; limit 5), `cutA` 23.2 ns (Model A evaluates its converter and curve). |
| R9 | **Per-voice test harness.** | `AnalogChain.h` renders each voice alone when no sync or ring bit is written (voice k = a chip given only voice k's writes minus two thirds of a chip given none), so both buses are exact and `a_routing_*` is compared: Model B within the scripted tolerance (3.15 % / 0.58 dB), Model A outside (30 % outliers, 3.6 dB mean: cutoff 300 with several voices routed, rows R1, R2). |
| R10 | **Committed gate data used for constants.** | Two constants were set against committed gates: row 14 (round 1) and row 8 (the 1.10 low-pass scale, round 2). Everything else in round 2 was fitted on the fitting traces only. **(review)** |
| R11 | **Internal-rate dependence** (finding 11). | Measured (python, the trapezoidal SVF against its analog prototype, low-pass where the response is above -12 dB, up to 16 kHz): up to 2.0 dB at 88.2 kHz (0.35 dB at 192 kHz); the best correction of g and k per (w, Q) still leaves up to 1.2 dB (w about 0.6, Q 4). Meeting 0.5 dB needs the filter run at twice the rate below 150 kHz (Model B's cost would then exceed 7 ns). **Not done; the gate was not added.** **(review)** |
| R12 | **Findings that stay open** (numbers on the committed gates, all printed by `bside_trace`, none gated). | Corners and peaks: `a_s3a_res_a` worst corner 45.8 %, peak 1.3 dB; `a_s3a_level_a` 74.7 %, 1.5 dB; `a_s3a_voices_a` and `a_s3a_cutoff_a` likewise outside (see the test output). Modes at cutoff 200 and 1500: 6.9 % outliers, 1.12 dB mean (allowed 5 %, 1 dB). Routing on Model A: 30 %. Resonance-write DC: up to 1.8 dB. Band-pass and two-tone distortion: up to 9.5 and 4.5 dB. Internal-rate dependence (row R11). |

## B-Side S3a: filter and output stage (2026-11-19, all review)

Contract: `docs/architecture.md` 8.19.1 (with 8.4.4, 8.9.4, 8.15.7). Code: `source/bside/chip/Filter.*`,
`source/bside/chip/OutputStage.*` (headers state the model). Tests: `tests/bside/unit/FilterOutputTests.cpp`,
`tests/bside/trace/AnalogChain.h` (our chain), `AnalogGates.h`, `ReferenceDataTests.cpp` ("analog tolerances
against our chip"), `AnalogReferenceTests.cpp`. Every constant is our own fit to data generated with the built
adapter on traces written for this step (outputs under the developer cache, never in the repository); the
emulator's source and tables were not opened, and no constant was looked up anywhere. Units: "render units"
are those of `Chip::render` (one voice at full envelope swings +-0.5); "reference units" are the reference's
output convention (8.15.7, 1.0 = its 16-bit full scale).

| # | Point | Reading taken (reason; measured figures) |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | **How the data was taken.** | Static levels: voices held by the test bit (pulse high or triangle low) at every envelope level, gated on and off alternately, every routing and mode, both models (p1, p4 to p6: 310 levels per model), read through an inverse of the board high-pass from the adapter's debug output; frequency responses: noise (oscillator 3, frequency 0xffff, sustain 8, as the committed traces) through the filter and through its bypass twin, complex transfer by cross-spectrum per segment, a two-pole low-pass fitted per setting (cutoffs every 32 and around every bit boundary, resonances at three cutoffs); corners as the gate finds them: 48-frame segments, the adapter's `.bsspec` and `AnalogMeasure` itself (73 cutoffs, Model A); distortion: triangles 40 Hz, 1 to 3 voices in phase, through the low-pass at 2047 and direct. **(review)** |
| 2 | **The mixer is a static curve after a gain that falls with the inputs connected** (Model A: about 13 % per direct voice, 5 to 8 % per selected filter output; Model B 0.4 %). Superseded in round 2: see "Round 2" row R4. | Model A's DC steps are not additive in the voices (removing 1, 2, 3 voices: 0.063, 0.138, 0.231) and three pulses compress (0.65 against 0.78 for three times one). Model B: a mild cubic (x + 0.044 x^2 + 0.002 x^3), clean intermodulation at the expected +4 dB over the second harmonic. Model A: a quintic `N` limited at its turning points (-3.30, 4.08 render units), `G = 1 / (1 + gv (direct - 3) + gl [LP] + gb [BP] + gh [HP])`. Static fit as built (round 2, one truth with `OutputStage.cpp`): Model A rms 0.0073 reference units (levels up to 0.65), largest 0.039; Model B rms 0.0004, largest 0.002. **(review)** |
| 3 | **Voice DC** (8.4.4). | Every voice adds `kVoiceDc` wherever it is routed and `kEnvDc` x its envelope converter value: Model A 0.331 and 0.377 render units (its waveforms sit above their zero: a pulse at full envelope is +0.88, a triangle at its bottom -0.12 around the voice's rest level), Model B -0.068 and 0 (centred). Mixer DC 0.379 (A) / -0.065 (B). Oscillator 3 off removes voice 3 from the direct path only (measured: the same step as routing it to a filter with no mode bit). The reference's output at volume 0 is a constant `Y0` (-0.388 A, -0.192 B; visible only as the power-on step). **(review)** |
| 4 | **Volume** (4 bits). | Model A: a fitted compressive table (vol 1 = 0.078, 4 = 0.302, 8 = 0.577, 15 = 1; the ratio does not depend on the operating point within 0.56 to 0.58 at vol 8); Model B: k / 15 within 0.004. Applied after the mixer curve (the vol 8 / 15 ratio is the same for DC levels from -0.14 to +0.35). |
| 5 | **Where the second harmonic on the triangle lives** (decisions S2b-2 row 5: -34 dB A, -40 dB B). | Model B: in the mixer (two triangles at 110 and 170 Hz give intermodulation at -35.7 dB, the second harmonic at -39.9 dB: one static curve of the sum). Model A: **not a static curve of the sum**: the second harmonic of one voice rises with the DC the other voices add (-34.2 dB alone, -27.5 / -22.9 dB with a second voice held at half / full level, -33.6 dB with it at its low end) but intermodulation of two moving voices is only -50 dB, which no static function of the sum can do (it would put intermodulation 4 dB above the harmonic). Modelled here as the static mixer curve (it meets the filtered-path gates); **not reproduced:** the direct-path second harmonic with two voices in phase (ours -45.6 dB, reference -34.0 dB) and the low intermodulation. A per-voice element whose curvature follows the mixer's operating point would be needed; it would live in the renderer (per voice) in S3b. **(review)** |
| 6 | **Filter signs and topology.** | Both models: the low-pass and high-pass outputs are inverted against the direct path, the band-pass is not (complex transfer phases 180 / -90 / +90 degrees at the corner), so low + high is a notch. Model B is an ideal state-variable filter within 3 % (outputs -0.997 lp, 1.011 bp, -1.025 hp; leaks of 0.05 and less are the reference's per-cycle discretisation and are not modelled). Model A's outputs are mixtures: low-pass about -0.74 lp (small signal), band-pass 0.13 lp + 0.72 bp (FC 1024), high-pass -0.02 lp - 0.21 bp - 0.55 hp, the band-pass and the high-pass's band term scaling with 1 / Q0. |
| 7 | **Model A distortion** (8.4.4 "non-linear integrators"). **Rewritten in round 2.** | Round 1 said the distortion is static and the cutoff does not move with the signal; the first half stands for a held tone (harmonics equal at 40, 160 and 640 Hz, cutoff 2047 and 1200, within 0.4 dB), **the second is refuted**: the reference's Model A cutoff and Q move strongly with the DC routed into the filter and with the noise level (round 2 rows R1, R2), which our filter does not model. The held-tone distortion is now a per-voice bend before the envelope (row R3) plus static functions of the low-pass state on the outputs (a quintic on the low-pass output, a cubic leak on the band-pass, a quadratic on the high-pass), fitted jointly with the static levels and the held-tone spectra. **(review)** |
| 8 | **Small-signal gain against static slope** (Model A). | Round 2: the low-pass output function is scaled by 1.10 after the joint fit (row R4) to meet the noise level of `a_modes_a` (level 0.80 -> 0.29 dB); this is a second use of committed gate data for a constant (row R10). **(review)** |
| 9 | **Model A cutoff curve.** | An 11-bit terminated ladder of ratio 2.1756 (fitted; the Model A waveform converter's own 2.2 fits nearly as well) and ln fc piecewise linear in its value over 24 points, fitted to the -3 dB points `AnalogMeasure` finds on 73 cutoffs of our own 48-frame trace, through the -3 dB / fc ratio of the chosen Q law. The reference's low end is not monotonic (FC 192: 355 Hz, 256: 331 Hz, 320: 340 Hz): fitted rms 3 %, largest 10.8 % (FC 320). A two-pole fit per setting puts Q as low as 0.37 between 300 and 1 500 Hz and misses the -3 dB point by up to 37 %; the reference's response there has a sharp knee and a shallow slope that no two-pole matches, so the -3 dB point was preferred (the gate's quantity). **(review)** |
| 10 | **Model A Q at resonance 0** against the cutoff. | 0.79 at 250 Hz, 0.62 at 500, 0.55 at 1 to 2 kHz, 0.65 at 4 kHz, 0.745 at 8 kHz, 0.77 at 21 kHz (piecewise linear in log fc); a judgement call between the two-pole fits (0.37 to 0.80 in the knee region) and the corner gate. **(review)** |
| 11 | **Resonance** (16 steps). | 1 / Q tables fitted at three cutoffs per model (Model B 1.41 -> 0.39, about Q = 0.71 x 2^(r/8); Model A Q factor 1 -> 2.90). The reference's corner falls with resonance (Model A to 0.87 x, Model B to 0.986 x at 15) and Model A's low-pass gain rises (1.20 x at 15): both tabled. The band-pass gain's resonance factor (1 + 0.42 (low-pass gain - 1)) comes from one point (FC 612, resonance 12). **(review)** |
| 12 | **Model B cutoff curve.** | fc = 5.2509e-9 FC^3 + 9.5264e-5 FC^2 + 6.91632 FC - 1.14 Hz (FC 64 .. 2047, largest error 0.22 %), at least 15 Hz: FC 0 lies below what the responses resolve (a judgement call; decisions "One analog estimator" puts it near 25 Hz). |
| 13 | **`variation`** (Model A). | Shifts the converter value d by (variation - 0.5) x 2 x 0.06 (about 120 register units at 0 or 1; 512 moves from 2 kHz to about 3.3 kHz / 1.2 kHz). The reference data has one curve (variation 0.5); the size of the shift is a judgement call. **(review)** |
| 14 | **Model A band-pass and high-pass gains, tuned on the gate data.** | Unchanged in round 2 (0.59, -0.59, -0.20): the fit on our own mode traces (`fitmodes`, cutoff 200, 600, 1500) was not completed (round 2 row R10); the review's point (no hand-tuning on gate data) stays open. **(review)** |
| 15 | **volumeDc off** (8.4.4). **Revised in round 2.** | The static DC (mixer DC, `kVoiceDc` on both paths through the filter's static output, `Y0`) is subtracted at the curve's operating point (no step at a routing write), and the volume moves behind the board high-pass, which removes the rest (the envelopes' DC and what the curves make of the audio): a volume write with held voices changes the output's DC by less than -60 dBFS once the high-pass has settled, on both paths and both models (tested; round 1's -19.6 / -22.0 dBFS on Model A are gone). Routing writes with silent voices: below -60 dBFS too (tested). **(review)** |
| 16 | **Board filters** (8.15.7). | One-pole high-pass 1.196 Hz and trapezoidal one-pole low-pass 15.4 kHz at the internal rate, fixed in Hz. **Round 2:** the high-pass confirmed by our own step responses through the adapter (volume 0 <-> 15 with silent voices, decay fitted over 10 to 150 ms): 1.08 to 1.21 Hz (A), 1.10 to 1.23 Hz (B), mean 1.16 Hz, the spread from the reference's dead band; 1.196 kept. The dead band is not reproduced. |
| 17 | **Numerics.** | The filter runs in double precision as the state-space form of the trapezoidal state-variable filter (the same arithmetic, a shorter dependency chain: 8.8 -> 6.5 ns for the Model B chain at 96 kHz); the states are flushed to 0 together below 1e-15 (flushing one alone froze the other at about 5e-13: it decays only through the first); round 2 removed round 1's separate flush of each state below 1e-30 (it never acted: the joint flush comes first) and its test. The cutoff is held below 0.45 x the internal rate. With a constant input at high resonance the output keeps a rounding residue below 1e-15 (-300 dB). |
| 18 | **What the single-sum harness can and cannot render** (until S3b). | `AnalogChain.h` sends the sum to the path of the voices that sound (envelope counter above 0); every voice's DC is exact per voice. Ambiguous samples (voices sounding on both paths) are counted and exclude a segment or frame. Skipped: `a_routing_a`, `a_routing_b` (8 of 10 segments each); the first 2 frames of `a_modes_*` (the power-on envelopes of voices 1 and 2 releasing on the direct path). **Round 2:** a trace without sync or ring writes is now rendered per voice (row R9), so `a_routing_*` are compared; the single sum remains the fallback for the others. |
| 19 | **Voice DC gate on Model B: reported, not gated.** | 8.9.4's row is Model A's. Model B's steps (-27 to -42 dBFS) lie within 13 dB of the reference's high-pass residual (-46.4 dBFS, 8.15.6), so the first frame after a write differs by up to 3.6 dB (frames 121, 241, 321, 401 of `a_voice_dc_b`: +1.5, -2.7, +1.8, +3.6 dB; the others within 0.3 dB). The write `18 10` in both traces changes only the mode (volume 0 before and after) and is not a volume step. |
| 20 | **Distortion gate scope.** | Gated: Model A `a_distortion_a` s00..s02 (drive 1, 2, 3 voices), harmonics 2..5 against the fundamental from the segment spectra (`toneLevelDb`), +-3 dB. Reported: s03 (three voices at sustain 8) and Model B (all within 2.2 dB). |

## CI: the validation tool's teardown crash (2026-11-23, review)

Code: `scripts/linux-check/pluginval-run.sh`, its self-test and samples, the Linux steps of `.github/workflows/build.yml`. Trap: `docs/engineering-notes.md`.

| # | Point | Reading taken (reason) |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | **A SIGSEGV of pluginval 1.0.4 on Linux x86_64 is accepted only on the exact signature:** no failure marker in the output, every started test completed and the last completed is "Fuzz parameters", backtrace frame #0 the `IEventHandler` map `operator[]` and frame #1 `RunLoop::Impl::registerEventHandler`, no frame mentioning `Exitium`. Any other signal, a crash during a test, another backtrace or a missing gdb fails the run. | The crash is a known use-after-free in the JUCE 8.0.3 inside pluginval (fixed upstream in JUCE 04e167d646) that happens after all tests have run and after our plugin is unloaded; without this, most CI runs fail by chance (two plugins, eight environments). The narrow signature keeps a real crash of our plugin, or an incomplete run, a failure. The final test name is hard-coded on purpose: a new pluginval that adds a later test makes the check stricter, not looser. **(review)** |
| 2 | **Alternative considered, not taken: build pluginval from pinned source with the fixed JUCE.** | It needs a new build job and an unpinned dependency in its own build (pluginval's submodules, toolchain), a supply-chain and maintenance decision left to the owner. The wrapper is temporary and says when to delete it (a pluginval Linux build with JUCE 9.0.2 or newer that runs on glibc 2.35). **(review)** |
| 3 | **The negative distribution check (glibc 2.31) runs pluginval directly, not through the wrapper.** | It asserts a loader refusal; no gdb is installed in that container and the wrapper could only accept a crash after a full run. **(review)** |

## M8 packaging of the first release (2026-11-19, all review)

Contract: `docs/architecture.md` section 10. Code: `scripts/package-release.sh`, `resources/release/`, the release part of `.github/workflows/build.yml`, the `mac` step of `scripts/preflight.sh`.

| # | Point | Reading taken (reason) |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | **The macOS bundle is signed again (ad hoc) as the last build step** (`CMakeLists.txt`, `vs_configure_plugin`). | Found while building the packer: on a plain Ninja build `codesign --verify --deep --strict` fails with "a sealed resource is missing or invalid", because JUCE writes `Contents/Resources/moduleinfo.json` after the linker's ad-hoc signature, so the seal does not cover it. The contract's `verify` (which runs that command) would fail on every bundle, and a downloaded bundle would carry a broken seal. Signing again with identity `-` (no secret, no identity) after JUCE's own post-build step gives a valid seal; pluginval and the validator then see the same bundle that is packed. **(review)** |
| 2 | **All three zips are packed on one macOS runner** (job `pack`), not the Windows zip in Git Bash. | The macOS zip needs `ditto`, `lipo` and `codesign`; the Windows and Linux zips need only Info-ZIP `zip`, which macOS and Ubuntu runners have and Git Bash on Windows does not. One job, one script, one tool set. The script itself stays portable (bash 3.2, Git Bash) for the checks. **(review)** |
| 3 | **Job split: `pack` (zips, verify, self-test, sums), `zip-smoke` (host_smoke on the unpacked bundle on macOS, Windows x64, Linux x86_64), `package` (draft release, the only job with `contents: write`).** | The contract names one `package` job that packs and releases; the draft must be made only after the zips have been loaded in a host, and only the writing job may hold write access, so the zips are made in a read-only job. The draft is replaced by deleting a draft of the same tag and creating a new one (a draft has no tag; `gh release upload` finds drafts only by listing); a published release of the tag stops the run in `plan` and again in `package`. **(review)** |
| 4 | **The host_smoke program is uploaded by the three release rows** (`host-smoke-<row>`, release runs only). | `zip-smoke` needs it and a fresh runner would otherwise rebuild JUCE. |
| 5 | **macOS zip: `ditto -c -k --norsrc --noextattr --noqtn --keepParent`.** | A plain `ditto -c -k` adds a `__MACOSX/._*` entry for every extended attribute; `--norsrc` and `--noextattr` stop that, the ad-hoc signature lives in the Mach-O file and `_CodeSignature/CodeResources`, not in attributes, so it survives. `verify` proves it (zip listing, no metadata entries, then `codesign --verify --deep --strict` on the unpacked bundle). |
| 6 | **Minimum Windows is 10.** | JUCE 9.0.5 defines `WINVER` and `_WIN32_WINNT` as Windows 10 (`juce_core/native/juce_BasicNativeHeaders.h`); the build sets nothing lower. Windows on ARM needs Windows 11 for the x64 emulation, which the ARM64 build does not use; the text says "Windows 10 or newer". Not tried on any Windows here. **(review)** |
| 7 | **VST3 folders in INSTALL.txt:** macOS `~/Library/Audio/Plug-Ins/VST3` and `/Library/Audio/Plug-Ins/VST3`; Windows `%LOCALAPPDATA%\Programs\Common\VST3` and `C:\Program Files\Common Files\VST3`; Linux `~/.vst3`, `/usr/local/lib/vst3`, `/usr/lib/vst3`. | Read from the code of the JUCE host (`juce_VST3PluginFormatHeadless.cpp`, `getDefaultLocationsToSearch`) and of the VST3 SDK's hosting module (`module_linux.cpp`, `module_win32.cpp`, `FOLDERID_UserProgramFilesCommon`/`FOLDERID_ProgramFilesCommon`) in the pinned submodule. |
| 8 | **The macOS and Windows "unsigned" steps are the standard ones:** `xattr -dr com.apple.quarantine <bundle>` (and Privacy & Security > Open Anyway); Properties > Unblock before unpacking, or `Get-ChildItem -Recurse \| Unblock-File`. | Not tried with a real downloaded zip: no signed-download chain exists here. The owner gate (10.4) is where these texts are tested; they are the first thing to correct if a step fails. **(review)** |
| 9 | **THIRD-PARTY.txt lists, with full licence texts, the code vendored in JUCE that the linked modules compile** (zlib, HarfBuzz, SheenBidi, LunaSVG, PlutoVG, libpng, jpeglib, libwebp, FLAC, libogg, libvorbis, Opus, opusfile, libopusenc, the VST3 SDK), plus JUCE itself and Inter. | The object files of a real Vintage build show these modules' vendored sources; the linker removes what is not called (no FLAC symbol remains in the binary), so the text says a binary may hold only part of the list. Versions and licence names come from `JUCE.spdx.json` of the pinned tag (it says libogg 1.3.6, `JUCE_UPSTREAM.txt` in the tree says 1.3.4). The jpeglib licence requires a documentation notice for binary-only distribution ("based in part on the work of the Independent JPEG Group"): included. Regenerate the file when JUCE is upgraded. **(review)** |
| 10 | **B-Side is recognised by identity, not by a 4-character search in binaries.** `verify` and `pack` compare the class IDs of `moduleinfo.json` exactly with `resources/release/IDENTITY.txt` (Vintage's two; a B-Side build has other ones ending `42737931`) and, on macOS, the bundle identifier of `Info.plist`; the two long identifiers (`com.exitium.bside`, `Exitium B-Side`) are searched in every byte of every file, NUL-stripped (so UTF-16) and case-insensitive, and with `Bsy1` also in file names and the four texts. | In an 18 MB binary the code `Bsy1` matches random bytes case-insensitively about 3 % of the time (8 / 2^32 per position, 1.8e7 positions), so it would fail a correct build by chance; the long identifiers have about 1e-23 and 7e-30 expected random matches. Measured: 0 matches of all three in the real Vintage binary, and 4 / 2 / 2 in the real B-Side binary. `docs-check.sh` checks that `IDENTITY.txt` still agrees with the codes and bundle id in `CMakeLists.txt`, and the `mac` step of the local gate (and the CI `pack` job) packs the freshly built bundle, which compares it with the file. **(review)** |
| 11 | **Release runs share one concurrency group, `release`; normal runs keep `<workflow>-<ref>`** (`cancel-in-progress: ${{ !inputs.release }}`). | The setting is read from the NEW run: with one group per ref and `true`, a later normal dispatch on the same ref cancelled a running release, and two releases on different refs ran at once and both made a draft. Now a release run only ever waits for another release run (cancel is false for it), and a normal run is in a different group, so it can neither cancel nor be cancelled by a release. A pending release is replaced by a newer pending one (GitHub keeps one pending run per group). **(review)** |
| 12 | **The version is read from `CMakeLists.txt` by `sed`; the CHANGELOG check takes the lines between `## [<version>]` and the next `## ` and needs some text** (`package-release.sh changelog`, tested on fixtures by `selftest`). | One place for the number; the same function gives the draft's text. |
| 13 | **Release-run guards in `plan` and `package`:** the tag `v<version>` must be absent or point at the built commit (`package-release.sh tag-check`, also through an annotated tag; any API error but 404 stops); the repository must be public before a draft is made (`repo-public`); a draft is re-read right before it is deleted; all checkouts use `persist-credentials: false`. | The draft is created for `$GITHUB_SHA` and `SOURCE.txt` promises a reachable source. A private repository stops the release by design until the owner makes it public. **(review)** |
| 14 | **`verify` is strict:** no symlink entries at all (the real bundle has none), no duplicate or case-colliding entries, an exact file list per platform (Windows also allows JUCE's `desktop.ini` and `Plugin.ico`), exactly the macOS slices arm64 and x86_64, modes recorded in the zip (755 folders and binaries, 644 files), INSTALL.txt of the right platform, minimum content of every text, LICENSE.txt equal to the repository's (`--license`; CI and the local gate pass it), all `Version` values of `moduleinfo.json` equal, a size limit (200 MB) before unpacking, and the B-Side scan runs on NUL-stripped bytes (so UTF-16) in any letter case, with an unreadable or unsearchable file counted as a failure. `pack` sets the modes itself (`upload-artifact` drops them). | Found by an independent review with hostile zips; each has a case in `selftest`. The Windows list assumes JUCE's folder icon files (`extras/Build/CMake/JUCEUtils.cmake`, `desktop.ini` and `Plugin.ico`); the first real Windows artifact may show another extra file, which `verify` will name. **(review)** |
| 15 | **No separate full-source archive in the first release:** the source is the tagged repository plus the pinned upstream submodules; `SOURCE.txt` lists their URLs and commits (read from the checkout at pack time) and gives the clone, checkout and `git submodule update --init --recursive` commands, and says that GitHub's automatic source archives lack the submodules. | Owner's decision for now (coordinator, 2026-11-19). If a self-contained archive is wanted later, it is one more asset. **(review)** |
| 16 | **Install texts say "x64" and "ARM64", not hardware vendors; macOS keeps "Apple silicon and Intel".** | The vendor names of CPUs are not our brand rule's concern, but architecture terms are enough on Windows and Linux; on macOS "Apple silicon" is the operating system vendor's own name for its architecture. **(review)** |
| 17 | **The release text starts with a fixed header** (`resources/release/RELEASE-NOTES-HEADER.md`): unsigned, no installers, Windows and Linux ARM64 experimental, source named in SOURCE.txt; then the CHANGELOG section. | The experimental status must be visible where users download. |
| 18 | **`verify` checks the whole entry list (folders included) against the expected one of the platform, before unpacking, and refuses names with a non-ASCII or control byte, a backslash, `//`, a `.` or `..` component or a component ending in a dot or space; every entry must come from a Unix host and be parsed (the number of parsed mode lines must equal the number of names); the real stream size is counted (stops at limit + 1); a failing `unzip -tq` stops it; the script runs in the C locale.** | Second independent review. An entry the mode parser dropped bypassed the symlink and mode checks; path aliases (`./`, `//`), normalisation variants and empty folders passed the older checks. The header-lie case is already stopped by `unzip -tq` (size mismatch), so the stream count is a second line of defence. **(review)** |
| 19 | **The install texts name what each ARM64 build really went through** (Windows ARM64: built, unit-tested, loaded by the host smoke test; no pluginval, no VST3 validator. Linux ARM64: also the VST3 validator; no pluginval; plan rows of `build.yml`), and say "not signed with a developer certificate / not notarised"; headings are underlined by `@UL@` to their real length. | Accuracy; the macOS bundle does carry an ad-hoc signature. |

## Pre-public audit (2026-11-19, owner decisions)

An independent audit read the whole history (436 commits, every branch and pull-request ref, the
pull-request texts, the Actions logs that still existed) before the repository becomes public.
No secret or credential was found. The owner decided:

- **Commit author e-mail stays as it is** (confirmed again; no history rewrite).
- **Two history-only findings are accepted, no history rewrite:** a filter integrated-circuit part
  number in one code comment of the first engine commit (reworded the same day; the name is on the
  hashed deny-list now), and a session link in the message of the first merge commit on `main`
  (it opens only for the account owner).
- **The wireframe picture of the Browser page is removed** (`docs/ui/wireframes/07-browser.png`
  showed two invented program names, one of them a nickname of a third-party instrument). The
  real page is shown in `docs/screenshots.md`. The name check reads text only, never pictures:
  look at every picture before it is committed.
- **B-Side material is published as it is.** The chip can be identified from technical facts
  (clock rates, register map, the pinned commit of the reference emulator), which an emulation
  cannot hide; the hashed deny-list can be guessed back and is a guard against slips, not a
  secret. The two reference-data manifests state provenance and licence. Open for B-Side's own
  release: check the reference emulator's licence against how its outputs and source were used.
- **The 71 build artifacts of 2026-11-12 were deleted** before the repository becomes public.
- **Fixed at the tip:** a tracked Python bytecode file that embedded a home path (removed; the
  local gate now refuses bytecode and home paths), a product name in a test comment.
- **Screenshots of the editor** live in `docs/images/` (owner's own, 2026-11-19); the README
  shows the Sound page and links to `docs/screenshots.md`.

## M8 release hardening: typed parameter text (2026-11-19, review)

- **Typed text never gives a non-finite value; text that names NaN or infinity gives the parameter's default (review).**
  Before, `juce::String::getFloatValue` turned "nan" into NaN, and `juce::jlimit` passes NaN through, so 70 Vintage
  float parameters (every `makeFloat` one plus `master.volume`) returned NaN from `getValueForText`. Now text
  containing "nan" or "inf" (any case, any position: "-nan", "nan(0x1)", "Infinity") and any result that is not
  finite ("1e999", a huge digit string) gives the default, then finite values clamp to the range as before.
  Same choice as B-Side's parsers (`finiteOr`, "no usable value gives the default"): JUCE hands a parser only the
  text, so "keep the current value" is not available. Consequence: "inf" and "1e999" no longer clamp to the maximum
  (they used to for most parameters). Display text and ordinary input are unchanged. Shared helpers:
  `textIsNotFinite`, `finiteFloatFromText` (`ParamFactory.h`). Choice and integer parameters were checked and are
  already safe (JUCE's choice parser and `getIntValue` cannot leave the range); B-Side was not changed.

## B-Side alias follow-up step (2026-11-19, all review)

Contract: `docs/architecture.md` 8.20 (defects 1 and 2 of 8.18.1; owner decision 2026-11-18: fix, no exemption).
Code: `source/bside/chip/RenderTables.h` (`render::OutputRounding`), `source/bside/chip/ChipRender.cpp` (its use, the event values in `renderSweep`). Gates:
`tests/bside/unit/ChipRenderExactTests.cpp`. "Before" is the renderer of `bside/contracts-s3a-alias` (the render
cost step), measured with the same harness in a build of that branch.

| # | Point | Reading taken (reason) |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Defect 1, the mechanism: the output's float rounding, not the rendering.** | `render` sums the oscillators in double and wrote `static_cast<float>` of the sum. A width-4095 (or width-1) pulse sits at one converter rail, about 0.5 off zero, where a float step is 2^-25, while its fundamental is only about -66 dB re 1. At f = rate x p / q the rendered signal repeats every q samples, so its rounding errors repeat too and gather on the lines at multiples of rate / q (a dozen or so in the band) instead of spreading over the spectrum. **Isolating measurement** (the exact measurement of 8.20: rectangular DFT over whole periods, the double sum captured before the conversion by a scratch hook): 88200 x 3 / 73 Hz, width 4095: float -97.34 dB, the same signal in double -119.28 dB, rounded to float after removing its mean -119.25 dB; 88200 x 3 / 71 Hz, width 1: -98.67 / -120.95 dB; 96000 x 2 / 59 Hz, width 4095: -99.96 / -119.31 dB. Over 1 760 rows (all seven internal rates, widths 1, 2, 4094, 4095, 1.5 to 3.85 kHz, q up to 100, 140 at 352.8 and 384 kHz) the double sum's worst is -115.6 dB: the kernels and the line simulation were never short. **(review)** |
| 2 | **Defect 1, the fix: the conversion's rounding error is noise-shaped** (`render::OutputRounding`, `RenderTables.h`; `renderRound` in the first commit). | The error is fed back through a fixed FIR, so the error reaching the output is shaped by NTF (z) = (1 - 1.75 z^-1 + z^-2) (1 - 0.5 z^-1 + z^-2): two zero pairs on the unit circle (0.505 and 1.318 rad), coefficients exact in binary, at most -8.16 dB from 0 to 20 kHz at 88.2 and 96 kHz and -8.52 dB in the bands of the other internal rates (the band is widest at 88.2 kHz, 0 .. 1.425 rad), up to +19.4 dB above it, where S4's decimator removes it. An FIR in the error cannot become unstable: the output stays within 4.75 float steps of the double sum. **Alternatives measured** on the 1 760 rows (float worst before -97.3 dB): two pairs at 0.5 and 1.3 rad (next to the exact-coefficient pairs taken) -114.8 dB; the same with a zero at DC -115.4 dB; three pairs -115.5 dB (no real gain, more error above the band); one pair (0.95 rad) -101.0 dB; a first-order loop (1 - z^-1) -101.2 dB, worse than plain rounding on rows where plain rounding was exact (it turns a zero error into a cycling one). **Not taken:** a double-precision output (changes the interface of 8.17, which 8.20 keeps); removing a DC offset before the conversion (changes the output's meaning, steps at width writes); dither (adds noise everywhere, also to patches without narrow pulses). **(review)** |
| 3 | **A constant output stays exact: plain rounding once the value has stood still for 128 conversions** (`OutputRounding::kSettle`). | Shaping a constant makes the loop cycle by a few float steps: a silent or held output, exactly constant before, would carry idle tones (the first version failed the bin-centred gate on a silent Model A saw+pulse, -23.8 dB re a "fundamental" of -207 dB re 1). After 128 equal values in a row the error state is cleared and the value rounded plainly; any change starts the loop again from zero. 128, not less: with 32 the flat stretches between a 1.9 kHz pulse's edges at 96 kHz (about 33 samples) were rounded plainly and the exact scan kept only 4.0 dB of margin (96000 Hz, width 1, 1 939 Hz); with 64, 128, 512 or never settling: 13.4 dB at 96 kHz in each case, and from 60 to 400 Hz (stretches longer than 128) -113.8 dB at worst with 128 (-115.0 never settling). Pinned since review round 1 by a direct test of the class ("settles after 128 equal values and starts again from a cleared state", the count written out as 128): settling after 32 fails 96 of its 142 assertions, not clearing the error state on settling fails 2. Clearing is relied on because the batched path's silent loop skips conversions once settled, so what follows a stillness must not depend on what came before it. **(review)** |
| 4 | **The chip's own contribution is shaped, not the caller's buffer.** | `render` adds into the caller's buffer; `renderRound` shapes the float the chip adds, and the addition into a non-zero buffer is the caller's rounding. A chip shaping the buffer's sum would carry other chips' content in its error state and would never settle while the buffer changes (the bench accumulates into its buffer: the silent chip cost 6.4 instead of 1.9 ns). **For S4:** a part that sums 16 chips into one float buffer rounds again at every addition, and a periodic sum would again gather those errors; sum the chips in double and convert once, shaped the same way. With a zeroed buffer (every test, every gate) both readings are identical. **Measured by the review (round 1), binding the next step:** rendering into a buffer pre-filled with -0.75 (the buffer's addition rounds plainly at a float step of 2^-24) gives -96.52 dB by the exact measurement at 96 kHz, seven rows above -100 dB; two chips summed in one float buffer leave 0.86 dB of margin. The orchestrator puts the consequence into the contract (S3b's two-bus renderer gives its buses in double precision; the summing path is gated by the exact measurement at S4); the interface is not changed in this step. **(review)** |
| 5 | **Defect 2, the mechanism: a sync reset (or ring flip) on the same instant as one of the target's own boundaries lost its step.** | At the sweep position the values just before a digital event were re-derived from the phase (`renderValueAt`, which takes the cell from floor (phase)). At an exact integer frequency ratio a sync reset falls exactly where the target wraps (a saw), folds or wraps (a triangle; also at half-integer ratios and ratio 1/2) or changes cell (combined waveforms). When the target's phase had rounded onto the boundary while the boundary's own event time had rounded a little later (so the reset came first), the re-derived cell was the one after the boundary: the value before the reset equalled the value after it, so no band-limited step was emitted (saw), or the slope change was wrong (triangle), and the naive output jumped unfiltered. Whether the phase rounds onto the boundary depends on the arithmetic of each reset, and for these ratios it alternated between consecutive resets: the line at half the source's rate. **Isolating measurement:** an independent model of the synced saw (the continuous phase with the short kernel's band-limited steps at the exact wraps and resets, as the `[kernel]` test) against the renderer, Model B saw at 3 kHz, 96 kHz: ratio 2.71 and 2 agree within 1.5e-7; ratio 3 differs by up to 0.479 every 192 samples (every second source period), and the event log at those samples shows the reset taken with the phase at exactly 1.0 and an emitted step of 0 (the other resets: phase 0.99999999999999989, step -1.00024). **(review)** |
| 6 | **Defect 2, the fix: the line's own state at the sweep position** (`renderSweep`). | When the event lies at the sweep position (not behind it), the values before it come from the line's tracked cell (`renderValue`, `renderDerivative`), which records whether the boundary on the same instant has been taken; behind the sweep position (an event reached up to two cycles late, whose boundaries the sweep has passed) the phase is still re-derived, as before. Over 400 random patches the value differs from the old one in 3 events, all a sync reset at a saw's phase of exactly 1.0. Measured afterwards: the independent model agrees within 1.4e-7 at ratio 3 too. A further mutation that keeps the old slope (`renderDerivativeAt`) fails the new gate on triangle rows (38 assertions). **(review)** |
| 7 | **Defect 2, which ratios: settled.** | Measured (96 and 88.2 kHz; 1, 3 and 3.85 kHz for the faster oscillator; the target synced by a silent saw at an exact ratio; 7-term Blackman-Harris over 2^16 samples, not bin-centred; worst component more than 9 bins off the source's lines, re the strongest line), before -> after, with **the chip's own per-cycle output at the same frequency** (its digital stream, 2^20 cycles, windowed, the band 0 .. 20 kHz: an ideal band limit): triangle, saw, pulse: ratio 1 -44.5 -> -111.1 (chip there -94.9), 2 -111.1 -> -111.1 (chip -88.8), 3 -53.3 -> -111.1 (chip -96.4), 4 -111.1 -> -111.1, 5 -58.7 -> -111.1 (chip -72.5), 1/2 -70.7 -> -110.9 (triangle at its fold), 3/2 -75.7 -> -109.8, 5/2 -83.1 -> -108.5; ratios 4/3, 5/3, 2.71 were never affected (-110 to -115). Combined waveforms: ratios 1, 2, 3, 4, 5, 1/2, 3/2, 5/2 between 0.0 and -4.3 dB before (the worst rows Model A triangle+saw+pulse; Model B triangle+saw -35 to -45 dB at ratios 2 and 4), -111.8 to -118.2 dB after. **Ratios 2 and 4:** not affected for a saw, triangle or pulse in this grid, affected for combined waveforms; the reviewer (clean at 2 and 4) measured saws, the render cost step's report (-5.7 / -13.9 dB) took its worst rows from Model A triangle+saw. Both were right. Which ratio is hit depends on the boundaries a waveform has (a saw only its wrap, a triangle its fold too, a combined waveform every cell change) and on how each reset's arithmetic rounds, not on the ratio's parity. The chip's own output carries lines at these frequencies at -63 to -113 dB (its 1 MHz sampling and its whole-cycle resets, -50 dB at worst elsewhere in the band, which the renderer does not reproduce by design, S2b-2 row 3): no rendered line now needs the "chip + 1 dB" allowance of 8.20. **(review)** |
| 8 | **The gates** (`ChipRenderExactTests.cpp`). | **Narrow pulses:** the exact measurement (Bluestein's algorithm gives the rectangular DFT of any length; every bin up to the band of each rate configuration; the harmonics are exact bins). Fast subset in the suite (`[render][alias][exact]`, 3 s for both gates): the 11 worst rows found at 88.2 and 96 kHz (the old report's and those a first-order shaping makes worse) and one row near 3.85 kHz at each other rate, widths 1 and 4095, held to -100 dB and -103 dB. Hidden full scan (`[.render-exact]`, 32 s): every p / q in lowest terms with q <= 100 (140 at 352.8 and 384 kHz), 300 Hz to 3.85 kHz, both models, widths 1, 2, 64, 4032, 4094, 4095 (Model A against its own budget), then 13 more widths at the four worst frequencies of each rate: 6 760 rows, all with 3 dB to spare. **Exact ratios:** the target and source registers num x M and den x M (M a quarter-integer: the increments are exact multiples, asserted). Fast subset (`[render][alias][sync-exact]`): 88.2 and 96 kHz, both models, saw, triangle, pulse 1300, triangle+saw, triangle+saw+pulse 1024, ratios 1, 2, 3, 5, 1/2, 3/2, 5/2, 1 and 3.85 kHz (280 rows). Hidden (`[.render-sync-integer]`, 2 min 20 s): every internal rate, 17 ratios (1 to 8, 1/2, 1/3, 1/4, 3/2, 5/2, 7/2, 4/3, 5/3, 3/4, 5/4), nine waveforms and widths, 110 Hz to 3.85 kHz: 11 970 rows held to the sync budgets of 8.4.5 with 3 dB to spare and no large correction; 882 silent targets skipped (a pulse, or a combined shape with the pulse, reset before it opens at a ratio below 1; checked to be only such rows). The old hidden reports (`[.render-exact]` and `[.render-sync-integer]` in `ChipRenderCostTests.cpp`) now assert too (the sync report above the register's top: the -80 dB of 8.18.1 for strongest lines above -50 dB re 1). **(review)** |
| 9 | **Output against the render cost step's renderer.** | 400 random patches (both models, both shape sets, all seven rates, Authentic in a quarter, up to 12 kHz in a fifth, random writes, random `renderUntil` stops; a third without any sync bit, a third without any pulse bit): **without sync** (141 patches) at most 4.8e-7 (-126.4 dB re 1), **without pulses** (133) at most 4.8e-7, neither (2) 3.6e-7: only the output's rounding (row 2), which every patch shows. With sync: one patch differs by more (0.233, 45 samples): the 3 resets of row 6, each a lost step before. **(review)** |
| 10 | **Cost** (`chip_bench`, 96 kHz, ns per internal sample per chip, best of two runs of best of 5, back to back with the base). | Base / this step: `silent` 1.9 / 2.0, `typical` 12.2 / 13.3, `typicalA` 36.2 / 35.5, `sawsA` 42.9 / 42.1, `s4gate` 30.4 / 30.9, `combined` 96.0 / 91.4, `comb12kA` 187.1 / 179.2, `comb12kB` 162.7 / 156.7, `worst` 324.7 / 323.9, `noise` 169.4 / 162.8, `noisesrc` 179.3 / 177.4, `landA` 192.4 / 192.7: within 5 % of 8.18.1 in every case (the runs themselves scatter by about 5 %). The conversion costs about a nanosecond per sample where the output moves; a settled output (a silent chip) takes the old loop. **(review)** |
| 11 | **Known defects 3 and 4 of 8.18.1** (a width write moves the pulse edge two cycles early; a frozen line against one crawling below one accumulator step). | Not touched: neither is in the code this step changed (`gateAt` at writes; the frozen-line landings). They stay recorded as they were. |
| 12 | **Floating-point contraction differs between compilers.** | The shaping expression `y - (2.25 e0 - 2.875 e1 + 2.25 e2 - e3)` may be contracted into fused multiply-adds by Apple Clang (contraction within an expression is its default) and not by MSVC, and GCC's default depends on the language mode; the low bits of the feedback, and so of some output samples, can then differ between compilers (as elsewhere in the renderer, S2b-2 row 11 and engineering notes). No test compares rendered output bits across builds: every bit-identity test (any split, batched against general path, copies of a chip) compares two renders of the same build; the committed reference data are digital traces and audio features compared within 0.1 to 0.75 dB; the tests against independent models allow 1e-6, and the shaping's bound (4.75 float steps of the value, 5.7e-7 for values below 2) holds whatever the contraction, since every stored error is exactly the rounding of its own value. So no threshold can be crossed by it. **(review)** |
| 13 | **Review round 1: tests added.** | (1) The exact gate on the general path (batching switched off; a pulse whose sync bit is set with a frozen, silent source), 16 rows: a general path that rounds plainly fails 10 assertions (-97.3 dB). (2) The shaping measured directly: the shaped error's averaged density over plain rounding's at most the NTF's in-band maximum + 0.7 dB at every rate configuration (measured -7.95 dB at 88.2 / 96 kHz, -8.23 dB at the others, against -8.16 / -8.52 exact; the window's leakage near the band's edges and the averaging account for the difference), and the output within 4.75 float steps (measured 4.51): a first coefficient of 2.0 fails 7 assertions (-4.1 dB). (3) The settle count and the clearing (row 3). **(review)** |

## B-Side render cost step (2026-11-18, all review)

Contract: `docs/architecture.md` 8.18. Design as built: "RENDERING" at the end of `source/bside/chip/Chip.h`.
Figures: `chip_bench`, this Mac, arm64, non-LTO, best of 5, ns per internal sample per chip at 48 / 96 / 192
kHz, measured back to back with the binary of the branch point while another agent was building (indicative).

| # | Point | Reading taken (reason) |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Where the time went** (S2b-2, 96 kHz). | Measured per stage with a sampling profiler before each change. Light patches: about 18 ns of the digital core per sample plus about 40 ns of per-sample machinery (pieces, event lists, three sweeps, re-anchoring, ring) even without a single event; Model A triangle and saw: the converter terms' loop over the bits in every value read (45 % of the time) and their events; Model A saw combinations: per-cycle stepping of the digital core (70 %); combined waveforms: the long kernel's second-order correction (three kernel reads per tap) and the general sweep per cell change. The changes below follow that order. **(review)** |
| 2 | **The batched path** (`renderBatch`). | Where nothing couples the lines to the digital core (no sync bit, no ring, no noise or per-cycle mode, no unconfirmed reset, no write-back, every envelope counter unable to change over the run), whole samples are rendered with the same sweeps and the same finish (`renderFinishOsc`, `renderOutput` shared) but without pieces and event lists; the digital phase at a sample's end is phase + increment x cycles, and the digital core is advanced once over the run (equal to stepping). Bit-identical to the general path, output, digital state and diagnostics (test aid `renderBatchingEnabled`; random patches, writes, envelopes, blocks; around Model A landings; under the state check). A deselected oscillator whose ring has settled adds a constant; a chip with nothing moving sums constants in a tight loop. The digital core is stepped per sample instead under `renderCheckStates` and for a pulling oscillator whose top bit may stay up. **(review)** |
| 3 | **Envelope stability for the batch** (`envStableSteps`, `envInert`). | A counter cannot change before a write while the envelope is frozen at zero (decay, release) or holds its sustain level with no counter step pending; while only the rate counter moves it cannot change for the steps to the match. A long advance of an inert envelope steps one period ((compare + 1) x divider period) and, if the state came back, skips whole repetitions: exact by construction (the steps depend only on the state and the registers). Tested against stepping at every rate and divider period, also off the rate counter's cycle. **(review)** |
| 4 | **The Model A pull-down in closed form** (`pulls`, `pullRun`). | A Model A combination with the saw (no noise, no test bit) no longer forces per-cycle stepping: its lower 55 bits run on as without the pull-down (so the noise clock's bit-19 rises are unchanged), lower-half stretches are jumped to their carry, a top bit that stays up is followed step by step (bounded by the jump, at most 4 096). The last step's output takes the phase before its pull-down, oscillator 1 reads oscillator 3's top bit before it (8.15.2 (c) step 1); `nextSyncRise` stops before every carry of such a source. In the batched path such an oscillator keeps a closed form (modulo 2^55) where every cell it can land on in the upper half has output bit 11 clear (all our fitted shapes, to the top of Smooth's range), else the digital core is stepped per sample. Tests: against stepping with both shape sets, pulse gates, ring and sync around it, jumps ending on every step (a mutation of the ring read is caught). **(review)** |
| 5 | **The write-back's per-cycle mode.** | The envelopes read nothing of the oscillators: `stepOscillators` steps the oscillators cycle by cycle and the envelopes advance over the same cycles in one go afterwards (`advanceCycles` and the renderer's per-cycle pieces). Noise with another waveform: 199 -> 164 ns at 96 kHz. **(review)** |
| 6 | **The kernels as tables** (`render::KernelTables`). | Both kernels laid out per table point with the 16 taps side by side, so a step reads two rows (four for a Hermite read) with one weight and lands in the ring in at most two contiguous runs; a pure step skips the ramp table. The short kernel keeps its own points and its float interpolation. The long kernel is read by cubic Hermite interpolation between its own 512 points with the exact derivatives (step' = kernel, ramp' = step) instead of the linear read with a second-order correction (three kernel reads per tap): the same accuracy class. A combined waveform's cell steps (no slope change) read the long kernel refined to 2 048 points by that interpolation, linearly (two rows instead of four). Pulse edges keep the Hermite read: read linearly from 2 048 (or 4 096) points, the narrowest pulse's nearly cancelling edge pair lost 1.5 to 2 dB of its 5.3 dB margin (Kaiser measurement, width 4095, 3.85 kHz, 88.2 kHz); with the split it is 5.34 dB as before. The kernel layout is about 510 KiB, built once. **(review)** |
| 7 | **The Model A converter terms as tables.** | The deviation of the index bits from imin on is one table read (`devSaw`, `devTri`, `triTail`; the bits below imin are 0 in the renderer's index) instead of a loop over the bits; imin is read from the exponent bits instead of ldexp (checked equal over 2 x 10^7 values). Same results up to the order of additions. The converter terms themselves are unchanged (every bit below half the internal rate as its own square wave); see row 12 for what would halve their events. **(review)** |
| 8 | **Written-out sweeps** (`renderSweepLine`, `renderSweepTable`). | Without digital events, ring flips or held values in a stretch, a triangle or saw line and a combined waveform's line sweep in a loop of their own: `renderNextLineEvent`, `renderValue` and `renderDerivative` written out for the mode with their constants taken once (`triSawValue` is the one place the triangle and saw value is written). Event times by the inverse slope in these loops and the emit time by the inverse of cycles per sample (rounding differences of about 1e-16). **(review)** |
| 9 | **Combined waveforms above the register's range (Smooth, up to 12 kHz)** (revised after the review). | Coarse staircases were built first and **rejected by the review**: a switch between levels clicked (the band-limited step stopped aliasing, not the change of shape: up to -40 dB re 1 within 40 samples of a switch, -26 dB on glides) and the pulse gate, sync and pull-downs cut the coarse staircase so that its images fell into the band (-18.3 dB re 1, Model B triangle+saw+pulse width 1024 at 88.2 kHz; -13.9 dB with a synced target; our report's -30.7 dB missed those cases). **Now** the same staircase is kept and only how its steps are emitted changes: above the register's top the cell steps within a stretch of the line (between two other events, at most a sample) are one step at the stretch's centre with their time moments up to the fourth (`RenderTables.h` `kStepMoments`: prefix sums per cell, the long kernel and its first three derivatives; the Taylor series of the band-limited step, its error about (2 pi f delta)^5 / 120 per unit of step, delta <= half a sample). A pulling oscillator's upper half and stretches with digital events keep the steps one by one. Entered 3 % above the register's top, left at it; the waveform does not change at a switch. Measured against one by one, in the band (<= 20 kHz): about -78 dB re 1 at worst (every combination, widths 1024 and 3000, a synced target, 4 to 12 kHz, 88.2 and 96 kHz; the review's -18.3 / -13.9 dB cases: -83.8 / -80.5 dB); near switches no more than the steady difference (the review's 5.9 -> 6.1 kHz case: -86.8 dB near the write, -82.9 steady). **Third review:** the stretches also end at two fixed points per sample (`kMomentParts` = 2: a step lies at most a quarter sample from its stretch's centre; the stretch ends are fixed in the sample, so they do not depend on where `renderUntil` stops, row 19). Measured (`[.render-above-alias]`, asserted on a subset in `[render][levels][alias]`): combined waveforms whose strongest line in 0 .. 20 kHz is above -60 dB re 1, both models, the four combinations, widths 1, 64, 1024, 2048, 3000, 4032, 4095, plain, synced and ring targets, 4 to 12 kHz, 88.2, 96 and 192 kHz, 7-term Blackman-Harris, not bin-centred: plain and ring targets alias at most **-106.4 dB re the strongest line** (one stretch per sample: -79.6 dB, Model B triangle+pulse width 1 at 5.3 kHz, 88.2 kHz: the limit missed). **Corrected after the fourth review:** the earlier reading "synced targets cannot meet -80 dB, the chip resets them at whole cycles" was wrong: the chip's own per-cycle output is 25 to 46 dB cleaner; the measurement used an exact integer ratio (3) between source and target, where the rendering has an older artefact (row 22). Synced rows now use ratio 2.71 and are held to the same -80 dB; the strongest line is taken outside DC's main lobe (a quiet shape's DC offset stood in for it before). Over the full grid, rows with a strongest line above -50 dB re 1 (1 209 rows): at most **-81.5 dB re the strongest line** (Model A saw+pulse, widths 1 and 64, synced, 12 kHz, 88.2 kHz; every plain and ring row below -85 dB). **Not held: shapes with a strongest line between -60 and -50 dB re 1** (12 rows, printed by the hidden test): worst -76.1 dB re the strongest line (fitted Model A triangle+saw synced at 12 kHz, 88.2 kHz, strongest line -60.0 dB re 1, so about -136 dB re 1; one by one -111.6 dB), the moments' error on a borderline-quiet shape. Three parts per sample settle it (-98.4 dB) but cost 228 / 196 ns (`comb12kA` / `comb12kB`, 96 kHz), over the 200 ns target; 2.5 parts cost as much. Cost at 12 kHz (96 kHz, `chip_bench` alone): 184 (Model A), 162 ns (Model B), target 200 ns. At and below the register's top nothing changes (tested bit-identical, also at 2 % above it when coming from below). **(review)** |
| 10 | **The three defects of 8.17 and a sibling** (`[defects]`, each failing on the S2b-2 renderer). | (1) A synced pulse at frequency 0 kept its gate after a reset (a frozen line has no next edge). (1b, found by the review) a frozen Model A pull-down (frequency 0, then a saw+pulse combination above the cell) kept its gate open: the one-cycle-later re-read was scheduled as a phase, which a frozen line never reaches. Both now go through `renderScheduleReread`: a phase on a moving line, a time on a frozen one (carried to the next sweep if needed; the batched path leaves such a line to the general one). The state check no longer skips a frozen line at a boundary; its reason to skip one: within three cycles of a reset or while that re-read is due (`renderFrozenStateChecks` proves it checks them). (2) Placeholder shapes, a width write in the step before a ring source's rise: the line simulation pulls such a source one cycle after the rise where the chip's next comparison is low; the line's own re-read reads the rendered gate even in cell 0x800. (3) A large frequency drop on a sync source after render() at a sample end: the target's prediction is dropped when the source's line falls below its top bit again. Long runs clean: 50 000 near-write patches, 3 000 schedules. **(review)** |
| 11 | **Output against the S2b-2 renderer.** | Over 400 random patches with random write schedules (both models, both shape sets, every internal rate, Authentic in a quarter, random blocks of `render` and `renderUntil`; a scratch harness built once against the branch point and once against the branch, not committed): at most 3.6e-7 (-128.9 dB re 1) in the patches up to 3.85 kHz; in the patches reaching 12 kHz at most 4.3e-3 per sample (the step moments, row 9; per sample, not band-limited to 20 kHz); after the third review (two parts per sample, row 9) 3.0e-4 (-70.5 dB re 1), the patches up to 3.85 kHz unchanged. **(review)** |
| 12 | **Targets not met: Model A triangle and saw** (96 kHz, alone on this Mac: `typicalA` 35.0, `sawsA` 41.1, `s4gate` 30.1 ns against 24; `combined` 85.5 against 60, row 13). | Per stage (profiler shares): `sawsA` batched sample loop 46 %, steps added 29 %, line sweep 25 %, digital core 3 %; `typicalA` 49 / 28 / 22 / 3 %; `s4gate` batched loop 47 %, steps 23 %, table and line sweeps 19 %, pull-down predictions 6 %, digital core 4 %. The converter terms make about two events per sample (every boundary of the lowest rendered bit). **Dropping a free saw's bit at half a toggle per sample** (the review's suggestion; its square wave then lies above a quarter of the internal rate) was built and measured against today's rule in the band (<= 20 kHz): free saw, saw as a ring source and vibrato -98 dB re 1 or lower, but -91.5 dB around gate toggles (the envelope steps modulate the dropped square waves into the band), -62.5 dB around test-bit phase resets and -63.2 dB around saw <-> triangle switches (a dropped square wave cut at the jump): a changed sound in the band at or below 3.85 kHz, so **not adopted** (it would have given `sawsA` 28.2, `typicalA` 30.8, `s4gate` 26.0 ns, still above 24). Grouping the bits' steps of a stretch by their moments (row 9) costs about as much as the two steps it replaces and would add in-band error near -109 dB re 1. What would be needed: a cheaper per-sample loop for the batched lines (about half of these cases) or fewer rows per step; not found within the gates. **(review)** |
| 13 | **Target not met: `combined`** (Model A, three combined waveforms; 85.5 ns at 96 kHz alone, target 60). | About 5.6 cell changes per sample (the triangle+pulse alone 3.6). Per stage (profiler shares): table sweeps 49 %, long-kernel steps 29 %, batched sample loop 12 %, pull-down predictions and the rest 10 %. Candidates not done: fewer rows per long step (a float long table loses the narrow pulses' cancellation); grouping the steps by their moments below the register's top (row 9) would change the sound in the band (about -80 dB re 1). **(review)** |
| 14 | **The digital core alone** (the "digital core only" rows). | `typical` per sample about 1 ns above the branch point (the pull-down checks in `skip`), `worst` 128 -> 37 ns. The rendering no longer calls it per sample where it batches. `envAdvance` and `envSteps` are forced inline with `BS_CHIP_INLINE` (`always_inline` on Clang and GCC; **plain `inline` on MSVC**, where `__forceinline` raises C4714 wherever it cannot inline, e.g. in a Debug build, and warnings are errors). **(review)** |
| 15 | **`s4gate`, the S4 gate's patch** (8.5: "pulse, saw, a combined waveform", Model A). | Pulse (a width write every tick) about 220 Hz, saw about 440 Hz, triangle+saw about 330 Hz (the combined waveform with the most expensive digital rule, the pull-down), envelopes sustaining. **(review)** |
| 16 | **No division by a zero increment** (review, HIGH). | `pullRun` divided by the increment: a Model A saw combination at frequency 0 (a control written before any frequency, a trace) raised SIGFPE on x86-64. A lower half that never moves is now taken as it is. Every division and modulo added in this step was checked: the line sweeps divide only with a positive slope, the envelope skip by a period of at least 9 steps, the emit by the cycles per sample (prepared positive); increments below one accumulator step divide fine. Regression test over every saw combination, both halves, frequency 0 and increments below one accumulator step; the sanitizer build (ASan + UBSan, `halt_on_error`) passes every `bside` and `trace` test. **(review)** |
| 17 | **Mutations the review found surviving.** | Killed by new assertions: the state check skipping frozen lines (12 failed), the entry hysteresis at 0.97 x instead of 1.03 x (16), one extra converter bit (92), a missing moment term (226). **Corrected after the third review:** `envInert` ignoring a pending counter step is **not** equivalent: `renderUntil` to a sample's end keeps the sample in progress, a sustain write there (counter a multiple of 0x11, step pipeline 1) leaves the step pending when the batched path takes over after that sample (the review's case: Model B, 96 kHz, decay 3, counter 0xaa at cycle 8928); now tested at 173 such last cycles over every rate and decay 0 to 3, failing with the check removed. One is equivalent and kept as a defence: `kMargin = 0` in the batched path (the margin covers rounding differences of about 1e-16 between the batch's comparison and the sweep's; no test produces such a tie). **(review)** |
| 18 | **Narrowest pulses at small-denominator frequencies** (review, for the record; predates this step). | A rectangular DFT over whole signal periods finds the -100 dB budget missed by up to 2.7 dB for Model B widths 4095 and 1 at frequencies with a small-denominator ratio to the internal rate (88200 x 3 / 73 Hz, width 4095: -97.3 dB; 3780 Hz: -98.4; 88200 x 3 / 71 Hz, width 1: -98.7; 96000 x 2 / 59 Hz: -100.0); `main` gives the same. Hidden report `[.render-exact]` reproduces it; no kernel change in this step. **(review)** |
| 19 | **Pieces aligned to the sample, not to the caller's stops** (third review, items 1 and 2). | The output depended on where `renderUntil` stopped although no write followed: below the register's top (the review's Model A placeholder case at 384 kHz, Authentic, a synced triangle at 0x15 with oscillator 2 pulling at 0x31, a raw 0x0f = 0x02 write: a different line simulation after a stop) and above it (the step moments' stretches). **Cause:** a piece, the renderer's unit of line simulation (pull cells, glitch carries, sync crossings, the stretches), ended at every stop, so each decision was taken from wherever the caller stopped. **Now** a piece starts at a sample's start or at a write and ends at the sample's end (`renderPieceOpen`, `renderPieceAdvance`, `renderPieceClose`); a stop inside a piece only advances the digital core; a write closes the open piece at its cycle (as before). Tested: random cases rendered with and without stops at random non-write cycles (1 to 3 000 cycles ahead, random block sizes, both models and shape sets, every write kind, sync and ring, no chains, Authentic in a quarter) must be bit-identical: 150 seeds in the suite (`[split]`), 4 000 in the hidden `[.render-split-long]`, and 12 000 low-frequency seeds in a scratch search, all clean; the second round's renderer (`9836e71`) failed 7 of the 150 and 15 of the 21 regression assertions (seeds the long run and the review found, `[split]` regression case). Below 3.85 kHz the output changes against the second round only where a stop used to fall inside a sample (the output is now the one without that stop). **(review)** |
| 20 | **A frozen Model A saw combination pulled down by a write** (third review, item 3). | Frequency 0 in the upper half, then a width write that closes the comparison (placeholder shapes) or a waveform write onto a cell whose bit 11 is clear (fitted): the chip pulls the line down one or two steps on. `renderLanding` returned at once for a zero increment, so nothing was predicted: one large correction, and a ring target kept the old top bit. Now a frozen line is landed like a moving one (it lands on the same cell every step, so the search ends after two steps); the general sweep pulls it at the landing's time, its ring target flips (oscillator 1 one cycle later, as at every pull-down), and the batched path leaves it to the general path while the landing is pending. Siblings checked: every early return for a zero increment in the landing and pull-down code. Regression test for both shape sets, width and waveform writes, with and without a ring target (failing on the second round's renderer). **(review)** |
| 21 | **Mutations of the third review.** | Killed by new assertions: `envInert` ignoring a pending step (row 17); the batched path taking a line whose frozen re-read is pending (writes 0 to 2 cycles before a sample's end, 4 failed); the frozen re-read at the sweep's start forcing the gate low (a width below the pulled cell, where the re-read finds it high, 40 failed); a pulling oscillator's upper half emitted by moments without a sync chain (12 failed). **Equivalent:** the same re-read in the sweep's event loop forcing the gate low (it is reached only after a sync reset of a frozen line, where the accumulator is 0 and a width above 0 keeps the comparison low). **The frozen re-read scheduled at the pull-down's own cycle instead of one later** (corrected after the fourth review): **equivalent only for the fitted tables.** A pull-down with the gate open comes only from an upper-half cell whose bit 11 is clear and lands 2 048 cells below; on the fitted saw+pulse and triangle+saw+pulse shapes every such lower cell is 0, so the gate's re-read cannot change the output (a pull-down with the gate closed lands below the width, where the gate stays closed). Asserted on the fitted tables ("the fitted Model A saw combinations pull down onto cells whose value the gate does not change"), so a refit that breaks it fails. The placeholder shapes break it (935 such cells of triangle+saw+pulse); a test there has no oracle yet (row 24). **Fourth review, killed:** the batched path taking a frozen line whose landing is still pending (`landPull`; a write 0 to 2 cycles before a sample's end, oscillators 2 and 3; 4 failed) and a frozen source's ring flip without oscillator 1's one-cycle delay (12 failed), both by "a frozen Model A saw combination pulled down by a write sounds as one crawling below one accumulator step". **(review)** |
| 22 | **Open, older than this step: synced targets at an exact integer frequency ratio** (fourth review; left to the alias follow-up step). | At an exact integer ratio between a sync source and its target the rendering alternates between source periods (a line at half the source's rate); identical on `main`, at and below 3.85 kHz too, where no test measured an integer ratio. The reviewer's figures, 96 kHz (oscillator 1 a saw with its gate off at register reg / 3, oscillator 2 at reg = floor (hz x 2^24 / 985248 / 3) x 3 + 0.75, saw + sync + gate, width 2048), against the chip's own per-cycle output: Model B saw at 3 000 Hz -43.8 dB re the strongest line (chip -89.8 dB); 3 848 Hz -55.0 dB; Model B triangle+saw at 3 500 Hz -41.1 dB (chip -65.3); Model A triangle+saw at 3 848 Hz -26.9 dB (chip -59.8), at 9 300 Hz -9.5 dB; `renderCorrections` 0.
