Hidden report `[.render-sync-integer]` (ratios 2, 3, 4, 5, 3.0001 and 2.71; both models; saw and the four combinations; 1 to 3.848 kHz and 9.3 kHz; 88.2 and 96 kHz; strongest line above -60 dB re 1), on this branch: worst per ratio -5.7 (2), -9.5 (3), -13.9 (4), -33.0 (5), -87.9 (3.0001), -88.2 dB (2.71) re the strongest line, the worst rows Model A triangle+saw at 9.3 kHz. **Differs from the reviewer** at ratios 2 and 4 (clean, -110 to -153 dB, in its measurement): its setup at those ratios may differ (here the source is always the target's register divided by the ratio); to be settled in the follow-up step. **(review)** |
| 23 | **Open, older than this step: a width write changes the rendered gate two cycles before the chip's output** (fourth review). | 8.17 states a lead of 0 to 1 cycle. At a write `gateAt` takes the new comparison at once, while the chip uses it one step later. Against the chip's band-limited per-cycle output, a width write that closes the comparison is off by 0.0417 at a one-cycle lead (Model A, placeholder shapes, control 0x71, 88.2 kHz) and by 0.080 for a pure pulse; a waveform write matches to 2.7e-4 (the reviewer's measurement; not re-measured here). **(review)** |
| 24 | **Open: a frozen line and one crawling below one accumulator step render differently in a few cases.** | Both make the same digital steps (checked cycle by cycle), neither shows a state mismatch or a large correction, and which is right is not settled (no band-limited per-cycle oracle in the suite). Placeholder shapes, Model A triangle+saw+pulse at 88.2 kHz, a frequency-0 or 1e-7 write right after `render()` (oscillator 1 frozen in cells 3000 .. 3500, widths 16 to 2 284): 0.0138 at the write's sample (hidden report `[.render-frozen-crawl]`, exact setup in its comment; the reviewer found it bit-identical in every case it tried, not in this one). With a ring target and a waveform write right after `render()` (fitted shapes, oscillator 2 or 3): up to 0.05 at 96 kHz and 0.004 at 128 kHz (printed, not held, by the `[frozen-pull]` test; writes inside a sample agree within 1e-3). A pull-down onto a non-zero lower cell with the gate open (placeholder shapes, row 21): 0.005. A rough band-limited reference built from the chip's per-cycle output could not tell the two apart (its own kernel error is about 0.15 at the step). **(review)** |

## B-Side parameter step (2026-11-18, all review)

Contract: `docs/architecture.md` 8.7 (63 per-part parameters, `chip.clock`, 202 in all), 8.8, 8.14. Code:
`source/bside/params/` (`PartParamList.h`, `PartMappings.h`, `ParameterLayout.*`). The parameters are inert:
the audio thread reads none of them yet.

| # | Point | Reading taken (reason) |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Unit of a host value.** 8.7 gives ranges in physical units for most rows and "0 ... 1" for a few. | A host value is the contract's own range, in its own unit: cents (-100 ... +100), percent (0 ... 100, -50 ... +50, -100 ... +100, 0 ... 200), register units (0 ... 2047), Hz (0.5 ... 12, 10 ... 400), integers; "0 ... 1" rows (velocity amounts, mod wheel vibrato, unison detune, portamento time, pan -1 ... +1) stay 0 ... 1 with display text in the mapped unit. Nothing is a hidden normalised value (Vintage's cents and percent rows are). The conversions to chip units are in `PartMappings.h` (pulse width 50 % -> 2048, 100 % -> 4095; PW offset +-50 % -> +-2048; cutoff offset +-100 % -> +-2047). **(review)** |
| 2 | **Stepped integer or choice?** | Numeric stepped ranges are `SteppedIntParameter` (the shared type the arp step notes use, stepped for hosts): `oscN.coarse` (-24 ... +24 st), the four envelope nibbles, `filter.resonance`, `chip.volume`, `tick.mult` (1 ... 8). Sets of unrelated labels are choices: `assign.unison_voices` (2, 4, 8, 16) and `tick.sync` (6, 12, 24, 48 per beat), as Vintage does for the unison count. **`bend.range` (0 ... 12 st) is a choice with labels "0 st" ... "12 st"**, because Vintage's identical parameter is one (a user moving between instruments sees the same control). **(review)** |
| 3 | **Order of `osc3.mute`.** The table lists it after the `oscN.*` rows. | The `oscN.*` block is the 11 rows from `wave` to `filter`; `osc3.mute` follows the third block, then `filter.cutoff`. (Reading `oscN.filter` as the last row of each block or `osc3.mute` as part of block 3 gives the same order.) |
| 4 | **Envelope text.** 8.7: attack 2, 8, ... 8 s; decay and release three times that. | Under one second whole milliseconds ("250 ms", "750 ms"), from one second seconds with one decimal, a trailing ".0" dropped ("1 s", "1.5 s", "24 s"). Typed text: "ms" is milliseconds, any other "s" seconds, a bare number milliseconds (Vintage counts a bare number as seconds; here the nominal times start at 2 ms); the nearest nominal time wins (nearest in the log domain). Values in the DSP step are the nominal times only through `PartMappings.h`; the real chip rates are the engine's business. **(review)** |
| 5 | **Tick free rate** (10 ... 400 Hz "exponential"). | A float in Hz whose host range maps exponentially (`NormalisableRange` with lambdas; geometric mean 63.2 Hz at the middle). The default 50 Hz is therefore not at the middle. **(review)** |
| 6 | **Vibrato rate** (0.5 ... 12 Hz) and the other continuous rows without a stated curve. | Linear in the host range; the contract states a curve only for the tick rate. The editor (M7 for B-Side) may use its own slider curve. **(review)** |
| 7 | **Labels.** The contract names the values but not always the text. | "6 per beat", "12 per beat", "24 per beat", "48 per beat" for `tick.sync`; "Model A" / "Model B"; "Smooth" / "Authentic"; filter modes exactly as listed ("Low+Band", ...); "50 Hz system" / "60 Hz system"; the multiplier is shown "x1" ... "x8"; the velocity names use Vintage's arrow ("Velocity → Volume"). Part names are prefixed "Lower " and "Upper " as Vintage. **(review)** |
| 8 | **`chip.clock` in programs.** It is outside the `lower.`/`upper.` prefixes and is not excluded in the preset schema. | It is part of a program's `global` section like the 75 shared parameters (except master volume and the limiter). Confirmed by owner (2026-11-19): saved with the program, because the clock is part of the sound. |
| 9 | **Display text of a continuous value.** | One decimal for percentages ("50.0 %", "+10.0 %"), two for the vibrato rate, whole cents and register units; typed text round-trips to the same text, not to the same float (a displayed number is only as exact as its text; tested as idempotence). Zero is shown without a sign ("0 ct", "0.0 %"). |
| 10 | **Binding.** | `PartParamBinding::bind` (off the audio thread) caches the atomics and throws `vs::ConfigError` for a missing parameter in every build type; `read (PartParams&)` is lock-free and sanitises like Vintage's (non-finite -> default, out of range -> limited). No smoothing yet: `PartParamList.h` records which entries are continuous (to be smoothed) and which stepped. |
| 11 | **Files and sessions.** | Nothing needed changing in the preset code: a program, tone or bank file or a session that lacks parameters loads them at their defaults (the shared rule), and a saved file lists them. `stateVersion` stays 1 (nothing is released). |
| 12 | **JSON value cap raised from 160 000 to 192 000** (`BSideTables.cpp`; the file cap stays 12 MiB). | The existing "largest legitimate bank" test demands at most 90 % use of the value cap: with 202 parameters per program the worst bank has 144 455 values (was 136 327), 90.3 % of 160 000. 192 000 leaves it at 75 %. The worst bank is now 10 109 368 bytes (was 9 884 536), still 24 % under the 12 MiB cap (the test asks for 15 %), so the file cap is unchanged. The test that pins the number (`TablePresetTests.cpp`) was updated. **(review)** |
| 13 | **Typed text always gives a finite value in the range** (review round). | Every B-Side text -> value path returns a finite, in-range value; text with no number, "nan", "inf", "Infinity" or an exponent past the double range (1e999) gives the parameter's **default**. This includes velocity, portamento and pan, whose shared parser (`vs::fromText`, untouched) accepts "nan" and reads "inf" as full right: B-Side guards the text before calling it. Tested for all 127 parameters. **(review)** |
| 14 | **Number parsing** (`bs::parseFirstNumber`). | The first well-formed number in the text: optional sign, digits, optional fraction, optional exponent; a unit after it is ignored. "1e3" is 1000, "-1e3" -1000, " 12 ct " 12, "+7" 7; "--5" is **-5** (the scan skips a sign that is not followed by a digit and takes the next position); "abc" and "" have no number (the default). The envelope parser reads the sign and exponent too (a negative time is the shortest setting). **(review)** |
| 15 | **Choices read typed text like a person types it.** | B-Side's choice parameters are a small subclass of `AudioParameterChoice` (in `ParameterLayout.cpp`) that overrides the text -> value function: a label in any case ("on", "TRI+SAW", "60 hz system"); a bare number that equals the leading number of exactly one label ("24" -> "24 per beat", "5" -> "5 st", "4" -> unison "4", leading zeros ignored); anything else is JUCE's own rule (an unknown text is the first label). No change in `source/common/` was needed. **(review)** |
| 16 | **Smooth resolution is not quantised by the mappings** (8.4.6). | `pulseWidthRegister`, `pwOffsetRegister` and `cutoffOffsetRegister` return the continuous register value as a float (0 ... 4095, +-2048, +-2047; 50 % pulse width is 2047.5). `authenticRegister (value, lo, hi)` is the separate rounding and limiting helper for Authentic resolution and for integer display (round to nearest, halves away from zero; non-finite gives lo). The filter cutoff parameter is itself already a continuous register value. |
| 17 | **Envelope text: nearest nominal time in the log domain, pinned.** | A typed time maps to the setting whose nominal time has the smallest ratio distance (|log (nominal / typed)|), so the decision point between 250 and 500 ms is 353.6 ms (a linear rule would say 375 ms): "360 ms" is setting 10 (500 ms), "350 ms" setting 9. Chosen because the settings are spaced roughly geometrically (2, 8, 16, ... 8000 ms). Pinned by tests that fail under the linear rule. **(review)** |

## B-Side S2b-2: band-limited waveform rendering (2026-11-15, all review)

Contract: `docs/architecture.md` 8.4.2, 8.4.4, 8.4.5, 8.9.4, 8.15. Design as built: "RENDERING" at the end of
`source/bside/chip/Chip.h`. The reference emulator was not run for this step (only committed reference
features were used); its source and tables were not opened.

| # | Point | Reading taken (reason) |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | **What `render` covers of 8.4.4** ("adds the output after volume, before the board filter", 8.4.7). | S2b renders oscillator -> waveform converter -> x envelope converter, summed; routing, filter, oscillator 3 off, voice DC, volume and board filters wait for S3. `render` adds the plain sum of the three voices (d (v) minus the converter's mid-scale, times the envelope), so S3 can take the voices apart later without changing the digital side. **(review)** |
| 2 | **Splitting a sample at a write.** 8.4.7 has `render (out, n)` in whole samples; 8.16 renders "to `nextTickCycle()`". | Added `renderUntil (out, maxSamples, untilCycle)`: it stops inside a sample at the requested cycle, so a write made then lands at its exact cycle; the next call completes the sample. `render (out, n)` stays (whole samples). A write right after `render` (at a sample boundary) is placed at its cycle too, with values taken up to one cycle later. **(review)** |
| 3 | **The ideal waveform** (what "band-limited form of the chip's waveform" means when the chip itself is sampled at 1 MHz). | The continuous line through the digital accumulator: saw wraps and triangle folds where the line crosses them, pulse edges one cycle after the line crosses the width (the comparison is one cycle late, 8.15.2 (c)), so the rendered waveform leads the chip's digital output by 0 to 1 cycle. Without that, the chip's own 1 MHz sampling (edge jitter of up to one cycle) would alias at about -50 dB, and the budgets of 8.4.5 could not hold. **(review)** |
| 4 | **Sync resets** happen in the digital core at the cycle after the source's top bit rose. | Rendered at the source's crossing (sub-cycle), predicted from the source's line and confirmed by the digital reset; the target's line then runs offU = its advance between the two ahead of the digital accumulator. Placing the reset at the digital cycle aliases at about -40 dB (measured during development). A prediction the digital core does not confirm within three cycles (the sync-chain exception, 8.15.2 (d)) is corrected band-limited at the next sample end and counted (`renderCorrections`). **(review)** |
| 5 | **Model A converter** (8.4.4: "an unterminated ladder with a ratio of about 2.2"). | A resistor-ladder network solved for its bit weights (`RenderTables.cpp`), ratio 2.2, unterminated, for the 12-bit waveform and the 8-bit envelope converter; **Model B ideal** (ratio 2, terminated: binary weights), as "near-ideal" in 8.4.4. Our own model, not fitted further. **Asserted against the reference features** (`RenderReferenceTests.cpp`, `[dac]`, our tolerances, no number in 8.9.4): envelope converter levels per frame (`a_env_dac_*`, every counter value, frames from 24 on where both lie above -40 dBFS, one gain): rms 0.13 dB (A) / 0.09 dB (B), worst frame 0.70 / 0.36 dB (allowed rms 0.3, worst 1.0); waveform converter (`a_dac_*` saw 110 Hz, saw 1 kHz, pulse 110 Hz): harmonics 2..8 within 0.1 to 0.3 dB (allowed 0.6). The first frames are left out: power-on and the first gate carry S3's voice DC and board high-pass (the earlier "constant 0.4 dB" for Model B was the gain fitted with those frames in). A Model A converter made ideal fails the test (mutation). **Finding for S3:** the reference's triangle at 110 Hz has a second harmonic at -34 dB (A) / -40 dB (B) and a fourth at -43 / -52 dB; a converter that is linear in its bits cannot make even harmonics on a triangle (its two halves are complementary codes), so this is a nonlinearity after the converters (mixer or output stage); the triangle segment is not compared. **(review)** |
| 6 | **Converter-bent triangle and saw** (8.4.5 permits mip tables for shapes the DAC bends). | Closed form instead: d (v) = g v + sum e_i bit_i (v); every index bit whose square wave stays below half the internal rate is rendered as its own square wave with exactly placed edges, the faster ones as their mean (a band-limited step where the set changes with the frequency). Exact for sync, ring and writes, which a table read is not. **(review)** |
| 7 | **Combined waveforms** (8.4.5 names mip-mapped tables). | Rendered as exact band-limited staircases: our fitted shapes change value at only 51 to 664 of the 4096 cells per period, so every change is a BLEP. Mip tables were built first: free-running they met the budget, but the pulse gate (and ring, sync, pull-down) multiplies a band-limited table by a hard edge and aliased at -50 to -75 dB, which BLEP/BLAMP corrections of the jump and slope cannot remove. Cost: up to about 2.6 million steps per second per oscillator at the register maximum (Model A saw+pulse). **(review)** |
| 8 | **Model A pull-down** (8.15.2 (c) step 4) in the renderer. | The chip pulls in the first step whose landing cell (whole cycles) has output bit 11 clear, the pulse comparison (made on the landing before) included. From a rise the line is pulled where it enters such a cell, the gate taken as the chip compared it on the landing before the rise (whole cycles; the chip's own comparison when a write followed that step). After a write inside the upper half the chip's landings are followed on the digital accumulator (`renderLanding`, also when the line, less than a cycle ahead, has already wrapped; above one cell per step the landings skip cells) and the pull-down is scheduled at that landing. (Following the landings from every rise too was built and measured: no test and no harness count changed, so it was removed.) The one-cycle output glitch the chip shows at that cycle (the upper-half value for one cycle) is not rendered in the oscillator's own output (staircase-level detail); as oscillator 1's ring or sync source it is (row 26). The pulse gate is re-read from the new line one cycle after a pull-down. **(review)** |
| 9 | **Noise.** "sample-and-hold at the noise clock with the contract's treatment". | Each noise clock (a shift or a fill under the test bit) is a band-limited step at its exact cycle; noise with another waveform (write-back) renders the digital output cycle by cycle, a step wherever it changes (the core steps every cycle there anyway). |
| 10 | **How aliasing is measured** (8.4.5: "as for Vintage"). | At the internal rate, in 0 .. min (20 kHz, 20/48 x host rate), for every internal rate of 4.4 (88.2, 96, 128, 176.4, 192, 352.8, 384 kHz), bin-centred with a rectangular window (Smooth frequencies put the fundamental on a bin), against the fundamental; for sync, ring and combined waveforms against the strongest legitimate component, as Vintage's sync gate does (their fundamental can be weak; Model A saw combinations play at 2 f0 after the pull-down). B-Side has no decimator yet (S4): what it adds is measured with it. Sync: Vintage's ratios (1.37, 2.71, 0.71, 1.0031, 1.9969); no oscillator above the register maximum (3.85 kHz). Write discontinuities: a write every tick at its exact cycle, 7-term Blackman-Harris window, every component more than 9 bins from the signal's own lines. **(review)** |
| 11 | **A second, stronger BLEP kernel (coordinator decisions, 2026-11-15; the budget is not relaxed).** | With the shared kernel (8 zero crossings, Kaiser beta 8, float) Model A's combined waveforms missed "<= -96 dB up to 2 kHz" by up to 10 dB, and (independent review) narrow pulses (width <= 64 or >= 4032) reached -85 dB and Model B combined shapes with sync -88 dB: that kernel attenuates what folds into the band by at most 85.5 dB (`[.render-diag]`). `common/dsp/BlepTable.h` now takes zero crossings, Kaiser beta and the sample type as parameters (`BasicBlepTable<Z, betaTenths, Sample = float>`; `BlepTable` is Vintage's unchanged 8 / 8.0 / float). **Chosen: 8 zero crossings, Kaiser beta 12, double precision**, for every step of an oscillator playing a **pulse or a combined waveform, on both models** (and the writes that enter or leave one), read with a second-order correction of the table's linear interpolation (without it the nearly silent Model A triangle+saw+pulse stays 4 dB over). Searched: 8/10 to 16/12 (bin-centred rows, Model A combined) and 8/11, 8/12, 8/13, 10/12, 10/13, 12/13 (the second measurement, narrow pulses): 8/12 meets every row of both measurements with >= 5.3 dB to spare (the narrowest pulses at 88.2 kHz, 3.85 kHz: a pulse narrower than one chip cycle, two full steps that nearly cancel); 8/11 left those at 3.7 dB. Double precision did not change the figures (the floor was aliasing, not table precision) and is kept because the steps of a narrow pulse cancel to about -66 dBFS. Same length as the short kernel: the latency stays 8; triangle, saw, noise and the per-cycle modes keep the short kernel (row 25 for how that is tested); the fade of a deselected output takes the long kernel (row 23). **(review)** |
| 12 | **Latency.** | The chip adds `Chip::kLatencySamples` = 8 internal samples, the long kernel's window (which every step shares; both kernels have 8 zero crossings, so the short kernel's steps are not delayed further). Measured: a write's step lands at exactly cycle / cycles-per-sample + 8 (deviation below 6e-7 with each step's own kernel), through `renderUntil` and through `render` followed by a write (the step sits at the write's cycle, up to a cycle before the sample's end). At the host rate: 8 / factor samples (4 at 2x, 2 at 4x, 8 at 1x) plus S4's decimator. |
| 13 | **Agreement with the reference** (8.9.4). | Noise third-octave spectra (`a_noise_a`, `a_noise_b`, 8 frequency settings each) within 0.18 dB of the reference after one gain per trace (allowed 1.5 dB), rendered at 192 kHz through test stand-ins of the board filters (8.15.7) and a test decimator. The gain is fitted because the absolute scale is S3's. The other analog tolerances need the filter and the output stage (S3) and stay skipped. |
| 14 | **CPU** (`chip_bench`, this Mac, -O3, best of 5; noted for a cost step before S4). | ns per internal sample per chip at 48 / 96 / 192 kHz: typical (pulse with a width sweep, saw, triangle; Model B) 59 / 53 / 51, of which the digital core alone 18 / 17 / 16; worst (Model A, three synced oscillators at 3.85 kHz, every waveform switched every tick incl. combined shapes) 1297 / 719 / 426 (digital core 232 / 121 / 65); Model A combined waveforms on all three 676 / 364 / 206; noise with pulse on all three 293 / 166 / 102; **the review's cases**: silent chip 76 / 60 / 56; the typical patch on Model A 160 / 167 / 174; Model A three saws 218 / 226 / 239; three combined waveforms at 12 kHz (Smooth allows 12 kHz) 9718 / 4922 / 2525 on Model A, 8232 / 4215 / 2182 on Model B. After the second review (2026-11-16): typical 60 / 56 / 54, worst 1369 / 753 / 445, Model A typical 195 / 200 / 208, three Model A saws 255 / 263 / 273; new cases: a noise+saw source syncing and ringing (Model A) 438 / 316 / 258, Model A saw combinations at 3 kHz with a control write every tick 1200 / 640 / 355. "Typical" and "worst" understate. **The S4 gate (8.5: 16 voices <= 5 % of a core at 48 kHz 2x, filter included) allows about 31 ns per chip at 96 kHz.** Saving candidates (review): an idle / event-free fast path per oscillator (about 35 of the silent chip's 54 ns are overhead); the Model A converter terms through a table of the deviation, or dropping the bits below 6; a higher-resolution long table instead of the second-order correction; a pitch cap or tables for combined waveforms above 3.85 kHz. **(review)** |
| 15 | **Sync and ring from a Model A saw combination (review H1, defect fixed).** | The joint line simulation that predicts sync resets and ring flips did not apply the Model A pull-down, so such a source (triangle+saw, saw+pulse, triangle+saw+pulse) rose in the simulation and reset or flipped its target every period (sync: -44 dB against the chip's stream at 220 Hz, ring: inverted, +6 dB). Now the simulation pulls the source down where `renderSweep` does (the first upper-half cell whose output has bit 11 clear, the pulse gate included; `StepTable::firstClear`): a source pulled at once (all our fitted saw combinations are, at cell 0x800) never rises, never syncs, and its top bit never shows; a source pulled later (the S2a placeholder shapes, at 0xc00) rises, syncs, and flips its ring target back at the pull. Tested against the chip's own output stream (`[stream]`). **(review)** |
| 16 | **The pulse gate after a phase jump.** | After a sync reset the one-cycle-late comparison needs no re-read (the next edge computed from the new line already lies one cycle on: the re-check removed there); after a Model A pull-down landing above `slope` (a later pull-down) it does, and stays, tested with the placeholder shapes (a mutation without it fails). |
| 17 | **Small corrections (review); what `renderCorrections()` guarantees.** | The output starts on the chip's value at activation and after `reset()` (the samples before the first one are pre-filled with it; was an unbandlimited step 0 -> about -0.8); the Model A converter bits switch with hysteresis (row 22); `prepare` and the constructor always fetch both BLEP tables, and a Chip must be constructed and prepared off the audio thread; the render tables (about 180 KiB) are built in place; the physical output peak is asserted at 1.7 (three voices of +-0.5 plus about 12 % band-limited overshoot; 1.671 measured over random patches; was 4.0). **Corrections, exactly (after the third review, 2026-11-16):** no correction above 1e-4 of a period and no state mismatch (row 27) in 50 000 patches of the review's harness kind (writes 1 to 40 cycles into a sample, four write kinds, Authentic in a quarter; hidden `[.render-corrections-long]`, 66 s on this Mac) nor in 3 000 random write schedules of every write kind (hidden `[.render-schedule-long]`, 11 s); the suite runs 1 200 and 120 of them (`[corrections]`, `[schedule]`) plus every case the reviews and the long runs found. Smaller corrections remain: the sub-cycle time of a sync reset, rounded (at most 4e-6 of a period, a band-limited step of about -108 dB at the sample end). The three larger ones of the second round were defects (row 27). The smoothed difference from the chip's own stream stays at most 0.25 (asserted 0.3): the chip's sampling at its clock, which the continuous rendering does not reproduce (row 19); no rms bound, since a static combined waveform at the top of the range reaches 0.1 rms against the continuous rendering with nothing wrong. **(review)** |
| 18 | **Second alias measurement** (review). | Kaiser window (beta 20) after removing the mean, 2x zero padding, the lines from the actual increments, every bin more than 8 bins from a line; 20 fundamentals 60 Hz to 3.85 kHz (10 for sync and ring); internal rates 88.2 and 192 kHz in the suite (`[kaiser]`), all four of the reviewer's rates once with the reviewer's harness (7 800 cases, 0 corrections, >= 5.3 dB inside every budget). The mean must go first: the reviewer's harness reported the window's leakage of a quiet shape's large DC (-6 dBFS) at 8.5 bins (23 Hz at 88.2 kHz, 100 Hz at 384 kHz) as alias (narrow pulses -100.2 dB; with the mean removed -105.3 dB). Silent outputs (Model A saw+pulse at widths >= 2048: pulled down at once with the gate closed) are skipped (nothing to measure). |
| 19 | **Noted for the owner (review), no change.** | Sync on combined-waveform targets: the 0-1 cycle lead of the rendered line changes single harmonics against the chip's own stream (Model A triangle+saw synced at a 1.36 kHz source: 13.4 dB off on one harmonic). Measuring before the decimator is valid only with a decimator that attenuates 24-44 kHz by about 85 dB or more: S4 must gate after its decimator. At exact power-of-two periods the chip visits only every N-th table cell and differs from the continuous line (inherent to this reading). **(owner to review)** |
| 20 | **Predictions across a write (second review, item 4).** | At a write the renderer keeps or drops what it predicted and the chip has not done yet: a predicted pull-down always ends there (the line goes back to the digital phase; if the chip has not pulled yet, it decides with the new controls through `renderLanding` / `renderPullDown`); a predicted sync reset whose sync bit the write cleared is dropped. offU (the sub-cycle time between a rendered and a digital sync reset, held as a phase) is rescaled with the frequency at a frequency write, and so is the line of an unconfirmed sync reset, so the two lines stay less than a cycle apart in time (held as a phase, the offset grew to many cycles at a lower frequency, and the chip's pull-downs then came "late"). The ring state is set after the sources' pull-downs at the write. Effect in the review's harness (2 400 patches each): corrections with frequency writes 6 -> 0, width writes 2 -> 0, control writes 124 -> 60, mixed writes 76 -> 8. **(review)** |
| 21 | **Two defects the stream schedules found (fixed).** | (1) Two lines in step (the same frequency and phase, e.g. both from power-on) cross their top bits at the same instant; the joint simulation took the first crossing and skipped the other (its next boundary, computed from exactly 0.5, was the next half period): oscillator 1's ring from oscillator 3 was inverted for half of every period (smoothed difference 0.5 to 0.98). A boundary reached exactly at another line's crossing is now taken at once. (2) A sync reset that the sweep reaches more than a cycle late (from a per-cycle source, whose resets come from the digital core) lost the pulse gate's falling edge one cycle after it; the pulse stayed high until its next rising edge, which then inverted it (difference 1.0). The edge is now emitted at its own time. Both are regression cases in `[schedule]`. **(review)** |
| 22 | **Hysteresis of the Model A converter bits (second review, item 6).** | A bit is dropped when its square would toggle once per sample and taken back only below **half** of that (was 0.8: a +-12 % vibrato around 90 % of a limit switched 23 times in 2 s). Between half and the limit its content lies at 22 to 44 kHz at 88.2 kHz and higher, above the band; a kept bit there is the static state the alias gates measure. The state continues only from a triangle or saw; from another mode it is seeded afresh from the frequency and no switch is counted (it was seeded from the other mode's terms). Measured: the reviewer's vibratos (+-3 to +-25 %, around 80, 90 and 100 % of the limit, 2 s each) at most one switch; a glide 150 -> 525 -> 150 Hz at 96 kHz three (bit 3 and bit 4 dropped, bit 4 back). `[hysteresis]`. **(review)** |
| 23 | **Write discontinuities without a budget row (second review, item 8).** | Measured with the write-discontinuity method of the budget (7-term Blackman-Harris, lines every tick, every internal rate) and asserted at its -90 dB: a pulse deselected every other tick (the held output and its fade steps) -158 dB at 88.2 kHz down to -183 dB, the same figure on either kernel (the fade steps now take the long one, as the pulse they follow; harmless, but not needed by this measurement); a noise combination entered from noise every other tick (noise+saw, noise+pulse, noise+triangle+saw+pulse; per-cycle mode on the short kernel; a test-bit reset first makes the schedule periodic) -127 dB at 88.2 kHz down to -168 dB. The per-cycle mode keeps the short kernel: its output is short (the write-back clears it within a few hundred cycles; noise+pulse and the four-way combination mostly step to 0 at once) and lies far inside the budget. **(review)** |
| 24 | **The converter test against the reference (second review, item 5).** | Envelope converter from the second frame (Model B from frame 23: its reference starts with a power-on step through the board's high-pass, -12.5 dB at frame 0 falling below -40 dB by frame 17, S3's): Model A rms <= 0.15 dB, worst frame <= 0.75 dB (measured 0.13 / 0.70); Model B <= 0.10 / 0.40 (0.09 / 0.36). Waveform converter on the two saws: harmonics 2..8 within 0.35 dB on Model A (measured 0.2 / 0.3) and 0.15 dB on Model B (0.1 / 0.1). The pulse segment is no longer compared: two levels, which any converter maps alike. An ideal Model A converter fails the test (mutation); a ladder ratio of 2.1 instead of 2.2 still passes: the traces do not pin the ratio that closely. **(review)** |
| 25 | **The short kernel's steps (second review, item 7).** | The golden of the short-only renderer is gone: regenerated by today's renderer it proved nothing, and today's output differs from the old renderer for reasons that are fixes (pull-downs, the ring and gate defects, hysteresis, fades on the long kernel), which a golden would have to list one by one. Instead `[kernel]`: a free-running Model B saw (no converter terms) against an independent model, the continuous ramp minus one integrated `Chip::ShortBlep` step at each wrap's exact time, kLatencySamples late: within 3e-8 (asserted 1e-6) at 96 and 192 kHz. With `[timing]` (steps at writes, the same construction) the short kernel's steps are pinned in time and size. **(review)** |
| 26 | **Per-cycle sources, oscillator 1's glitch, the gate at the rise (second review, items 1 to 3).** | A noise combination (per-cycle mode) as a sync or ring source is not predicted: on Model A its pull-down depends on the noise output, so its top-bit changes come from the digital accumulator cycle by cycle and its targets' resets from the digital core. Oscillator 1 reads oscillator 3's top bit before oscillator 3's pull-down in the same step (8.15.2 (c) step 1): it sees the rise for one cycle, so a ring flip (or sync reset) and the flip back one cycle later are rendered for oscillator 1 only. The pulse comparison that decides a pull-down at the rise is taken on the digital line at whole cycles (the landing before the rise), not from the continuous line (widths just below 2048 were wrong). Each has `[stream]` cases against the chip's own output stream. **(review)** |
| 27 | **Third review (2026-11-16): seven defects, a white-box check, wider schedules.** | Fixed: (F1) a write took a ring target's state from a per-cycle source's line, which carries offU, instead of the accumulator the flips come from: inverted for good behind a Model A noise+saw source; now from the source's own top bit, at writes and at activation. (F2) a write that stops the source's rise after its line has crossed (its test bit, a Model A saw combination pulled down at the write, a Model A per-cycle source with the saw) left the target's predicted reset standing; now dropped and the target re-synced. (F3) a digital sync reset could confirm a pending pull-down. (F4) a width write one step before the rise: the gate forced open from the stale comparison was not re-read; now re-read one cycle on when the chip's comparison on its first upper-half landing is low (the reviewer's patch re-read it from the continuous line always, which the long run showed pulling where the chip does not: a landing above the new width). (F5) a reset reached late lost the rising edge after the falling one at narrow widths. Found by the long runs: at width 0 a late reset made a falling edge (the comparison is always high); a flip back carried for oscillator 1 survived a write that had taken the ring state afresh. Also: the gate is re-derived after a correction; the random schedules made sync chains through single-oscillator writes (a generator fault, fixed: a write of one oscillator only changes that one). **White-box check** (`Chip::renderCheckStates`, tests only): at every sample end, away from writes, pending predictions and boundaries (three cycles plus offU), the rendered gate must equal the chip's comparison and a ring target's state its source's top bit (oscillator 1: as it sees a per-cycle source, before the pull-down); on in `[schedule]` and `[corrections]`. Schedules now cover every waveform value, gate, sync and ring toggles, raw register bytes, `powerOnKeepingTime`, `clearEnvelopeCounters`, dense writes (30 to 600 cycles apart), Authentic. Not observable in any test or long run, kept as the consistent reading: the per-cycle source's top bit at activation (offU is 0 there) and the gate after a correction (the corrections left are below 4e-6 of a period). Reverted as unobservable: the clamp of a sync reset's sub-cycle time at -1 cycle (a round-two guess; back to 0). **(review)** |

## B-Side S2b-1: our own combined-waveform tables (2026-11-15, all review)

Contract: `docs/architecture.md` 8.4.5, 8.15.2 (c), 8.15.8 (owner decision: our own fits, never copied).
The reference emulator was only run (through the adapter, on our own traces); its source and tables
were not opened.

| Decision | Reason |
|---|---|
| **What is observable.** The read register shows output bits 11..4 only; bits 3..0 are not observable through the trace interface at all. They also never reach the digital state (8.15.2 uses out bits 11..4 for the read value and the write-back, bit 11 for the Model A pull-down), so the digital comparison needs exactly the observable bits. Bits 3..0 are the model's extrapolation and matter only for the audio (S2b rendering). **(review)** | The `.bsspec` features are too coarse to recover single low bits; no audio is stored. |
| **Model: a resistor network per combination and chip model** (CombinedWaveforms.h): each selected waveform drives every bit line through its own pull-up or pull-down conductance, neighbouring lines are coupled, a selected pulse ties every line to one common node, conductances and threshold have per-bit slopes; node voltages solved exactly, bit = voltage >= threshold. At most 11 parameters per combination (pulse-free ones use 8). Tables computed from the parameters at the first prepare. **(review)** | Few parameters, physically motivated (shared bit lines), predicts all 12 bits. Simpler kernels (distance-weighted average with a threshold) left 2 to 4 times more errors; a feedback (latching) extension gave no gain. |
| **Fit quality (full tables, pulse width 0; entries whose 8 observed bits all agree / observed bits wrong / max and RMS error of the observed value in rd1B steps), at the committed fixed point.** Model A: tri+saw 99.78 % / 0.027 % / 32 / 0.71; tri+pulse 97.27 % / 0.37 % / 128 / 13.8; saw+pulse 95.75 % / 0.70 % / 128 / 11.5; all three 98.71 % / 0.20 % / 128 / 12.4. Model B: tri+saw 98.78 % / 0.20 % / 128 / 2.7; tri+pulse 91.99 % / 1.20 % / 128 / 20.4; saw+pulse 92.48 % / 1.02 % / 128 / 18.2; all three 98.07 % / 0.27 % / 128 / 12.6. Pulse + noise (Tpn, all noise samples): Model A 96.4 % / 0.45 %, Model B 94.9 % / 0.71 %. **The pulse combinations fit worst** (Model B tri+pulse and saw+pulse about 92 %). Per plan: `bside_shapefit report tests/bside/shapes`; tolerances in `tests/bside/trace/ShapeDataTests.cpp` (fully agreeing plans pinned at 1.0, plans of at most 128 samples at the achieved count, sweeps 0.2 % below, bit fractions just above the achieved). **(review)** | Honest limit of a few-parameter model; a per-entry table would fit exactly but would be copied data, which Q5 rules out. |
| **Pulse width enters only as the mask** of 8.15.2 (c) step 3: at pulse widths 0x400 and 0xa00 every sample whose comparison is low reads 0 and the others agree with the width-0 shape within the fit error (one shape per combination, not per width). **(review)** | Measured (`s_pw_*`), as the contract assumes. |
| **Noise with two or more of triangle, saw and pulse: constant shape 0** (both models). 96 (A) / 48 (B) samples each read 0, except two Model B triangle+pulse+noise samples (noise output 0xf8 at index 0x87a reads 0x80; 0xfd at 0x7e8 reads 0xf8; fit 95.8 %). **(review)** | Measured (`s_noise_b/d/e/f_*`). |
| **Pulse with noise is indexed by the noise output (contract decision A, 2026-11-15).** The data first: a fixed noise output (0xfc, after two shifts from a full refill) reads the same value at 63 indices (`s_pn_noise_*`), and 96 (A) / 83 (B) distinct noise outputs, each at up to two indices and across all pulse-with-noise plans, never give two values (`s_pn_index_*`, `s_noise_c_*`). So the wording holds. Tpn is the network with the noise output (bits 11..4) driving the bit lines in the saw slot plus the pulse node; step 3's AND with the noise output stays. The `c_c_*` traces now agree at every exempt checkpoint (were 10 of 100 differing with the constant). | Measured; the constant of the first hand-back reached 20.8 % / 50 %. |
| **Fit procedure: a fixed point.** `bside_shapefit fit` runs deterministic rounds (one thread per table, own generator; 6 000 random starts, pattern search from the best 16, random joint moves) from the committed parameters until a round changes nothing; a round accepts strict improvements of the wrong-bit count only, so a round from a fixed point returns it unchanged. `bside_shapefit verify` checks that (about 1 min; a developer check, not in ctest). The committed fit took 4 rounds. Noise constants: per-bit majority. Hexadecimal literals; the parameters are `constexpr`-built and `constinit` (no static-initialisation order can see them unset). | Review finding: the first hand-back was not a fixed point (refit improved two tables). |
| **Floating point.** Every decision the constraints leave to the network keeps a margin of at least 2.7e-6 from its threshold (tested against kMinMargin = 1e-6), and the SHA-256 of both models' tables (incl. Tpn) is pinned in `bside_unit`; the GCC Linux run computes the same hashes. **(review)** | Tables computed at run time instead of stored. |
| **Measurement design** (`tests/bside/shapes/ShapePlan.h`): sweeps at frequency 0x1000 with a checkpoint per cycle (Model A reads the same step, Model B its latch, one step later); the Model A saw combinations' upper half and index 0 by one-step spot samples between sync resets (the pull-down sends a sweep back below 0x800); noise samples after a refill under the test bit, with random frequency and delay so the index and the noise output are independent, skipping steps with a noise shift. Index and masks are taken from the reference's own checkpoints and the design is checked. Only `.bstrace` and `.bsref` are committed (4.5 MB); the `.bsspec` outputs are not used. | Smallest data that covers every entry. |
| **`bside_independent`**: the independent model (IndependentModel.h, not ours to edit) has no table interface and keeps the S2a placeholder; its tests now build in their own executable whose sources are all compiled with `BS_CHIP_DEFAULT_SHAPES=placeholder`, so chip and model agree on everything except the table values (tested against the reference instead). Interface it would need: a shape callback or a `const ShapeTables&` (16 x 4096 values per model) passed to `Model::reset`, used where it computes `shape` today; then the executable can go. **(review)** | The alternative, editing that file, is forbidden to whoever edits the chip. |
| **Exemption budget (contract decision B, 2026-11-15; made a golden after the second review).** The marking rule stays; the replay test fails unless every `c_*` trace and field has exactly as many differing exempt checkpoints as `tests/bside/trace/exemption-budget.txt` says: more is a regression, fewer fails with "budget can be tightened: rebless"; a key matching no exempt trace and field, and a duplicate key, fail too. `BS_BLESS_EXEMPTION_BUDGET=<path>` writes the file and then fails ("blessed; rerun without the variable"). Today 81 of 6 850 exempt field checkpoints differ (placeholder: 1 752), all in rd1B of 12 traces, one of them a noise-combination trace (`c_b_b` rd1B 1); every acc* and noise* field is at 0, so effectively exact. | Owner-side decision; review: a ceiling hid improvements and a bless could pass. |
| **Model B read latch across waveform changes (contract question C): neither wording fits the data fully.** `s_latch_b` (triangle+saw at frequency 0x1000, 40 switches per case): 3 -> 6 for one step reads T3(X) (the latched shape) 30 times, T6(X) (a latched index) once, neither 9 times; 3 -> 4 -> 6: pulse alone reads 0xff (its own output) every time and leaves the latch (30 of 40 then read T3(X)); 3 -> 2: all 40 reads show saw(X) AND (saw(X) << 1), neither T3(X) nor the saw. Our chip latches the shape (matches the steady state, the pulse case and 30 of 40 of the others); not changed. Pinned by a test so a regeneration that changes it is seen; the test also runs our chip on the trace and pins how many of the reads it reproduces (event 1: 29, event 2: pulse step 40 and return 31, event 3: 5, of 40 each). Pulse with noise leaves the latch alone (`s_latch_pn_b`, 3 -> c -> 3 and 3 -> c, c -> 3, 30 indices each: the reference reads the old triangle+saw shape on return in 60 of 60; our chip reads its own T3 in 60 of 60 and reproduces the 54 where its fitted T3 equals the measured one). **Stopped on this point: needs a contract decision** (and probably more measurements of switches between other combinations). | Measured. |
| **Unobservable bits 3..0 constrained (review finding).** Two rules after the network. Rule 1 applies to **all 12 bits**, not only to bits 3..0: a line is high only if one of its selected triangle or saw inputs is high (it holds in all 33 934 measured samples according to the second review, and in every one of the 8 x 4096 full-table entries by my own check; the reviewer found it accounts for a large part of Model A's accuracy for the pulse combinations; it also removes e.g. bit 0 with the triangle). Rule 2 concerns bits 3..0 only: bits 3..0 only continue a run of set bits from above (85 to 99 % of measured set bits have their upper neighbour set; no entry consists of low bits alone). Tested by a plausibility test. The constraints are inside the fitted model, so the fit improved too (Model A saw+pulse 276 -> 230 wrong bits). **(review)** | They will feed the DAC; the read-back cannot decide them. |
| **Candidate for the next step: the Model B latch holds lines, not shapes (reviewer's hypothesis, recorded only; the chip is not changed).** On Model B the latch would hold the previous step's selected triangle/saw lines before the combining network (triangle+saw: x AND (x << 1); saw: x; triangle: tri(x)), and each read would pass the latched lines through the combining stage of the current waveform bits, then the pulse and noise masks. Measured by the reviewer at 29 indices per transition: 29/29 for all eight switches to a single waveform (3->1, 3->2, 2->1, 1->2, 6->2, 6->1, 5->1, 5->2); better than "new table at the latched index" for 3->5, 3->6, 1->6, 5->6; worse for 3->7; weak for 2->7 and 6->7. It would change only the Model B read value, never the output. **(review)** | Explains `s_latch_b` event 3 (saw(X) AND (saw(X) << 1)); needs its own measurement plan and a contract decision before code. |
| **Restart entry points for the program engine (D, E).** `Chip::powerOnKeepingTime()`: oscillators, envelopes, registers and the stored filter input to power-on, cycle and sample clock kept. `Chip::clearEnvelopeCounters (n)`: envelope n's rate counter position and divider counter to 0, nothing else (pending reset, divider period, pipelines, phase, counter, freeze untouched). Both keep advanceCycles equal to stepCycle (tested). **(review)** | Requested for 8.6.4 (Clean) and voice stealing. |
## B-Side S2c: patch program engine (2026-11-15, all review)

Where `docs/architecture.md` 8.6 (with 8.13 and 8.14) is silent, ambiguous or unbuildable as written, the engine
(`source/bside/engine/ProgramEngine.*`) takes the reading below. Rows marked **contract gap** need an owner or
contract decision; the rest are choices that only the next reader of the table's behaviour can notice.

| # | Point | Reading taken (reason) |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | **What a lane command writes.** 8.6.2 says a tick's writes are listed "with equal cycles, in that order" but not which registers a command touches. | A lane command always writes (literally, each time it runs, even when the value is unchanged): `wave` writes the frequency (only when it has a pitch) then control; `pitch` the frequency; `gate on/off` control; `adsr` registers 5 then 6; `set`/`sweep`/`pingpong` the pulse width (low byte then high nibble); `cutoff` registers 0x15 then 0x16; `res`/`route` register 0x17 (the other half taken from the current effective value); `mode`/`volume` register 0x18. Literal writes keep a tracker-style table honest (a `pitch +0` each tick is a write; a gate off and on in one tick reaches the chip as two writes, not as nothing). |
| 2 | **Registers that follow the host** (8.6.2 "ownership"). | Per field: frequency, envelope pair, control bits, gate, pulse width, cutoff, resonance, routing, mode, volume. A field a lane has written for this note is the lane's; any other is the host's. The engine writes a host-driven group when its effective value differs from the last value it wrote ("sync"): at every tick, at note-off, and when the caller calls `sync` (host parameter change). Value equality is exact, so an unchanged host writes nothing (tested at arbitrary cycles). |
| 3 | **Order inside a tick** (8.6.2 gives only the lane order). | Restart writes; then the three wave lanes' rows, then the host-driven wave registers of oscillators 1 to 3 (whether an oscillator the host leaves off needs its frequency depends on the controls the wave lanes just wrote, so every wave lane runs first); then for pulse lanes 1 to 3: the lane's rows, then that oscillator's pulse-width sync; then the filter lane, then the filter sync. Inside an oscillator the engine's own groups are frequency, envelope, control (the gate last, after its envelope). Restart writes go per oscillator: envelope, control. (review) |
| 4 | **Idle oscillators.** | An oscillator is active when `oscN.wave` is not Off or its wave lane has rows. An inactive oscillator gets only its control register from the host (so a test bit left by a restart is cleared); its envelope and pulse width are not written. Its **frequency** is written when it is the sync or ring source of an active oscillator whose effective control has the sync or ring bit (sources: 1 <- 3, 2 <- 1, 3 <- 2), so the timbre never depends on the voice's previous note (architect decision, review of S2c). |
| 5 | **Restart section vs policy** (8.6.4). `lead` is "ticks"; `gateoff` is used only by Look-ahead. | The section has two parts: the **restart writes** (gate off and envelope := `ad`/`sr` on all three oscillators; `phase` also sets the test bit) and the **tick 0 part** (`firstwave`, delayed row 0, test cleared by the next control write). Precondition and Clean write the restart writes when the voice becomes idle (`enterIdle`, called by the voice owner with the part's restart section) and remember the cycle; `lead` ticks (of the period at the note-on) is the "full lead". A note on a voice that is not in the restart state (never preconditioned, or preconditioned with a different restart section) runs the restart writes at tick 0, first in the tick, at the same cycle as the rows. **Authentic means only what the table's rows do: the restart section is not applied at all** (no idle-time writes, no writes at tick 0, no `firstwave`, no reset of a stolen voice, no direct operation) (architect decision, review of S2c). `gateoff` is parsed and ignored until S5. (review) |
| 6 | **Clean's direct operation** ("the rate counter and divider are set to 0 directly"). | A sink call (`clearEnvelopeTimers`) at the note's cycle before anything else, when the voice did not get the full lead (retrigger, steal, no restart section, short idle) under Clean. `ChipProgramSink` answers it with `Chip::clearEnvelopeCounters (n)` for **all three** oscillators of the voice's chip: 8.6.4 names no oscillator and the restart concerns the whole voice (reading chosen; review). It is not a register write and does not appear in register traces; the golden files show it as a comment line, and the live-versus-replay test applies it at that marker. |
| 7 | **Stolen voice** (8.6.4 end). | Clean and Precondition: `resetChip`, then the restart writes (mode not off), at the note cycle (the caller passes the cycle after the fade); Authentic does nothing special. `ChipProgramSink` answers `resetChip` with `Chip::powerOnKeepingTime()`: power-on state, the chip's cycle count keeps running. After the reset the engine's register shadow is zero, as the chip's. |
| 8 | **Retrigger without a restart section** (not in 8.6.4). | Under Clean and Precondition a gate that is still high is cleared at the note cycle before tick 0, so the chip always sees an attack edge. Authentic leaves it (no new attack, as on the chip). **Kept by the architect; for the owner to confirm (review).** |
| 9 | **`firstwave`.** "control := firstwave plus gate". | For every active oscillator at tick 0 (not under Authentic): control := (firstwave without gate) + gate while the note is held; envelope and frequency come from the host (the table's own values arrive with row 0 at tick 1); the wave lanes (only) wait one tick. The override of an oscillator ends when a lane writes its control, at tick 1, or at note-off (then the table's release row, or the host, decides the control: a note shorter than one tick does not attack again). (review) |
| 10 | **Tick clock.** "P is read at block start; a change applies from each voice's next tick". | The next tick's cycle is fixed when the previous tick runs (cycle + P at that moment). A change of P therefore applies from the tick after the one already scheduled. The caller gives P (`tickPeriodCycles`, pure function: Frame cycles by the clock (19 656 below 1 003 988 Hz, else 17 095), `round`-half-up everywhere, clamped 256 .. 2^30). |
| 11 | **Row timing details.** | A row with delay D >= 1 executed at tick t makes the next row run at tick t + D; a `sweep x n` adds at ticks t .. t + n - 1 and the next row runs at t + n; `pingpong` steps every tick from its own tick on until a release jump: the direction turns at a limit before the step (a value that starts on a limit does not repeat it), a step that would pass a limit is cut to it, so the value stops exactly on a limit and reverses there; a lane that runs past its last row ends like `end`; `repeat` counters are per row and reset when the note starts and when a repeat falls through. (review) |
| 12 | **Note-off** (8.6.2). | A tick on the same cycle runs first (the caller's order, 8.13 two bursts); then, per slot in lane order: a lane with a `release` label jumps there (also if it had ended, and leaving a sweep or ping-pong) and runs its burst; a wave lane with rows but no label clears its gate. **Once the note is off the table can no longer raise the gate**: every later control write has its gate bit cleared (waveform, sync, ring and test bits still apply, so a drum's waveform sequence plays out through the release without a second attack) (architect decision, review of S2c). A lane-less oscillator's gate follows the note. (review) |
| 13 | **Macros and resolution.** | `macro.pw_offset` and `macro.cutoff_offset` are added after the lane or host value and the sum clamped to 0..4095 / 0..2047, for lane and host values alike; `macro.sweep_depth` multiplies every sweep and ping-pong step, the lane's position is kept as a real number (a step of 0.5 at depth 0.5 is not lost). Authentic rounds the effective value half up to the register (frequency 1..65535, pulse width, cutoff); Smooth passes real values, frequency capped at 12 kHz. A non-finite host value is replaced by a safe default, never written. |
| 14 | **Frequency formula.** | Register value = 440 x 2^((m - 69) / 12) x 2^24 / clock with m = host semitones (+ the lane's relative offset, or the lane's absolute note alone). The host's semitones already contain key, glide, bend, vibrato, coarse and fine and unison detune (8.5); the program engine adds only the lane's part. |
| 15 | **Real time and robustness.** | `ProgramEngine` owns an 8 KB program copy and the lane state, no pointers and no heap; calls are noexcept and never allocate (counted in the property test, including over-aligned `new`). **Precondition: every program passed to `noteOn` / `enterIdle` has passed `validate()`** (the reader's output always does); debug builds assert it. Release builds bounds-check the indices the engine follows (lane and row ranges, command ranges, lane kind of each command: a wave command in the filter lane, a ping-pong outside a pulse lane, ... are ignored) so a corrupt program cannot read or write outside the engine; its musical behaviour is unspecified. A 128-row backstop per burst keeps a zero-delay loop from hanging. |
| 16 | **Chip sink time** (`ChipProgramSink`). | The chip keeps its cycle count through a steal (`powerOnKeepingTime`), so `cycle()` is the chip's cycle and equals the engine's time: a caller renders `engine.nextTickCycle() - sink.cycle()`. (An earlier version kept an offset because `reset()` zeroed the cycle count.) |

## B-Side S2a fidelity pass: the digital core follows 8.15 (2026-11-14, all review)

The core now implements `docs/architecture.md` 8.15 as written; every committed trace replays with 0
mismatches (`bside_trace`), and the eight developer-only tune traces (outside the repository) replay
exact at every checkpoint. The judgement calls below are where 8.15 is silent or where the replay
needed a reading; rows marked **contract gap** need a decision or a contract amendment.

| Decision | Why |
|---|---|
| **Contract gap: the exemption of 8.15.8 is implemented from its definition, not its list** (review round 1: the coordinator amends 8.15 to this). A field is exempt from the cycle a combined-waveform table value can reach it until the state it reached is overwritten (`source/bside/trace/ShapeExemption.h` follows each flow of 8.15.2: output, read value, Model B latch, Model A pull-down, sync, ring index, noise write-back, shift timing; the noise register and the noise output are tracked apart). **A table is used** when at least two of triangle, saw and pulse are selected, or pulse with noise; **noise with only triangle or only saw uses none** (that shape AND the noise output; `wave::usesTable`). Beyond the list of 8.15.8 this also exempts **the pulled-down oscillator's own accumulator** (Model A, saw with a table) and what reads it (the ring-modulated output of the next oscillator, hence rd1B). **An exemption ends** when the state is overwritten exactly: the accumulator at a test-bit reset or at a sync reset whose source's rise is certain; the noise register when a fill makes it all ones; the read value at the next exact output (Model B: once the latch holds an exact shape). Unit tests cover each end (`ChipReplayTests.cpp`, `[exemption]`), since no committed trace ends an exemption inside a trace. | The list alone fails five Model A traces: `c_3_a`, `c_6_a`, `c_7_a` (acc3 ours 0x801c96, reference 0x1c96 at cycle 1 162: our placeholder table keeps bit 11 where the reference's pulls the top bit down), `c_ring_3_a` (acc3 from cycle 1 040), `c_ring_order_a` (acc2 and rd1B from cycle 2 563). Round 1 (reviewer): `c_9_*` and `c_a_*` were exempt at all 400 checkpoints and equal anyway at all 400; with the table rule they are exact (0 exempt, 0 mismatched) and `bside_trace` asserts 0 exempt for them, as for every `d_*` and `a_*` trace. Per field with a table (Model A): 0xb and 0xe/0xf still exempt acc3 (a table and the saw make the pull-down uncertain; 61 checkpoints, all equal anyway, because the locked noise output forces the output to 0), 0xd (no saw) exempts no accumulator. Now: 0 mismatches, 3 915 of 5 236 `c_*` checkpoints exempt (was 4 315). 8.15.8's "acc and phase fields": the `.bsref` `phase` fields are envelope phases, which no oscillator reaches, so only `acc` is exempted. |
| **Contract gap: the noise output at power-on is the output of the power-on register** (0x7ffffe gives 0xfe0). 8.15.1 lists "output and read value 0" but not the noise output, which 8.15.3 recomputes only at a shift or fill. | It only matters for noise selected before the first shift, write-back or fill. No committed trace distinguishes it (a mutation to 0 passes `bside_trace`); a row test pins our choice. **Unproven** until a trace selects noise before the first shift (open item below). |
| **The "a pending reset waits" precedence of 8.15.4 is implemented but appears unreachable:** removing it changes no committed trace, no test and no state in 2 x 10^7 random cycles against the independent stepper. Right after a reset the rate position is at most 4, below the smallest match position (8), while every counter-step or divider pipeline completes within 4 steps of the reset or the gate write that started it. | Kept as specified; recorded so a later simplification does not count it as tested. |
| **The envelope counter is 8 bits in decay and release too** (8.15.4 step 4 says "counter - 1"; the coordinator amends it to mod 256). A release or decay step at counter 0 is possible when an attack phase has cleared the freeze before any attack step; the counter then wraps to 0xff. | **Proven by committed data:** `d_env_cases_b`, env3 0x00 -> 0xff in release at cycle 407 985 (and 447 990); `d_env_rates_b`, env1 and env3 at cycles 1 108 861 and 2 060 784 (between checkpoints; our chip, exact at every checkpoint, takes the step there). A mutation that stops the wrap fails `bside_trace`; the row test names these cycles. |
| **Model B read value with a combined triangle/saw: the latch (which holds this step's shape, after the table) is masked by this step's pulse and noise masks; the table is not applied a second time.** 8.15.2 (c) step 5 writes "latch AND the same two masks, then the combined table if any". | The order only matters for combined waveforms, whose read values are exempt (8.15.8). To settle with the fitted tables (S2b), which must say what "the table applied to a latched value" means. |
| **Combined-waveform placeholder restated for the new step structure:** the shape of a combination is the AND of the selected triangle and saw shapes (0xfff when neither is selected); pulse and noise enter through the masks of step 3, as for single waveforms. | 8.15.8: our own tables come with S2b; the AND keeps the documented first-order behaviour (a waveform pulls bits low) and keeps every flow of 8.15.2 exercised. |
| **Events:** a fill step under the test bit is reported as a `NoiseClockEvent` with `fill = true`; changes made by a write (the shift when the test bit is cleared, the accumulator reset when it is set) carry no event, because a renderer splits its sample at the write and reads the state there. An optional `oscStep` event per oscillator per step (sinks that declare `kWantsStepEvents`) feeds the exemption tracker; with it `advanceCycles` steps every cycle. | Keeps the event contract of the first version (self-contained, a sink never reads the chip) and gives the replay the per-step facts 8.15.8 asks for without a second copy of the rules. |
| **Fast path:** `advanceCycles` steps every cycle while an oscillator writes back into its noise register or, on Model A, combines its saw with another waveform, and steps each cycle in which a top bit rises while its target has the sync bit; otherwise it jumps (at most 4 096 cycles) oscillator by oscillator (accumulator in closed form, noise pipeline, fill and fade timers event by event, output, read value, Model B latch and pulse comparison from the last two cycles) and envelope by envelope (runs of pure rate-counter increments, `envStep` for every other cycle). Proven equal in the full state: the random equivalence tests, the independent stepper, and every checkpoint of every committed trace. | 8.4.2 asks for a closed form. Cost (scratch benchmark, loaded machine): 21 to 45 ns per internal sample at 96 kHz with three sounding voices, 2 to 4 times the first version, because the pipelined envelope has 3 non-trivial cycles per rate period (`docs/engineering-notes.md`). |
| **What the `c_*` traces prove until our own tables exist (S2b):** the envelopes, and every oscillator field and checkpoint outside a table's reach. Each `[replay]` line of `bside_trace` lists per trace the exempt checkpoints, how many of them equal the reference anyway, and per field "exempt / differing" (`exemptFieldsText`). The oscillator-path rules themselves are proven by the `d_*` traces (exact, no exemption), the `a_*` traces (exact, no exemption), `c_9_*`/`c_a_*` (exact write-back path) and the unit tests. | Honest scope of the exemption (review round 1). Per `c_*` trace, exempt / equal anyway: `c_3` A 175/36, B 175/32; `c_5` A 175/115, B 175/118; `c_6` A 175/104, B 175/107; `c_7` A 175/117, B 175/100; `c_9`, `c_a` 0 (were 100/100 each); `c_b` A 100/97, B 100/92; `c_c` A 100/83, B 100/83; `c_d` A 100/91, B 100/91; `c_e` A 100/100, B 100/99; `c_f` A 100/100, B 100/99; `c_ring_3` A 271/47, B 271/53; `c_ring_5` A 271/182, B 271/183; `c_ring_order` A 431/386, B 0. |
| **Open items for the next reference regeneration:** (1) a trace with a full noise fill after a combined waveform, so an exemption ends inside a trace; (2) a trace that selects noise before the first shift, so the power-on noise output is proven. | Neither case is in the committed data; both are covered only by unit tests today. |
| **The naive stepper is no longer independent of the implementer** (8.9.1). `tests/bside/unit/ChipStepperTests.cpp` was first written by a separate test agent from 8.4.3; for this pass the implementer rewrote it from 8.15. It still guards the fast path and catches slips, but it is not independent evidence. The independent evidence is the reviewer's own model written from 8.15 before reading the code (agrees on every `d_*` and `a_*` checkpoint, the eight tunes and 9 x 10^8 random cycles), added on a separate branch. | Review round 1. |

## B-Side S2a: chip digital core (2026-11-14, all review)

**Superseded by the fidelity pass above** (2026-11-14): the core now follows `docs/architecture.md`
8.15. The rows below describe the first version; the four KNOWN OPEN DEVIATION rows, the divider,
sustain, freeze, rate-counter and sync-chain rows are settled by 8.15 (8.15.2, 8.15.4, 8.15.5). The
rows on read registers at other addresses, `setFilter`, program-engine input and the event contract
still hold.

Contract: `docs/architecture.md` 8.4.1–8.4.3, 8.4.7. The normative cycle definition as built is the
header of `source/bside/chip/Chip.h`. Where 8.4.3 says "confirm" or is silent, the core implements the
behaviour below, from public technical documentation of the chip; **each row is a contract question
until reference data (8.9.5) confirms or corrects it**. Rows marked **KNOWN OPEN DEVIATION** are not
judgement calls: they are probably wrong against the documented chip and stay as they are only until
reference data settles the exact behaviour (orchestrator, review round 1). A correction changes `Chip::stepCycle` (and,
where noted, the fast path's event rules), never the replay or the formats.

| Decision | Why |
|---|---|
| **Sync chains (row 6, "confirm"):** a source whose own top bit rose in a cycle in which it is itself synced (its sync bit set and its own source's top bit rose) does not sync its target in that cycle. Edges are taken before any reset of the cycle. | The documented special case of the chip's sync logic; the plain reading ("the source rose, so the target resets") gives a different result only in this coincidence. |
| **KNOWN OPEN DEVIATION, likely wrong, to be settled by reference data — test bit and the noise register (row 7):** as built, setting the test bit zeroes the accumulator and its fraction and sets the noise register to 0x7fffff at once; both are held while it is set (no write-back then); clearing it does nothing (no noise clock). Power-on noise value: 0x7fffff. | Public documentation describes a different behaviour: the register's bits fill with ones over a model-dependent time while the test bit is set, and clearing the bit clocks the register once. Not implemented in S2a (orchestrator, review round 1); only the accumulator part (held at 0) is uncontested. |
| **Combined waveforms (row 8), digital value:** for both models the bitwise AND of the selected 12-bit outputs (`wave::combined`); the read register 0x1B and the noise write-back use it. **Write-back:** every cycle (after accumulators, noise clocks and sync), an oscillator with noise plus another waveform, not in test mode, clears the noise register's output bits (20, 18, 14, 11, 9, 5, 2, 0) where the combined output bit is 0 (clear only). | 8.4.5 fits the combined shapes per model (Q5) later; the AND is the documented first-order behaviour (a waveform can only pull bits low) and gives the documented lock-up. The per-cycle timing is the physical reading (the output pulls the register cells continuously); write-back only at a shift is the alternative. `wave::combined` is the single place to replace. |
| **Exponential divider (row 10, "exact threshold timing: confirm"):** the divider period is latched when the counter takes exactly the value 0xff (1), 0x5d (2), 0x36 (4), 0x1a (8), 0x0e (16), 0x06 (30) or 0x00 (1), whatever the phase, and kept otherwise. So a release from 0x80 after an attack from 0 runs at divider 2 (the last value passed was 0x5d going up), not 1 as the range table of 8.4.3 reads. | The documented hardware compares the counter with these values; the range reading of the contract is the same for an envelope that decays from 0xff and differs only after a partial attack. |
| **Sustain comparison is equality:** in decay the counter steps down unless it equals the sustain level (or is 0). Raising the sustain level above the counter therefore lets it decay to 0. | Contract row 10 says only "raising it does not raise the counter"; the equality comparator is the documented mechanism and is consistent with that sentence. |
| **Freeze at zero is by value:** in decay and release a counter of 0 never steps (no wrap), also when the gate went on and off before the first attack step. | Contract row 10, "the counter stops at 0 and does not wrap", taken literally. (A latched hold flag cleared by the gate would let that case wrap to 0xff.) |
| **KNOWN OPEN DEVIATION, likely wrong, to be settled by reference data — attack started at 0xff:** as built, the 8-bit counter wraps to 0 on the first attack step and climbs again. | Public documentation says the wrapped counter is then held at zero until the next rising gate (a hold flag set by the wrap). Not implemented in S2a (orchestrator, review round 1). |
| **Rate counter (row 11):** after 0x7fff it continues at 1, so a wrap takes 32 767 cycles; it reads 0 only right after a match. The delay after a smaller period is `0x7fff − count + period` cycles. | The documented counter has 32 767 states; 8.4.3's "up to 32 768 cycles" is approximate. **The `rate` field of `.bsref` is this count**; an adapter whose emulator stores another representation must convert it. |
| **KNOWN OPEN DEVIATION, likely wrong, to be settled by reference data — no write or pipeline delays:** as built, every write takes effect between two cycles, before the next cycle's step (frequency and pulse width from the next cycle; gate edges, rate periods, test bit at once), and every internal stage acts in the same cycle. Model A and Model B are digitally identical except through `wave::combined` (identical today). | Publicly documented delays that are not modelled: the gate edge takes effect a cycle or more after the write; the envelope counter steps one cycle after the rate counter's match; the divider period is latched with a delay; the noise register shifts some cycles after the bit-19 rise; the oscillator-3 read register is latched one cycle late. Some of these differ per model. Not implemented in S2a (orchestrator, review round 1). |
| **KNOWN OPEN DEVIATION, likely wrong, to be settled by reference data — oscillator-3 read with no waveform:** as built, 0x1B reads 0 when no waveform is selected. | Public documentation says the read returns the last output, held and fading over time (the same floating latch 8.4.4 models for the DAC input). Not implemented in S2a (orchestrator, review round 1). |
| **Read registers, other addresses:** addresses are taken modulo 32; every address other than 0x1B and 0x1C reads 0; 0x19–0x1F are not writable. **`setFilter` keeps bit 3 of 0x17 (external input, not modelled) at 0**; a trace's `write` stores the byte as written. | 8.4.1: "bit 3: external input (not modelled, 0)". A trace replays the exact register bytes; the program engine's path never sets the bit. |
| **Program-engine input:** `OscInput::frequency` is in register units (F, may exceed 16 bits in Smooth); Smooth keeps a 32-bit fraction below the accumulator's bit 0 and clamps F below 2^19 (about 30 kHz, so bit 19 rises at most once per cycle); the pulse width's integer part is `floor`; Authentic rounds both (and the cutoff) half away from zero (`std::round`, 8.4.6 "round") and clamps F to 0..65535. **The lower bound 1 of 8.4.6 belongs to the pitch-to-register mapping** (program engine and part, which turn a note into a register value); the chip itself accepts 0, as a register write of 0 stops an oscillator. `setOscillator` acts as writing registers 5, 6, then 4 at the current cycle; the frequency and pulse-width register bytes mirror the integer values. | 8.4.6 and 8.4.7 fix "integer values only" for Authentic and "a frequency with a fraction below the accumulator's last bit" for Smooth, but not the unit. |
| **Fast path:** `advanceCycles` jumps over runs of cycles in closed form and calls `stepCycle` for every cycle that has an event (a top-bit rise feeding a sync target, a rate match of a non-frozen envelope, any cycle of write-back mode); noise clocks inside a jump are counted in closed form; envelopes frozen at 0 or at their sustain level are jumped too; jumps are at most 4 096 cycles. Proven equal to per-cycle stepping by test (10^7 random cycles per model in ctest, 10^8 in `[.chip-long]`), states and events. **Events are self-contained and a sink must not read the chip** (review round 1): a sync reset carries the source's phase and increment (its sub-cycle crossing) and the target's phase before the reset; a noise clock and a write-back carry the register and its 12-bit output. Events of one closed-form jump come per oscillator, not by time; write-back mode is always stepped. Time, sample and envelope conventions: header of `Chip.h`. | 8.4.2 requires closed form; the cap keeps `jump × increment` inside 64 bits. Measured (scratch benchmark, -O2, this Mac): about 12 ns per internal sample per chip at 96 kHz with three sounding oscillators. |
## B-Side reference data (2026-11-14, all review except the owner decision; revised after review rounds 1 and 2)

Generated with the reference emulator through the adapter (`docs/bside-oracle-adapter.md`). Rows
marked **contract question** are differences between `docs/architecture.md` 8.4.3/8.4.4/8.9 and the
reference's observable behaviour, with evidence (committed trace, cycle). Rows marked **reference
convention** fix how the reference data is defined; our side copies them, they are not claims about
the hardware. Values marked **reference behaviour** are constants of the reference emulator (tuned
to one chip revision per model, approximate on real hardware): they were read in the emulator's
source first and then confirmed in the generated data at the cycle given. The contract questions
on the digital core below are answered by `docs/architecture.md` 8.15 (2026-11-14), which wins
where a row here is worded differently.

| Decision | Reason |
|---|---|
| **Owner decision (2026-11-14): "follow, own tables".** The digital core follows the reference emulator's per-cycle behaviour as specified from the reference data (architecture 8.15.1-8.15.5, written with the emulator's source open; its timing constants marked † included). The combined-waveform shapes (8.15.2 (c), steps 2 and 5) and all analog constants (8.4.4, 8.15.7) stay our own fits to generated data, never copied from the emulator's tables or source (Q5 stands for them). **Licence consequence, accepted by the owner:** B-Side is tied to GPL-compatible licensing for good (the reference is GPL-2.0-or-later; the project is AGPLv3, which is compatible). Still open: how the bit-exact rule treats checkpoints that depend on a combined-waveform shape (8.15.8). | The bit-exact rule (8.4.3) needs the reference's per-cycle rules, and the specification of them was written with the emulator's source open, so the digital core derives from it (hence the licence consequence). Shapes and analog constants can be fitted from generated data, so Q5 keeps them our own. |
| **Reference convention: the analysis plan is derived from the trace** (`SpecAnalysis.h`: the write cycles split the render; each gap with at least 14 whole frames after 8 frames of settling is a segment `s00`, `s01`, ...). | The trace format has no plan, and the adapter must not interpret anything beyond the trace grammar (comments included). One shared function keeps the reference and our tests aligned. |
| **Reference convention: output and time base.** The reference output is the chip's board-filter output before the emulator's playback stage (no background noise, no soft limiter, no 16-bit rounding), as floats where 1.0 is that stage's 16-bit full scale: board-filter output x 3/2 (Model A) or x 5/2 (Model B) / 32768. Input sample k of the resampler is the output produced by the chip's step k + 1 (the step from cycle k to k + 1); output sample i is the band-limited value at input position i x clock / 48000 (exact rational arithmetic); input positions before 0 take the value of step 1. A trace renders n = ceil (end x 48000 / clock) output samples; to do so the chip runs q + h + 2 input samples (cycles), q = floor ((n - 1) x clock / 48000), h = the kernel half-width (962 at 985 248 Hz, 998 at 1 022 727 Hz): 943 .. 963 cycles past `end` at 985 248 Hz, 979 .. 999 at 1 022 727 Hz, with no writes. Resampler: linear phase, flat to 20 kHz (< 0.001 dB), -120 dB from 24 kHz, DC gain exactly 1. | The emulator's own 48 kHz mode runs at 48 012 Hz (-0.36 cent) and passes aliases at -77 dB; its background noise (-72 dBFS) is on by default and its "off" setting is an undefined shift. The adapter's loop is proven equal to the emulator's own on every trace. Review round 1: the first resampler (stopband 28 kHz) let 24-28 kHz content alias into 20-24 kHz at -28 to -53 dB; it is now alias-free in 0..24 kHz. |
| **Reference convention: the top 4 kHz.** 20..24 kHz is the resampler's transition band (-6 dB at 22 kHz). The shared frame analysis (`featureFrames`, `kSpecBandLimitHz`) counts FFT bins below 20 kHz only, so band 22 is 16..20 kHz for the reference and for our replay alike; neither side filters separately. The 1/12-octave bins end at 19.1 kHz anyway. | The reference is alias-free; what remains above 20 kHz is the two sides' different transition shapes (resampler here, decimator there), which one shared band limit removes from every comparison. |
| **Reference convention: power-on state** (`d_power_on_*`): accumulators 0x555555, noise registers 0x7ffffe, envelope counters 0xaa in release at rate 0 (they run down to 0 in about 8 000 cycles), rate counters at position 0 with a reset pending (`div` 0x80), 0x1C reads 0 at cycle 0 and 0xaa from cycle 1, every analog state at zero (Model A's first frame is at -9 dBFS: the output's DC appears at once). **Contract question:** our `reset()` must produce this state, or the replay starts from a defined state written by the trace. | 8.4.7 says `reset()` gives the power-on state without defining it. |
| **Reference convention: rate and divider fields.** `rate` = the rate counter's position (0 .. 32766) on its 32 767-state sequence, one to one; the pending reset (set in the step where the counter matched the period, taken in a later step) is bit 7 of `div`; `div` bits 0..6 = the divider counter (it never exceeds 29; the adapter aborts above 0x7f). In steady state the position reads 1, 2, ..., P - 1, then P - 1 with the flag, then 1. **No format change.** The cleaner alternative needs one: `rate` 16 bits with the flag in bit 15 (`kStateFieldMax` 0x7fff -> 0xffff for the three rate fields). | Review round 1: round 1's "position + pending" was not one to one (pending at p and not pending at p + 1 read the same but continue differently). The pending reset can last more than one step (the envelope's other pipelines take precedence) and the counter can move meanwhile, so (position, pending) is the state. |
| **Analog references at the 50 Hz clock only; our filter is clock-independent.** Contract (architecture 8.15.7). | The reference's filter is stepped per cycle: its cutoff in Hz scales with the configured clock (identical per-cycle output at both clocks, so +3.80 % at the 60 Hz clock), while the real filter is continuous-time. Alternative: scale our cutoff by clock / 985 248 to mimic the reference. |
